### Q1. MHT CET 2026 (19 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The foot of the perpendicular from the origin to the plane is $N = (4, 5, a), a > 0$ and $l(ON) = 7$, then vector equation of the plane is ...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\vec{r} \cdot (4\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} - 2\sqrt{2}\hat{k}) = 49$
- **(B)** $\vec{r} \cdot (4\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} + 2\sqrt{2}\hat{k}) = 49$
- **(C)** $\vec{r} \cdot (4\hat{i} - 5\hat{j} + 2\sqrt{2}\hat{k}) = 49$
- **(D)** $\vec{r} \cdot (4\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} + 2\sqrt{2}\hat{k}) = 40$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\vec{r} \cdot (4\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} + 2\sqrt{2}\hat{k}) = 49$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given the foot of the perpendicular from the origin to the plane is $N(4, 5, a)$.

The length of the perpendicular is $l(ON) = 7$.

$|\vec{ON}| = \sqrt{4^2 + 5^2 + a^2} = 7$

$16 + 25 + a^2 = 49$

$a^2 = 8 \Rightarrow a = 2\sqrt{2}$ (since $a > 0$)

The normal vector to the plane is $\vec{n} = \vec{ON} = 4\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} + 2\sqrt{2}\hat{k}$.

The plane passes through the point $N$, so its position vector is $\vec{a} = 4\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} + 2\sqrt{2}\hat{k}$.

The vector equation of the plane is $\vec{r} \cdot \vec{n} = \vec{a} \cdot \vec{n}$.

$\vec{r} \cdot (4\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} + 2\sqrt{2}\hat{k}) = (4\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} + 2\sqrt{2}\hat{k}) \cdot (4\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} + 2\sqrt{2}\hat{k})$

$\vec{r} \cdot (4\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} + 2\sqrt{2}\hat{k}) = 16 + 25 + 8 = 49$

Answer: $\vec{r} \cdot (4\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} + 2\sqrt{2}\hat{k}) = 49$

</details>

### Q2. MHT CET 2026 (19 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of a plane passing through the intersection of two planes $\vec{r} \cdot (\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}) = 4$ and $\vec{r} \cdot (2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 3\hat{k}) = 7$ and parallel to Y-axis is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\vec{r} \cdot (2\hat{i} - 2\hat{k}) = 11$
- **(B)** $\vec{r} \cdot (\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}) = 3$
- **(C)** $\vec{r} \cdot (5\hat{i} - 3\hat{k}) = 18$
- **(D)** $\vec{r} \cdot (4\hat{i} - 3\hat{k}) = 28$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\vec{r} \cdot (5\hat{i} - 3\hat{k}) = 18$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the plane passing through the intersection of the given planes is

$\vec{r} \cdot [(\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}) + \lambda (2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 3\hat{k})] = 4 + 7\lambda$

$\vec{r} \cdot [(1 + 2\lambda)\hat{i} + (-2 + \lambda)\hat{j} + (3 - 3\lambda)\hat{k}] = 4 + 7\lambda$

Since this plane is parallel to the Y-axis, its normal vector is perpendicular to the Y-axis. The direction vector of the Y-axis is $\hat{j}$.

Taking the dot product of the normal vector with $\hat{j}$ and equating it to zero:

$(-2 + \lambda) = 0$

$\lambda = 2$

Substituting $\lambda = 2$ in the equation of the plane, we get:

$\vec{r} \cdot [(1 + 4)\hat{i} + (-2 + 2)\hat{j} + (3 - 6)\hat{k}] = 4 + 14$

$\vec{r} \cdot (5\hat{i} - 3\hat{k}) = 18$

Answer: $\vec{r} \cdot (5\hat{i} - 3\hat{k}) = 18$

</details>

### Q3. MHT CET 2026 (19 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The planes $5x - 2y + 3z = 9$ and $15x - 6y + 9z = -5$ are

**Options:**

- **(A)** Perpendicular
- **(B)** Intersect each other
- **(C)** Parallel
- **(D)** Pass through $(2, 2, 1)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Parallel**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equations of the given planes are $5x - 2y + 3z = 9$ and $15x - 6y + 9z = -5$.

The direction ratios of the normal to the first plane are $a_1 = 5, b_1 = -2, c_1 = 3$.

The direction ratios of the normal to the second plane are $a_2 = 15, b_2 = -6, c_2 = 9$.

Taking the ratio of the corresponding direction ratios, we get:

$\dfrac{a_1}{a_2} = \dfrac{5}{15} = \dfrac{1}{3}$

$\dfrac{b_1}{b_2} = \dfrac{-2}{-6} = \dfrac{1}{3}$

$\dfrac{c_1}{c_2} = \dfrac{3}{9} = \dfrac{1}{3}$

Since $\dfrac{a_1}{a_2} = \dfrac{b_1}{b_2} = \dfrac{c_1}{c_2}$, the normal vectors of the two planes are parallel.

Also, the ratio of the constant terms is $\dfrac{d_1}{d_2} = \dfrac{9}{-5} \neq \dfrac{1}{3}$, which means the planes are not coincident.

Therefore, the given planes are parallel.

Answer: Parallel

</details>

### Q4. MHT CET 2026 (19 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane that perpendicularly bisects the line segment joining the points $A(2,-1,3)$ and $B(3,1,0)$ is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x + 2y - 3z + 2 = 0$
- **(B)** $x + 2y - 3z - 2 = 0$
- **(C)** $x - 3z + 2 = 0$
- **(D)** $x + 2y - 3z = 0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $x + 2y - 3z + 2 = 0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The plane perpendicularly bisects the line segment joining $A(2,-1,3)$ and $B(3,1,0)$.

The plane passes through the midpoint of $AB$.

Midpoint $M = \left(\dfrac{2+3}{2}, \dfrac{-1+1}{2}, \dfrac{3+0}{2}\right) = \left(\dfrac{5}{2}, 0, \dfrac{3}{2}\right)$

The normal to the plane is parallel to the line segment $AB$.

Direction ratios of the normal $\vec{n} = (3-2, 1-(-1), 0-3) = (1, 2, -3)$

The equation of the plane passing through $M$ with normal $\vec{n}$ is:

$1\left(x - \dfrac{5}{2}\right) + 2(y - 0) - 3\left(z - \dfrac{3}{2}\right) = 0$

$x - \dfrac{5}{2} + 2y - 3z + \dfrac{9}{2} = 0$

$x + 2y - 3z + \dfrac{4}{2} = 0$

$x + 2y - 3z + 2 = 0$

Answer: $x + 2y - 3z + 2 = 0$

</details>

### Q5. MHT CET 2026 (19 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane containing the line $\dfrac{x+1}{4} = \dfrac{y-2}{-1} = \dfrac{z+5}{3}$ and parallel to the vector $\hat{i} + \hat{j} - \hat{k}$ is $ax + by + cz = 9$. Then, the value of $a + b - c$ is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $14$
- **(B)** $-10$
- **(C)** $-14$
- **(D)** $0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The plane contains the line $\dfrac{x+1}{4} = \dfrac{y-2}{-1} = \dfrac{z+5}{3}$, so it passes through the point $(-1, 2, -5)$ and is parallel to the vector $\vec{v}_1 = 4\hat{i} - \hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$.

The plane is also parallel to the vector $\vec{v}_2 = \hat{i} + \hat{j} - \hat{k}$.

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ to the plane is given by the cross product of $\vec{v}_1$ and $\vec{v}_2$:

$\vec{n} = \vec{v}_1 \times \vec{v}_2 = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 4 & -1 & 3 \\ 1 & 1 & -1 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(1 - 3) - \hat{j}(-4 - 3) + \hat{k}(4 - (-1)) = -2\hat{i} + 7\hat{j} + 5\hat{k}$

The equation of the plane passing through $(-1, 2, -5)$ with normal vector $-2\hat{i} + 7\hat{j} + 5\hat{k}$ is:

$-2(x + 1) + 7(y - 2) + 5(z + 5) = 0$

$-2x - 2 + 7y - 14 + 5z + 25 = 0$

$-2x + 7y + 5z + 9 = 0$

$2x - 7y - 5z = 9$

Comparing this with the given equation $ax + by + cz = 9$, we get:

$a = 2$, $b = -7$, $c = -5$

Therefore, the value of $a + b - c$ is:

$a + b - c = 2 + (-7) - (-5) = 2 - 7 + 5 = 0$

Answer: $0$

</details>

### Q6. MHT CET 2026 (15 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane containing the line $\bar{r} = (2\hat{i} - \hat{k}) + \lambda(\hat{i} + \hat{j} + 2\hat{k})$ and perpendicular to the plane $\bar{r} \cdot (3\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k}) = 20$ is……

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\bar{r} \cdot (3\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} - 4\hat{k}) = 10$
- **(B)** $\bar{r} \cdot (3\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} - 4\hat{k}) = 6$
- **(C)** $\bar{r} \cdot (3\hat{i} - 5\hat{j} - 4\hat{k}) = 6$
- **(D)** $\bar{r} \cdot (3\hat{i} - 5\hat{j} - 4\hat{k}) = 10$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\bar{r} \cdot (3\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} - 4\hat{k}) = 10$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The required plane contains the line $\bar{r} = (2\hat{i} - \hat{k}) + \lambda(\hat{i} + \hat{j} + 2\hat{k})$, so it passes through the point with position vector $\vec{a} = 2\hat{i} - \hat{k}$ and is parallel to the vector $\vec{b} = \hat{i} + \hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$.

The plane is perpendicular to the given plane $\bar{r} \cdot (3\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k}) = 20$, so its normal vector $\vec{n}$ is perpendicular to the normal vector of the given plane, $\vec{n}_1 = 3\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k}$.

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ of the required plane is given by the cross product of $\vec{b}$ and $\vec{n}_1$:

$\vec{n} = \vec{b} \times \vec{n}_1 = (\hat{i} + \hat{j} + 2\hat{k}) \times (3\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k})$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(1 - (-2)) - \hat{j}(1 - 6) + \hat{k}(-1 - 3)$

$\vec{n} = 3\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} - 4\hat{k}$

The equation of the required plane is $(\bar{r} - \vec{a}) \cdot \vec{n} = 0$, which can be written as $\bar{r} \cdot \vec{n} = \vec{a} \cdot \vec{n}$.

Substituting the values of $\vec{a}$ and $\vec{n}$:

$\vec{a} \cdot \vec{n} = (2\hat{i} - \hat{k}) \cdot (3\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} - 4\hat{k})$

$\vec{a} \cdot \vec{n} = (2)(3) + (0)(5) + (-1)(-4) = 6 + 0 + 4 = 10$

Therefore, the equation of the plane is $\bar{r} \cdot (3\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} - 4\hat{k}) = 10$.

Answer: $\bar{r} \cdot (3\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} - 4\hat{k}) = 10$

</details>

### Q7. MHT CET 2026 (14 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The distance of the point $2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - \hat{k}$ from the plane $\bar{r} \cdot (\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + 4\hat{k}) = 9$, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $13$ units
- **(B)** $\dfrac{13}{\sqrt{21}}$ units
- **(C)** $\dfrac{15}{\sqrt{21}}$ units
- **(D)** $\dfrac{13}{21}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\dfrac{13}{\sqrt{21}}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

The position vector of the given point is $\vec{a} = 2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - \hat{k}$.

The equation of the plane is $\vec{r} \cdot (\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + 4\hat{k}) = 9$.

The normal vector to the plane is $\vec{n} = \hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + 4\hat{k}$ and $d = 9$.

The perpendicular distance of the point from the plane is given by $D = \dfrac{|\vec{a} \cdot \vec{n} - d|}{|\vec{n}|}$.

Substituting the values, we get:

$\vec{a} \cdot \vec{n} = (2)(1) + (1)(-2) + (-1)(4) = 2 - 2 - 4 = -4$

$|\vec{n}| = \sqrt{1^2 + (-2)^2 + 4^2} = \sqrt{1 + 4 + 16} = \sqrt{21}$

$D = \dfrac{|-4 - 9|}{\sqrt{21}} = \dfrac{13}{\sqrt{21}}$ units.

Answer: $\dfrac{13}{\sqrt{21}}$ units

</details>

### Q8. MHT CET 2026 (14 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $L$ be a line $\dfrac{x-1}{1} = \dfrac{y+2}{3} = \dfrac{z-7}{-1}$ and $P$ be a plane passing through the midpoint of the segment joining $M(-4, 2, 1)$ and $N(2, -4, 3)$ and perpendicular to segment MN. If the line $L$ intersects the plane $P$ at point $S(\alpha, \beta, \gamma)$, then $\alpha + \beta + \gamma = \ldots$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $12$
- **(B)** $10$
- **(C)** $-1$
- **(D)** $-5$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $12$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The midpoint of the segment joining $M(-4, 2, 1)$ and $N(2, -4, 3)$ is given by $\left(\dfrac{-4+2}{2}, \dfrac{2-4}{2}, \dfrac{1+3}{2}\right) = (-1, -1, 2)$.

The direction ratios of the segment $MN$ are $(2 - (-4), -4 - 2, 3 - 1) = (6, -6, 2)$.

The normal vector to the plane $P$ is proportional to the direction ratios of $MN$, which can be simplified to $(3, -3, 1)$.

The equation of the plane $P$ passing through $(-1, -1, 2)$ with normal vector $(3, -3, 1)$ is:

$3(x + 1) - 3(y + 1) + 1(z - 2) = 0$

$3x - 3y + z - 2 = 0$

The equation of the line $L$ is $\dfrac{x-1}{1} = \dfrac{y+2}{3} = \dfrac{z-7}{-1} = t$.

Any point on the line $L$ can be written as $(t + 1, 3t - 2, -t + 7)$.

Substituting this point into the equation of the plane $P$ gives:

$3(t + 1) - 3(3t - 2) + (-t + 7) - 2 = 0$

$3t + 3 - 9t + 6 - t + 7 - 2 = 0$

$-7t + 14 = 0 \Rightarrow t = 2$

Substituting $t = 2$ into the coordinates of the point on the line gives the intersection point $S(\alpha, \beta, \gamma)$:

$\alpha = 2 + 1 = 3$

$\beta = 3(2) - 2 = 4$

$\gamma = -2 + 7 = 5$

Therefore, $\alpha + \beta + \gamma = 3 + 4 + 5 = 12$.

Answer: $12$

</details>

### Q9. MHT CET 2026 (13 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The vector equation of the plane determined by the lines $x = y, z = 0$ and $x + y = 0, z = 0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\vec{r} \cdot (\hat{i} + \hat{j}) = 0$
- **(B)** $\vec{r} \cdot (\hat{i} - \hat{j}) = 0$
- **(C)** $\vec{r} \cdot (2\hat{i}) = 0$
- **(D)** $\vec{r} \cdot \hat{k} = 0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\vec{r} \cdot \hat{k} = 0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The first line is given by $x = y, z = 0$. It passes through the origin and its direction vector is $\vec{b}_1 = \hat{i} + \hat{j}$.

The second line is given by $x + y = 0, z = 0$. It also passes through the origin and its direction vector is $\vec{b}_2 = \hat{i} - \hat{j}$.

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ to the plane containing these two intersecting lines is the cross product of their direction vectors:

$\vec{n} = \vec{b}_1 \times \vec{b}_2 = (\hat{i} + \hat{j}) \times (\hat{i} - \hat{j}) = -\hat{k} - \hat{k} = -2\hat{k}$

Since the plane passes through the origin, its vector equation is $\vec{r} \cdot \vec{n} = 0$.

$\vec{r} \cdot (-2\hat{k}) = 0$

$\vec{r} \cdot \hat{k} = 0$

Alternatively, both lines lie in the $xy$-plane, whose Cartesian equation is $z = 0$. The vector form of $z = 0$ is $\vec{r} \cdot \hat{k} = 0$.

Answer: $\vec{r} \cdot \hat{k} = 0$

</details>

### Q10. MHT CET 2026 (13 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the point $Q(\alpha, \beta, \gamma)$ is the mirror image of $P(10, 15, 20)$ in the plane $\vec{r} = \hat{k} + \lambda(-\hat{i} + \hat{j}) + \mu(-\hat{i} + \hat{k})$, $\lambda, \mu \in \mathbb{R}$ then the value of $3(\alpha + \beta + \gamma)$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-45$
- **(B)** $-120$
- **(C)** $-105$
- **(D)** $-129$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $-129$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the plane is given by $\vec{r} = \vec{a} + \lambda \vec{b} + \mu \vec{c}$, where $\vec{a} = \hat{k}$, $\vec{b} = -\hat{i} + \hat{j}$, and $\vec{c} = -\hat{i} + \hat{k}$.

The normal vector to the plane is $\vec{n} = \vec{b} \times \vec{c} = (-\hat{i} + \hat{j}) \times (-\hat{i} + \hat{k}) = \hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}$.

The equation of the plane is $\vec{r} \cdot \vec{n} = \vec{a} \cdot \vec{n}$, which gives $(x\hat{i} + y\hat{j} + z\hat{k}) \cdot (\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}) = \hat{k} \cdot (\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k})$.

$x + y + z = 1$

Using the formula for the mirror image $(\alpha, \beta, \gamma)$ of a point $(x_1, y_1, z_1)$ in the plane $ax + by + cz + d = 0$:

$\dfrac{\alpha - x_1}{a} = \dfrac{\beta - y_1}{b} = \dfrac{\gamma - z_1}{c} = -2\dfrac{ax_1 + by_1 + cz_1 + d}{a^2 + b^2 + c^2}$

Substituting the given values:

$\dfrac{\alpha - 10}{1} = \dfrac{\beta - 15}{1} = \dfrac{\gamma - 20}{1} = -2\dfrac{10 + 15 + 20 - 1}{1^2 + 1^2 + 1^2}$

$\dfrac{\alpha - 10}{1} = \dfrac{\beta - 15}{1} = \dfrac{\gamma - 20}{1} = -2\left(\dfrac{44}{3}\right) = -\dfrac{88}{3}$

From this, we get:

$\alpha = 10 - \dfrac{88}{3}$

$\beta = 15 - \dfrac{88}{3}$

$\gamma = 20 - \dfrac{88}{3}$

Adding these equations:

$\alpha + \beta + \gamma = 45 - 3\left(\dfrac{88}{3}\right) = 45 - 88 = -43$

Therefore, $3(\alpha + \beta + \gamma) = 3(-43) = -129$.

Answer: $-129$

</details>

---

### Q11. MHT CET 2026 (13 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane passing through the point $(0, 4, 3)$ and containing the line $\dfrac{x - 1}{1} = \dfrac{y - 3}{3} = \dfrac{z - 2}{2}$ is $ax + by + cz + d = 0$, $(a \geq 0)$. The value of $(a + b + c + d)$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0$
- **(B)** $1$
- **(C)** $2$
- **(D)** $3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The plane passes through the point $P(0, 4, 3)$ and contains the line $\dfrac{x - 1}{1} = \dfrac{y - 3}{3} = \dfrac{z - 2}{2}$.

The line passes through the point $Q(1, 3, 2)$ and has a direction vector $\vec{v} = \hat{i} + 3\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$.

The vector joining the points $P$ and $Q$ lies on the plane:
$\vec{PQ} = (1 - 0)\hat{i} + (3 - 4)\hat{j} + (2 - 3)\hat{k} = \hat{i} - \hat{j} - \hat{k}$

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ to the plane is perpendicular to both $\vec{PQ}$ and $\vec{v}$:
$\vec{n} = \vec{PQ} \times \vec{v} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & -1 & -1 \\ 1 & 3 & 2 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(-2 - (-3)) - \hat{j}(2 - (-1)) + \hat{k}(3 - (-1))$
$\vec{n} = \hat{i} - 3\hat{j} + 4\hat{k}$

The equation of the plane passing through $P(0, 4, 3)$ with normal vector $\vec{n} = (1, -3, 4)$ is:
$1(x - 0) - 3(y - 4) + 4(z - 3) = 0$
$x - 3y + 12 + 4z - 12 = 0$
$x - 3y + 4z = 0$

Comparing this with the given equation $ax + by + cz + d = 0$ (where $a \geq 0$), we get:
$a = 1$, $b = -3$, $c = 4$, $d = 0$

The value of $(a + b + c + d)$ is:
$1 - 3 + 4 + 0 = 2$

Answer: $2$

</details>

### Q12. MHT CET 2026 (12 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The volume of the tetrahedron formed by the plane $6x + 4y - 5z - 60 = 0$ and the coordinate planes is ........... cubic units

**Options:**

- **(A)** $300$
- **(B)** $30$
- **(C)** $360$
- **(D)** $400$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $300$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the plane is $6x + 4y - 5z = 60$.

Dividing by $60$, the intercept form of the plane is $\dfrac{x}{10} + \dfrac{y}{15} + \dfrac{z}{-12} = 1$.

The intercepts on the coordinate axes are $a = 10$, $b = 15$, and $c = -12$.

The volume of the tetrahedron formed by the plane and the coordinate planes is given by $V = \dfrac{1}{6} |abc|$.

Substituting the values, we get $V = \dfrac{1}{6} |10 \times 15 \times (-12)| = \dfrac{1800}{6} = 300$ cubic units.

Answer: $300$

</details>

### Q13. MHT CET 2026 (12 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The ratio in which the plane $\bar{r}\cdot(\hat{i}-2\hat{j}+3\hat{k})=17$ divides the line segment joining the points $(-1, 4, 7)$ and $(3, -5, 8)$ is ...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1:5$
- **(B)** $2:7$
- **(C)** $3:8$
- **(D)** $1:4$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $1:4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The Cartesian equation of the given plane is $x - 2y + 3z = 17$.

Let the plane divide the line segment joining the points $A(-1, 4, 7)$ and $B(3, -5, 8)$ in the ratio $\lambda : 1$.

The coordinates of the point of intersection are given by section formula as $\left(\dfrac{3\lambda - 1}{\lambda + 1}, \dfrac{-5\lambda + 4}{\lambda + 1}, \dfrac{8\lambda + 7}{\lambda + 1}\right)$.

Since this point lies on the plane $x - 2y + 3z = 17$, we substitute the coordinates into the equation of the plane:

$\dfrac{3\lambda - 1}{\lambda + 1} - 2\left(\dfrac{-5\lambda + 4}{\lambda + 1}\right) + 3\left(\dfrac{8\lambda + 7}{\lambda + 1}\right) = 17$

$3\lambda - 1 + 10\lambda - 8 + 24\lambda + 21 = 17(\lambda + 1)$

$37\lambda + 12 = 17\lambda + 17$

$20\lambda = 5$

$\lambda = \dfrac{1}{4}$

The required ratio is $1 : 4$.

Answer: $1:4$

</details>

### Q14. MHT CET 2026 (20 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the perpendicular distance of the plane passing through the point $Q(1, 0, -1)$ and containing the line $\vec{r} = (\hat{i} - 3\hat{j} + \hat{k}) + \lambda(2\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + \hat{k})$ from origin is $\dfrac{p}{\sqrt{53}}$ then $p = $ ...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4$
- **(B)** $1$
- **(C)** $5$
- **(D)** $9$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $5$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The plane contains the point $Q(1, 0, -1)$ and the line $\vec{r} = (\hat{i} - 3\hat{j} + \hat{k}) + \lambda(2\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + \hat{k})$.

The line passes through the point $A(1, -3, 1)$ and has the direction vector $\vec{b} = 2\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + \hat{k}$.

Since the plane contains both the point $Q$ and the line, it contains the vector $\overrightarrow{QA}$ joining $Q$ and $A$.

$\overrightarrow{QA} = (1 - 1)\hat{i} + (-3 - 0)\hat{j} + (1 - (-1))\hat{k} = -3\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ to the plane is perpendicular to both $\vec{b}$ and $\overrightarrow{QA}$.

$\vec{n} = \vec{b} \times \overrightarrow{QA} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & -2 & 1 \\ 0 & -3 & 2 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(-4 - (-3)) - \hat{j}(4 - 0) + \hat{k}(-6 - 0) = -\hat{i} - 4\hat{j} - 6\hat{k}$

Taking the normal vector as $\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 6\hat{k}$, the equation of the plane passing through $Q(1, 0, -1)$ is:

$1(x - 1) + 4(y - 0) + 6(z + 1) = 0$

$x + 4y + 6z + 5 = 0$

The perpendicular distance of this plane from the origin $(0, 0, 0)$ is:

$d = \dfrac{|1(0) + 4(0) + 6(0) + 5|}{\sqrt{1^2 + 4^2 + 6^2}} = \dfrac{5}{\sqrt{1 + 16 + 36}} = \dfrac{5}{\sqrt{53}}$

Comparing this with the given distance $\dfrac{p}{\sqrt{53}}$, we get $p = 5$.

Answer: $5$

</details>

### Q15. MHT CET 2026 (20 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A plane meets the co-ordinate axes in A, B, C such that the centroid of the triangle ABC is the point $(1, r, r^2)$, then the equation of the plane is,

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x + ry + r^2 z = 3r^2$
- **(B)** $r^2 x + ry + z = 3r^2$
- **(C)** $x + ry + r^2 z = 3$
- **(D)** $r^2 x + ry + z = 3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $r^2 x + ry + z = 3r^2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the coordinates of the points where the plane meets the axes be $A(a, 0, 0)$, $B(0, b, 0)$, and $C(0, 0, c)$.

The equation of the plane in intercept form is $\dfrac{x}{a} + \dfrac{y}{b} + \dfrac{z}{c} = 1$.

The centroid of the triangle $ABC$ is given by $\left( \dfrac{a}{3}, \dfrac{b}{3}, \dfrac{c}{3} \right)$.

Given that the centroid is $(1, r, r^2)$, we have:

$\dfrac{a}{3} = 1 \Rightarrow a = 3$

$\dfrac{b}{3} = r \Rightarrow b = 3r$

$\dfrac{c}{3} = r^2 \Rightarrow c = 3r^2$

Substituting the values of $a$, $b$, and $c$ into the equation of the plane, we get:

$\dfrac{x}{3} + \dfrac{y}{3r} + \dfrac{z}{3r^2} = 1$

Multiplying the entire equation by $3r^2$, we obtain:

$r^2 x + ry + z = 3r^2$

Answer: $r^2 x + ry + z = 3r^2$

</details>

### Q16. MHT CET 2026 (19 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The perpendicular distance from the origin to the plane containing the points $(1, -2, 1), (2, -1, -3)$ and $(0, 1, 5)$ is...(in units)

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{17}}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{3}{\sqrt{26}}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{5}{\sqrt{17}}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{7}{\sqrt{26}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\dfrac{5}{\sqrt{17}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the given points be $A(1, -2, 1)$, $B(2, -1, -3)$ and $C(0, 1, 5)$.

The vectors in the plane are:
$\vec{AB} = (2 - 1)\hat{i} + (-1 - (-2))\hat{j} + (-3 - 1)\hat{k} = \hat{i} + \hat{j} - 4\hat{k}$
$\vec{AC} = (0 - 1)\hat{i} + (1 - (-2))\hat{j} + (5 - 1)\hat{k} = -\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} + 4\hat{k}$

The normal vector to the plane is given by the cross product $\vec{n} = \vec{AB} \times \vec{AC}$:
$\vec{n} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & 1 & -4 \\ -1 & 3 & 4 \end{vmatrix} = \hat{i}(4 - (-12)) - \hat{j}(4 - 4) + \hat{k}(3 - (-1)) = 16\hat{i} + 4\hat{k}$

A simpler normal vector is $\vec{n}' = 4\hat{i} + \hat{k}$.

The equation of the plane passing through $A(1, -2, 1)$ with normal vector $4\hat{i} + \hat{k}$ is:
$4(x - 1) + 0(y - (-2)) + 1(z - 1) = 0$
$4x + z - 5 = 0$

The perpendicular distance from the origin $(0, 0, 0)$ to the plane $4x + z - 5 = 0$ is:
$d = \dfrac{|4(0) + 0(0) + 1(0) - 5|}{\sqrt{4^2 + 0^2 + 1^2}} = \dfrac{5}{\sqrt{17}}$

Answer: $\dfrac{5}{\sqrt{17}}$

</details>

### Q17. MHT CET 2026 (19 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let $\vec{r} \cdot (3\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + 7\hat{k}) = 32$ is the equation of a plane and the line having direction ratios $(5, b, 3)$ is parallel to the plane, then the value of b is...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $b = 13$
- **(B)** $b = 15$
- **(C)** $b = 16$
- **(D)** $b = 18$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $b = 18$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the plane is $\vec{r} \cdot (3\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + 7\hat{k}) = 32$.

The normal vector to the plane is $\vec{n} = 3\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + 7\hat{k}$.

The direction vector of the line is $\vec{d} = 5\hat{i} + b\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$.

Since the line is parallel to the plane, its direction vector is perpendicular to the normal vector of the plane.

$\vec{n} \cdot \vec{d} = 0$

$(3)(5) + (-2)(b) + (7)(3) = 0$

$15 - 2b + 21 = 0$

$36 - 2b = 0$

$b = 18$

Answer: $b = 18$

</details>

### Q18. MHT CET 2026 (19 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The plane $\dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{y}{3} + \dfrac{z}{4} = 1$ cuts the axes at the points A, B, C then the area of triangle ABC is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{29}$
- **(B)** $\sqrt{41}$
- **(C)** $\sqrt{61}$
- **(D)** $\sqrt{51}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\sqrt{61}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The plane $\dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{y}{3} + \dfrac{z}{4} = 1$ intersects the coordinate axes at $A(2, 0, 0)$, $B(0, 3, 0)$, and $C(0, 0, 4)$.

The area of $\Delta ABC$ is given by $\Delta = \sqrt{\Delta_{xy}^2 + \Delta_{yz}^2 + \Delta_{zx}^2}$, where $\Delta_{xy}$, $\Delta_{yz}$, and $\Delta_{zx}$ are the areas of the projections of $\Delta ABC$ on the $xy$, $yz$, and $zx$ planes respectively.

$\Delta_{xy} = \dfrac{1}{2} \times 2 \times 3 = 3$

$\Delta_{yz} = \dfrac{1}{2} \times 3 \times 4 = 6$

$\Delta_{zx} = \dfrac{1}{2} \times 4 \times 2 = 4$

$\Delta = \sqrt{3^2 + 6^2 + 4^2} = \sqrt{9 + 36 + 16} = \sqrt{61}$

Answer: $\sqrt{61}$

</details>

### Q19. MHT CET 2026 (18 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the equation of a plane passing through $A(1, p, 2)$, $B(3, 2, 4)$ and parallel to the z axis, is $3x - 2y - q = 0$, then $\ldots$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $p = -1,\ q = 5$
- **(B)** $p = 1,\ q = -5$
- **(C)** $p = -2,\ q = -5$
- **(D)** $p = 2,\ q = -5$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $p = -1,\ q = 5$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the plane is given as $3x - 2y - q = 0$.

Since the plane passes through the point $B(3, 2, 4)$, its coordinates must satisfy the plane equation. Substituting $x = 3$ and $y = 2$:

$3(3) - 2(2) - q = 0$

$9 - 4 - q = 0 \Rightarrow q = 5$

The plane also passes through the point $A(1, p, 2)$. Substituting $x = 1$, $y = p$, and $q = 5$ into the plane equation:

$3(1) - 2(p) - 5 = 0$

$3 - 2p - 5 = 0$

$-2p = 2 \Rightarrow p = -1$

Thus, $p = -1$ and $q = 5$.

Answer: $p = -1,\ q = 5$

</details>

### Q20. MHT CET 2026 (18 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The acute angle $\theta$ between the $xy$-plane and the plane passing through the point $(1, 2, 4)$ and parallel to the vectors with direction ratios $3, 2, -1$ and $1, -2, -2$ is...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\cos^{-1}\left(\dfrac{8}{5\sqrt{5}}\right)$
- **(B)** $\cos^{-1}\left(\dfrac{5}{8\sqrt{5}}\right)$
- **(C)** $\sin^{-1}\left(\dfrac{8}{5\sqrt{5}}\right)$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{\pi}{4}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\cos^{-1}\left(\dfrac{8}{5\sqrt{5}}\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the vectors parallel to the plane be $\vec{v}_1 = 3\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - \hat{k}$ and $\vec{v}_2 = \hat{i} - 2\hat{j} - 2\hat{k}$.

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ to the plane is given by the cross product of $\vec{v}_1$ and $\vec{v}_2$:

$\vec{n} = \vec{v}_1 \times \vec{v}_2 = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 3 & 2 & -1 \\ 1 & -2 & -2 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(-4 - 2) - \hat{j}(-6 - (-1)) + \hat{k}(-6 - 2) = -6\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} - 8\hat{k}$

The normal vector to the $xy$-plane is $\hat{k}$.

The acute angle $\theta$ between the two planes is the acute angle between their normal vectors, given by:

$\cos \theta = \dfrac{|\vec{n} \cdot \hat{k}|}{|\vec{n}| |\hat{k}|}$

$\cos \theta = \dfrac{|(-6\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} - 8\hat{k}) \cdot \hat{k}|}{\sqrt{(-6)^2 + 5^2 + (-8)^2} \sqrt{1^2}}$

$\cos \theta = \dfrac{|-8|}{\sqrt{36 + 25 + 64}} = \dfrac{8}{\sqrt{125}} = \dfrac{8}{5\sqrt{5}}$

$\theta = \cos^{-1}\left(\dfrac{8}{5\sqrt{5}}\right)$

Answer: $\cos^{-1}\left(\dfrac{8}{5\sqrt{5}}\right)$

</details>

### Q21. MHT CET 2026 (18 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the plane $\bar{r} = (\lambda + \mu)\hat{i} + (2 + \mu)\hat{j} + (3\lambda + 2\mu)\hat{k}$, where $\lambda$ and $\mu$ are parameters, intersects coordinate axes at points $(a, 0, 0)$, $(0, b, 0)$ and $(0, 0, c)$ then $a + b + c = $...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{7}{2}$
- **(B)** $5$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{8}{3}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{10}{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\dfrac{10}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The parametric equations of the plane are given by:

$x = \lambda + \mu$

$y = 2 + \mu$

$z = 3\lambda + 2\mu$

From the second equation, we have $\mu = y - 2$.

Substituting $\mu$ into the first equation:

$x = \lambda + y - 2 \Rightarrow \lambda = x - y + 2$

Now, substituting both $\lambda$ and $\mu$ into the third equation to eliminate the parameters:

$z = 3(x - y + 2) + 2(y - 2)$

$z = 3x - 3y + 6 + 2y - 4$

$z = 3x - y + 2$

Rearranging this, we get the Cartesian equation of the plane:

$3x - y - z + 2 = 0$

To find the $x$-intercept $a$, put $y = 0$ and $z = 0$:

$3a - 0 - 0 + 2 = 0 \Rightarrow a = -\dfrac{2}{3}$

To find the $y$-intercept $b$, put $x = 0$ and $z = 0$:

$0 - b - 0 + 2 = 0 \Rightarrow b = 2$

To find the $z$-intercept $c$, put $x = 0$ and $y = 0$:

$0 - 0 - c + 2 = 0 \Rightarrow c = 2$

The sum of the intercepts is:

$a + b + c = -\dfrac{2}{3} + 2 + 2 = 4 - \dfrac{2}{3} = \dfrac{10}{3}$

Answer: $\dfrac{10}{3}$

</details>

### Q22. MHT CET 2026 (18 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the plane $2x + 3y + z = 6$ cuts coordinate axes at A, B and C, then the volume of tetrahedron OABC (where O is the origin) is ......cubic units.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $30$
- **(B)** $6$
- **(C)** $36$
- **(D)** $180$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $6$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the plane is $2x + 3y + z = 6$.

Dividing by $6$, the intercept form of the plane is $\dfrac{x}{3} + \dfrac{y}{2} + \dfrac{z}{6} = 1$.

The intercepts on the coordinate axes are $a = 3$, $b = 2$, and $c = 6$.

The coordinates of the vertices of the tetrahedron are $A(3, 0, 0)$, $B(0, 2, 0)$, and $C(0, 0, 6)$, with $O(0, 0, 0)$ as the origin.

The volume of the tetrahedron $OABC$ is given by $V = \dfrac{1}{6}abc$.

Substituting the values, $V = \dfrac{1}{6}(3)(2)(6) = 6$ cubic units.

Answer: $6$

</details>

### Q23. MHT CET 2026 (16 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The vector equation of plane in parametric form, passing through the points (-1, 2, 0), (2, 2, -1) and parallel to the line $\dfrac{x-1}{1} = \dfrac{2y+1}{2} = \dfrac{z+1}{-1}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\bar{r} = (-\hat{i} + 2\hat{j}) + \lambda(3\hat{i} - \hat{k}) + \mu(\hat{i} + \hat{j} - \hat{k})$
- **(B)** $\bar{r} = (-\hat{i} + 2\hat{j}) + \lambda(3\hat{i} - \hat{k}) + \mu(\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - \hat{k})$
- **(C)** $\bar{r} = (\hat{i} - 2\hat{j}) + \lambda(3\hat{i} + \hat{k}) + \mu(\hat{i} + \hat{j} - \hat{k})$
- **(D)** $\bar{r} = (-\hat{i} + 2\hat{j}) + \lambda(3\hat{i} - \hat{k}) + \mu(\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\bar{r} = (-\hat{i} + 2\hat{j}) + \lambda(3\hat{i} - \hat{k}) + \mu(\hat{i} + \hat{j} - \hat{k})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The position vector of the point $A(-1, 2, 0)$ is $\vec{a} = -\hat{i} + 2\hat{j}$.

The plane passes through $A(-1, 2, 0)$ and $B(2, 2, -1)$. A vector parallel to the plane is $\vec{AB} = (2 - (-1))\hat{i} + (2 - 2)\hat{j} + (-1 - 0)\hat{k} = 3\hat{i} - \hat{k}$.

The given line is $\dfrac{x-1}{1} = \dfrac{2y+1}{2} = \dfrac{z+1}{-1}$. Rewriting it in standard form, we get $\dfrac{x-1}{1} = \dfrac{y+1/2}{1} = \dfrac{z+1}{-1}$.

The direction vector of the line is $\vec{v} = \hat{i} + \hat{j} - \hat{k}$. Since the plane is parallel to this line, $\vec{v}$ is also parallel to the plane.

The parametric vector equation of a plane passing through a point with position vector $\vec{a}$ and parallel to two non-collinear vectors $\vec{b}$ and $\vec{c}$ is given by $\vec{r} = \vec{a} + \lambda\vec{b} + \mu\vec{c}$.

Substituting $\vec{a} = -\hat{i} + 2\hat{j}$, $\vec{b} = \vec{AB} = 3\hat{i} - \hat{k}$, and $\vec{c} = \vec{v} = \hat{i} + \hat{j} - \hat{k}$, we get:

$\vec{r} = (-\hat{i} + 2\hat{j}) + \lambda(3\hat{i} - \hat{k}) + \mu(\hat{i} + \hat{j} - \hat{k})$

Answer: $\bar{r} = (-\hat{i} + 2\hat{j}) + \lambda(3\hat{i} - \hat{k}) + \mu(\hat{i} + \hat{j} - \hat{k})$

</details>

---

### Q24. MHT CET 2026 (16 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $d$ is the distance of point (2, 5, 10) from the plane containing the lines $\bar{r} = (4\hat{j} - \hat{k}) + \lambda(\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - 2\hat{k})$ and $\bar{r} = (2\hat{i} + \hat{j}) + \mu(\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - 2\hat{k})$, then $d^2 = $

**Options:**

- **(A)** $145$
- **(B)** $13$
- **(C)** $90$
- **(D)** $79$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $90$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given lines are:
$\bar{r} = (4\hat{j} - \hat{k}) + \lambda(\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - 2\hat{k})$
$\bar{r} = (2\hat{i} + \hat{j}) + \mu(\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - 2\hat{k})$

The lines are parallel with direction vector $\vec{b} = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - 2\hat{k}$.
Let $A(0, 4, -1)$ be a point on the first line and $B(2, 1, 0)$ be a point on the second line.
The vector joining $A$ and $B$ is $\vec{AB} = 2\hat{i} - 3\hat{j} + \hat{k}$.

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ to the plane containing these two lines is perpendicular to both $\vec{b}$ and $\vec{AB}$:
$\vec{n} = \vec{b} \times \vec{AB} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & 2 & -2 \\ 2 & -3 & 1 \end{vmatrix}$
$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(2 - 6) - \hat{j}(1 - (-4)) + \hat{k}(-3 - 4) = -4\hat{i} - 5\hat{j} - 7\hat{k}$

Taking the normal vector as $4\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} + 7\hat{k}$, the equation of the plane passing through $A(0, 4, -1)$ is:
$4(x - 0) + 5(y - 4) + 7(z + 1) = 0$
$4x + 5y + 7z - 13 = 0$

The perpendicular distance $d$ of the point $P(2, 5, 10)$ from this plane is:
$d = \dfrac{|4(2) + 5(5) + 7(10) - 13|}{\sqrt{4^2 + 5^2 + 7^2}}$
$d = \dfrac{|8 + 25 + 70 - 13|}{\sqrt{16 + 25 + 49}}$
$d = \dfrac{90}{\sqrt{90}} = \sqrt{90}$

Therefore, $d^2 = 90$.

Answer: $90$

</details>

### Q25. MHT CET 2026 (16 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The direction ratios of the normal to the plane passing through (1, 0, 0), (0, 1, 0) which makes an angle of measure $45^\circ$ with the plane $2x + 3y = 7$ are....

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{6},\ 1,\ 1$
- **(B)** $1,\ 1,\ \sqrt{6}$
- **(C)** $\sqrt{13},\ \sqrt{13},\ \sqrt{6}$
- **(D)** $\sqrt{13},\ \sqrt{13},\ 2\sqrt{6}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\sqrt{13},\ \sqrt{13},\ 2\sqrt{6}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the equation of the plane be $ax + by + cz + d = 0$.

Since the plane passes through $(1, 0, 0)$ and $(0, 1, 0)$, we have:
$a + d = 0 \Rightarrow a = -d$
$b + d = 0 \Rightarrow b = -d$

The direction ratios of the normal to the plane are $(a, b, c) = (-d, -d, c)$.
Dividing by $-d$, the direction ratios can be written as $(1, 1, k)$, where $k = -\dfrac{c}{d}$.

The normal to the given plane $2x + 3y = 7$ has direction ratios $(2, 3, 0)$.

The angle between the planes is $45^\circ$. Using the formula for the angle between two normals:
$\cos 45^\circ = \dfrac{|1(2) + 1(3) + k(0)|}{\sqrt{1^2 + 1^2 + k^2} \sqrt{2^2 + 3^2 + 0^2}}$

$\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}} = \dfrac{5}{\sqrt{2 + k^2} \sqrt{13}}$

Squaring both sides:
$\dfrac{1}{2} = \dfrac{25}{13(2 + k^2)}$

$13(2 + k^2) = 50$

$26 + 13k^2 = 50$

$13k^2 = 24$

$k^2 = \dfrac{24}{13} \Rightarrow k = \pm \dfrac{2\sqrt{6}}{\sqrt{13}}$

The direction ratios of the normal are $\left(1, 1, \pm \dfrac{2\sqrt{6}}{\sqrt{13}}\right)$.

Multiplying by $\sqrt{13}$, the direction ratios are $(\sqrt{13}, \sqrt{13}, \pm 2\sqrt{6})$.

Answer: $\sqrt{13},\ \sqrt{13},\ 2\sqrt{6}$

</details>

---

### Q26. MHT CET 2026 (15 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane passing through the point $(1,2,1)$ and perpendicular to the planes $x + 2y + 2z - 7 = 0$ and $3x + 3y + 2z - 5 = 0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2x + y - 2z - 2 = 0$
- **(B)** $2x - 4y + 3z + 3 = 0$
- **(C)** $2x + 4y - 5z - 5 = 0$
- **(D)** $x + 4y - 6z - 3 = 0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $2x - 4y + 3z + 3 = 0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the normal vector to the required plane be $\vec{n}$.

Since the required plane is perpendicular to the given planes, its normal vector is perpendicular to the normal vectors of the given planes, $\vec{n}_1 = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$ and $\vec{n}_2 = 3\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$.

$\vec{n} = \vec{n}_1 \times \vec{n}_2 = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & 2 & 2 \\ 3 & 3 & 2 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(4 - 6) - \hat{j}(2 - 6) + \hat{k}(3 - 6) = -2\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} - 3\hat{k}$

The equation of the plane passing through $(1, 2, 1)$ with normal vector $\vec{n}$ is:

$-2(x - 1) + 4(y - 2) - 3(z - 1) = 0$

$-2x + 2 + 4y - 8 - 3z + 3 = 0$

$-2x + 4y - 3z - 3 = 0$

$2x - 4y + 3z + 3 = 0$

Answer: $2x - 4y + 3z + 3 = 0$

</details>

### Q27. MHT CET 2026 (15 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane containing the lines $\dfrac{x-1}{2} = \dfrac{y+1}{\lambda} = \dfrac{z}{2}$ and $\dfrac{x+1}{5} = \dfrac{y+1}{2} = \dfrac{z}{\lambda}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x \pm y + 1 = 0$
- **(B)** $y \pm z + 1 = 0$
- **(C)** $x \pm z + 1 = 0$
- **(D)** $y \pm z - 1 = 0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $y \pm z + 1 = 0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For the two lines to be coplanar, the shortest distance between them must be zero.

The first line passes through $A(1, -1, 0)$ with direction ratios $\vec{d}_1 = (2, \lambda, 2)$.

The second line passes through $B(-1, -1, 0)$ with direction ratios $\vec{d}_2 = (5, 2, \lambda)$.

The vector joining the points is $\vec{AB} = (-2, 0, 0)$.

The condition for coplanarity is $[\vec{AB}, \vec{d}_1, \vec{d}_2] = 0$:

$\begin{vmatrix} -2 & 0 & 0 \\ 2 & \lambda & 2 \\ 5 & 2 & \lambda \end{vmatrix} = 0$

$-2(\lambda^2 - 4) = 0 \Rightarrow \lambda = \pm 2$

Case 1: $\lambda = 2$

$\vec{d}_1 = (2, 2, 2)$ and $\vec{d}_2 = (5, 2, 2)$.

The normal vector to the plane is $\vec{n} = \vec{d}_1 \times \vec{d}_2 = (0, 6, -6)$, which is parallel to $(0, 1, -1)$.

The equation of the plane passing through $A(1, -1, 0)$ is:

$0(x - 1) + 1(y + 1) - 1(z - 0) = 0 \Rightarrow y - z + 1 = 0$

Case 2: $\lambda = -2$

$\vec{d}_1 = (2, -2, 2)$ and $\vec{d}_2 = (5, 2, -2)$.

The normal vector to the plane is $\vec{n} = \vec{d}_1 \times \vec{d}_2 = (0, 14, 14)$, which is parallel to $(0, 1, 1)$.

The equation of the plane passing through $A(1, -1, 0)$ is:

$0(x - 1) + 1(y + 1) + 1(z - 0) = 0 \Rightarrow y + z + 1 = 0$

Combining both cases, the equation of the plane is $y \pm z + 1 = 0$.

Answer: $y \pm z + 1 = 0$

</details>

---

### Q28. MHT CET 2026 (13 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The vector equation of the plane which is at a distance of $5$ units from the origin and normal to the vector $2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\vec{r} \cdot (2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}) = 12$
- **(B)** $\vec{r} \cdot (2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}) = 15$
- **(C)** $\vec{r} \cdot (2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}) = 9$
- **(D)** $\vec{r} \cdot (2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}) = 18$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\vec{r} \cdot (2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}) = 15$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The normal vector to the plane is $\vec{n} = 2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}$.

The magnitude of the normal vector is $|\vec{n}| = \sqrt{2^2 + 1^2 + (-2)^2} = \sqrt{9} = 3$.

The unit normal vector is $\hat{n} = \dfrac{\vec{n}}{|\vec{n}|} = \dfrac{2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}}{3}$.

The vector equation of a plane at a perpendicular distance $d$ from the origin is given by $\vec{r} \cdot \hat{n} = d$.

Substituting $d = 5$ and $\hat{n} = \dfrac{2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}}{3}$, we get:

$\vec{r} \cdot \left( \dfrac{2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}}{3} \right) = 5$

$\vec{r} \cdot (2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}) = 15$

Answer: $\vec{r} \cdot (2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}) = 15$

</details>

---

### Q29. MHT CET 2026 (13 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane passing through the intersection of planes $2x - y + z = 3$, $4x - 3y + 5z = -9$ and parallel to the line $\dfrac{x+1}{2} = \dfrac{y+3}{4} = \dfrac{z-3}{5}$ is...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $11x - 3y - 2z - 54 = 0$
- **(B)** $11x + 3y - 2z - 54 = 0$
- **(C)** $11x - 3y + 2z - 54 = 0$
- **(D)** $11x - 3y - 2z + 54 = 0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $11x - 3y - 2z - 54 = 0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the plane passing through the intersection of the given planes is

$(2x - y + z - 3) + \lambda (4x - 3y + 5z + 9) = 0$

$(2 + 4\lambda)x - (1 + 3\lambda)y + (1 + 5\lambda)z - (3 - 9\lambda) = 0$

Since this plane is parallel to the line $\dfrac{x+1}{2} = \dfrac{y+3}{4} = \dfrac{z-3}{5}$, its normal is perpendicular to the line. Therefore,

$2(2 + 4\lambda) - 4(1 + 3\lambda) + 5(1 + 5\lambda) = 0$

$4 + 8\lambda - 4 - 12\lambda + 5 + 25\lambda = 0$

$21\lambda + 5 = 0 \Rightarrow \lambda = -\dfrac{5}{21}$

Substituting $\lambda = -\dfrac{5}{21}$ in the equation of the plane, we get

$(2x - y + z - 3) - \dfrac{5}{21}(4x - 3y + 5z + 9) = 0$

$21(2x - y + z - 3) - 5(4x - 3y + 5z + 9) = 0$

$42x - 21y + 21z - 63 - 20x + 15y - 25z - 45 = 0$

$22x - 6y - 4z - 108 = 0$

$11x - 3y - 2z - 54 = 0$

Answer: $11x - 3y - 2z - 54 = 0$

</details>

---

### Q30. MHT CET 2026 (13 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The co-ordinates of the foot of the perpendicular from the origin to the plane $2x - 3y - 6z = 49$ are...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(2, -3, -6)$
- **(B)** $\left(\dfrac{2}{7}, \dfrac{-3}{7}, \dfrac{-6}{7}\right)$
- **(C)** $(-2, 3, 6)$
- **(D)** $\left(\dfrac{-2}{7}, \dfrac{3}{7}, \dfrac{6}{7}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $(2, -3, -6)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the plane is $2x - 3y - 6z = 49$.

The direction ratios of the normal to the plane are $(2, -3, -6)$.

The equation of the line passing through the origin and perpendicular to the plane is $\dfrac{x}{2} = \dfrac{y}{-3} = \dfrac{z}{-6} = k$.

Any point on this line is of the form $(2k, -3k, -6k)$.

Since this point is the foot of the perpendicular, it must lie on the plane. Substituting these coordinates into the equation of the plane:

$2(2k) - 3(-3k) - 6(-6k) = 49$

$4k + 9k + 36k = 49$

$49k = 49 \Rightarrow k = 1$

Therefore, the coordinates of the foot of the perpendicular are $(2(1), -3(1), -6(1)) = (2, -3, -6)$.

Answer: $(2, -3, -6)$

</details>

---

### Q31. MHT CET 2026 (13 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let a plane P pass through the point $(3, 7, -7)$ and contain the line $\dfrac{x - 2}{-3} = \dfrac{y - 3}{2} = \dfrac{z + 2}{1}$. If the distance of the plane P from the origin is d, then $d^2$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2$
- **(B)** $3$
- **(C)** $4$
- **(D)** $6$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The plane contains the line $\dfrac{x - 2}{-3} = \dfrac{y - 3}{2} = \dfrac{z + 2}{1}$, so it passes through the point $B(2, 3, -2)$ and is parallel to the vector $\vec{b} = -3\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + \hat{k}$.

The plane also passes through the point $A(3, 7, -7)$.

The vector joining $A$ and $B$ is $\vec{AB} = (2 - 3)\hat{i} + (3 - 7)\hat{j} + (-2 - (-7))\hat{k} = -\hat{i} - 4\hat{j} + 5\hat{k}$.

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ to the plane is perpendicular to both $\vec{b}$ and $\vec{AB}$.

$\vec{n} = \vec{b} \times \vec{AB} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \ -3 & 2 & 1 \ -1 & -4 & 5 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(10 + 4) - \hat{j}(-15 + 1) + \hat{k}(12 + 2) = 14\hat{i} + 14\hat{j} + 14\hat{k}$.

The direction ratios of the normal are proportional to $1, 1, 1$.

The equation of the plane passing through $B(2, 3, -2)$ with normal direction ratios $1, 1, 1$ is:

$1(x - 2) + 1(y - 3) + 1(z + 2) = 0$

$x + y + z - 3 = 0$

The distance $d$ of the plane from the origin $(0, 0, 0)$ is:

$d = \dfrac{|0 + 0 + 0 - 3|}{\sqrt{1^2 + 1^2 + 1^2}} = \dfrac{3}{\sqrt{3}} = \sqrt{3}$

$d^2 = 3$

</details>

### Q32. MHT CET 2026 (13 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The plane $\dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{y}{3} + \dfrac{z}{4} = 1$ cuts the co-ordinate axes at the points A, B, C respectively. Then the area of triangle ABC is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{61}$ sq. units
- **(B)** $\dfrac{\sqrt{61}}{2}$ sq. units
- **(C)** $\dfrac{\sqrt{61}}{4}$ sq. units
- **(D)** $\dfrac{\sqrt{71}}{2}$ sq. units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\sqrt{61}$ sq. units**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the plane is given by $\dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{y}{3} + \dfrac{z}{4} = 1$.

The intercepts on the coordinate axes are $a = 2$, $b = 3$, and $c = 4$.

The coordinates of the points where the plane cuts the axes are $A(2, 0, 0)$, $B(0, 3, 0)$, and $C(0, 0, 4)$.

The area of the triangle $ABC$ can be found using the areas of its projections on the coordinate planes, denoted as $\Delta_{xy}$, $\Delta_{yz}$, and $\Delta_{zx}$.

$\Delta_{xy} = \dfrac{1}{2}ab = \dfrac{1}{2}(2)(3) = 3$

$\Delta_{yz} = \dfrac{1}{2}bc = \dfrac{1}{2}(3)(4) = 6$

$\Delta_{zx} = \dfrac{1}{2}ca = \dfrac{1}{2}(4)(2) = 4$

The total area of the triangle is given by $\Delta = \sqrt{\Delta_{xy}^2 + \Delta_{yz}^2 + \Delta_{zx}^2}$.

$\Delta = \sqrt{3^2 + 6^2 + 4^2}$

$\Delta = \sqrt{9 + 36 + 16} = \sqrt{61}$ sq. units.

Answer: $\sqrt{61}$ sq. units

</details>

---

### Q33. MHT CET 2026 (11 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The vector equation of the plane passing through the point $A(-2, 7, 5)$ and parallel to the vectors $4\hat{i} - \hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$ and $\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}$ is ...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\bar{r} \cdot (-2\hat{i} + 7\hat{j} + 5\hat{k}) = 26$
- **(B)** $\bar{r} \cdot (4\hat{i} - \hat{j} + 3\hat{k}) = 27$
- **(C)** $\bar{r} \cdot (4\hat{i} - \hat{j} + 5\hat{k}) = 26$
- **(D)** $\bar{r} \cdot (-4\hat{i} - \hat{j} + 5\hat{k}) = 26$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\bar{r} \cdot (-4\hat{i} - \hat{j} + 5\hat{k}) = 26$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ to the plane is perpendicular to both the given vectors. Thus, it is given by their cross product:

$\vec{n} = (4\hat{i} - \hat{j} + 3\hat{k}) \times (\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k})$

$\vec{n} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 4 & -1 & 3 \\ 1 & 1 & 1 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(-1 - 3) - \hat{j}(4 - 3) + \hat{k}(4 + 1)$

$\vec{n} = -4\hat{i} - \hat{j} + 5\hat{k}$

The plane passes through the point $A(-2, 7, 5)$, so its position vector is $\vec{a} = -2\hat{i} + 7\hat{j} + 5\hat{k}$.

The vector equation of the plane is given by $\vec{r} \cdot \vec{n} = \vec{a} \cdot \vec{n}$.

Calculating $\vec{a} \cdot \vec{n}$:

$\vec{a} \cdot \vec{n} = (-2\hat{i} + 7\hat{j} + 5\hat{k}) \cdot (-4\hat{i} - \hat{j} + 5\hat{k})$

$\vec{a} \cdot \vec{n} = (-2)(-4) + (7)(-1) + (5)(5)$

$\vec{a} \cdot \vec{n} = 8 - 7 + 25 = 26$

Substituting these into the equation of the plane, we get:

$\vec{r} \cdot (-4\hat{i} - \hat{j} + 5\hat{k}) = 26$

Answer: $\bar{r} \cdot (-4\hat{i} - \hat{j} + 5\hat{k}) = 26$

</details>

---

### Q34. MHT CET 2026 (11 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the points $(1, 1, \mu)$ and $(-3, 0, 1)$ are equidistant from the plane $\vec{r} \cdot (3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} - 12\hat{k}) + 13 = 0$, then the value of $\mu$ are

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1, \dfrac{-7}{3}$
- **(B)** $1, \dfrac{7}{3}$
- **(C)** $-1, \dfrac{7}{3}$
- **(D)** $1, \dfrac{3}{7}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $1, \dfrac{7}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the given plane in Cartesian form is $3x + 4y - 12z + 13 = 0$.

The perpendicular distance of a point $(x_1, y_1, z_1)$ from the plane $Ax + By + Cz + D = 0$ is given by $\dfrac{|Ax_1 + By_1 + Cz_1 + D|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2 + C^2}}$.

The distance of the point $(1, 1, \mu)$ from the plane is:

$d_1 = \dfrac{|3(1) + 4(1) - 12(\mu) + 13|}{\sqrt{3^2 + 4^2 + (-12)^2}} = \dfrac{|20 - 12\mu|}{13}$

The distance of the point $(-3, 0, 1)$ from the plane is:

$d_2 = \dfrac{|3(-3) + 4(0) - 12(1) + 13|}{\sqrt{3^2 + 4^2 + (-12)^2}} = \dfrac{|-9 - 12 + 13|}{13} = \dfrac{|-8|}{13} = \dfrac{8}{13}$

Since the points are equidistant from the plane, $d_1 = d_2$.

$\dfrac{|20 - 12\mu|}{13} = \dfrac{8}{13}$

$|20 - 12\mu| = 8$

This gives two cases:

$20 - 12\mu = 8 \Rightarrow 12\mu = 12 \Rightarrow \mu = 1$

$20 - 12\mu = -8 \Rightarrow 12\mu = 28 \Rightarrow \mu = \dfrac{28}{12} = \dfrac{7}{3}$

The values of $\mu$ are $1, \dfrac{7}{3}$.

Answer: $1, \dfrac{7}{3}$

</details>

---

### Q35. MHT CET 2025 (5 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The distance of the point $(5,3,-1)$ from the plane passing through points $(2,1,0),(3,-2,4)$ and $(1,-3,3)$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}$ units
- **(B)** $\frac{4}{\sqrt{3}}$ units
- **(C)** $\sqrt{3}$ units
- **(D)** $\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the given points be $A(2,1,0)$, $B(3,-2,4)$ and $C(1,-3,3)$.

The vectors $\overrightarrow{AB}$ and $\overrightarrow{AC}$ are given by:

$\overrightarrow{AB} = (3-2)\hat{i} + (-2-1)\hat{j} + (4-0)\hat{k} = \hat{i} - 3\hat{j} + 4\hat{k}$

$\overrightarrow{AC} = (1-2)\hat{i} + (-3-1)\hat{j} + (3-0)\hat{k} = -\hat{i} - 4\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$

A normal vector $\vec{n}$ to the plane is the cross product of $\overrightarrow{AB}$ and $\overrightarrow{AC}$:

$\vec{n} = \overrightarrow{AB} \times \overrightarrow{AC} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & -3 & 4 \\ -1 & -4 & 3 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(-9 + 16) - \hat{j}(3 + 4) + \hat{k}(-4 - 3) = 7\hat{i} - 7\hat{j} - 7\hat{k}$

Dividing by $7$, a simpler normal vector is $\hat{i} - \hat{j} - \hat{k}$.

The equation of the plane passing through $A(2,1,0)$ with normal vector $\hat{i} - \hat{j} - \hat{k}$ is:

$1(x - 2) - 1(y - 1) - 1(z - 0) = 0$

$x - y - z - 1 = 0$

The perpendicular distance $d$ of the point $P(5,3,-1)$ from the plane $x - y - z - 1 = 0$ is:

$d = \dfrac{|1(5) - 1(3) - 1(-1) - 1|}{\sqrt{1^2 + (-1)^2 + (-1)^2}}$

$d = \dfrac{|5 - 3 + 1 - 1|}{\sqrt{3}}$

$d = \dfrac{2}{\sqrt{3}}$ units

Answer: $\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}$ units

</details>

---

### Q36. MHT CET 2025 (5 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of plane passing through $(1,0,0)$ and $(0,1,0)$ and making an angle $45^{\circ}$ with the plane $x+y-3=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+y \pm \sqrt{2} z-1=0$
- **(B)** $3 x+y \pm \sqrt{3} z-3=0$
- **(C)** $x+y \pm \sqrt{3} z-1=0$
- **(D)** $2 x+2 y \pm \sqrt{3} z-2=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $x+y \pm \sqrt{2} z-1=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the equation of the plane be $ax+by+cz+d=0$.

Since the plane passes through $(1,0,0)$, we have $a+d=0 \Rightarrow a=-d$.

Since the plane passes through $(0,1,0)$, we have $b+d=0 \Rightarrow b=-d$.

Substituting $a$ and $b$ into the equation of the plane, we get:

$-dx-dy+cz+d=0$

Dividing by $-d$, the equation becomes:

$x+y-\dfrac{c}{d}z-1=0$

Let $k = -\dfrac{c}{d}$. The equation of the plane is $x+y+kz-1=0$.

The normal vector to this plane is $\vec{n_1} = \hat{i} + \hat{j} + k\hat{k}$.

The normal vector to the given plane $x+y-3=0$ is $\vec{n_2} = \hat{i} + \hat{j}$.

The angle between the planes is $45^{\circ}$, so:

$\cos 45^{\circ} = \dfrac{|\vec{n_1} \cdot \vec{n_2}|}{|\vec{n_1}| |\vec{n_2}|}$

$\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}} = \dfrac{|(1)(1) + (1)(1) + (k)(0)|}{\sqrt{1^2+1^2+k^2} \sqrt{1^2+1^2+0^2}}$

$\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}} = \dfrac{2}{\sqrt{2+k^2} \sqrt{2}}$

$\sqrt{2+k^2} = 2$

Squaring both sides, we get:

$2+k^2 = 4$

$k^2 = 2 \Rightarrow k = \pm \sqrt{2}$

Substituting the value of $k$ back into the equation of the plane, we get:

$x+y \pm \sqrt{2}z-1=0$

Answer: $x+y \pm \sqrt{2} z-1=0$

</details>

---

### Q37. MHT CET 2025 (27 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane through the line of intersection of $\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(2 \hat{i}-3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}})=1$ and $\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(\hat{i}-\hat{\mathrm{j}})+4=0$ and perpandicular to $\bar{r} \cdot(2 \hat{i}-\hat{j}+\hat{k})+8=0$ is $\bar{r} \cdot(-5 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+12 \hat{k})=\mu$. Then $\mu=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** 47
- **(B)** 37
- **(C)** -37
- **(D)** 8

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 47**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the plane passing through the line of intersection of the given planes is:

$(\vec{r} \cdot (2\hat{i} - 3\hat{j} + 4\hat{k}) - 1) + \lambda (\vec{r} \cdot (\hat{i} - \hat{j}) + 4) = 0$

$\vec{r} \cdot ((2 + \lambda)\hat{i} + (-3 - \lambda)\hat{j} + 4\hat{k}) - 1 + 4\lambda = 0$

This plane is perpendicular to the plane $\vec{r} \cdot (2\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k}) + 8 = 0$. Therefore, the dot product of their normal vectors is zero:

$(2 + \lambda)(2) + (-3 - \lambda)(-1) + (4)(1) = 0$

$4 + 2\lambda + 3 + \lambda + 4 = 0$

$3\lambda + 11 = 0 \Rightarrow \lambda = -\dfrac{11}{3}$

Substituting $\lambda = -\dfrac{11}{3}$ in the equation of the plane:

$\vec{r} \cdot \left(\left(2 - \dfrac{11}{3}\right)\hat{i} + \left(-3 + \dfrac{11}{3}\right)\hat{j} + 4\hat{k}\right) - 1 + 4\left(-\dfrac{11}{3}\right) = 0$

$\vec{r} \cdot \left(-\dfrac{5}{3}\hat{i} + \dfrac{2}{3}\hat{j} + 4\hat{k}\right) - \dfrac{47}{3} = 0$

Multiplying the entire equation by $3$:

$\vec{r} \cdot (-5\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 12\hat{k}) = 47$

Comparing this with the given equation $\vec{r} \cdot (-5\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 12\hat{k}) = \mu$, we get:

$\mu = 47$

Answer: 47

</details>

---

### Q38. MHT CET 2025 (27 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane passing through the point $(1,2,1)$ and perpendicular to the planes $x+2 \mathrm{y}+2 \mathrm{z}-7=0$ and $3 x+3 \mathrm{y}+2 \mathrm{z}-5=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2 x-4 y+3 z+3=0$
- **(B)** $2 x-4 y+3 z-3=0$
- **(C)** $2 x+4 y-3 z-7=0$
- **(D)** $2 x-4 y-3 z+9=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $2 x-4 y+3 z+3=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The normal vector of the required plane is perpendicular to the normal vectors of the given planes.

The normal vectors of the given planes are $\vec{n_1} = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$ and $\vec{n_2} = 3\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$.

The normal vector of the required plane is given by their cross product:

$\vec{n} = \vec{n_1} \times \vec{n_2} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & 2 & 2 \\ 3 & 3 & 2 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(4 - 6) - \hat{j}(2 - 6) + \hat{k}(3 - 6)$

$\vec{n} = -2\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} - 3\hat{k}$

The direction ratios of the normal to the required plane are proportional to $2, -4, 3$.

The equation of the plane passing through the point $(1, 2, 1)$ with normal direction ratios $2, -4, 3$ is:

$2(x - 1) - 4(y - 2) + 3(z - 1) = 0$

$2x - 2 - 4y + 8 + 3z - 3 = 0$

$2x - 4y + 3z + 3 = 0$

Answer: $2 x-4 y+3 z+3=0$

</details>

---

### Q39. MHT CET 2025 (26 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane passing through the point $(1,1,1)$ and through the line of intersection of $x+2 y-z+1=0$ and $3 x-y-4 z+3=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4 x-3 y-2 z+1=0$
- **(B)** $3 x-2 y+2 z-3=0$
- **(C)** $8 x-5 y-11 z+8=0$
- **(D)** $5 x-4 y+2 z-3=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $8 x-5 y-11 z+8=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the plane passing through the line of intersection of the given planes is

$(x+2y-z+1) + \lambda(3x-y-4z+3) = 0$

Since the plane passes through the point $(1,1,1)$, we substitute $x=1, y=1, z=1$ into the equation:

$(1+2(1)-1+1) + \lambda(3(1)-1-4(1)+3) = 0$

$3 + \lambda(1) = 0$

$\lambda = -3$

Substituting $\lambda = -3$ back into the equation of the plane:

$(x+2y-z+1) - 3(3x-y-4z+3) = 0$

$x + 2y - z + 1 - 9x + 3y + 12z - 9 = 0$

$-8x + 5y + 11z - 8 = 0$

$8x - 5y - 11z + 8 = 0$

Answer: $8 x-5 y-11 z+8=0$

</details>

### Q40. MHT CET 2025 (26 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the plane $\frac{x}{2}+\frac{y}{3}+\frac{z}{6}=1$ cuts the co - ordinate axes at points $A, B, C$ respectively, then area of the triangle ABC is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{14}$ sq. units
- **(B)** $3 \sqrt{14}$ sq. units
- **(C)** $\frac{1}{\sqrt{14}}$ sq. units
- **(D)** $3 \sqrt{13}$ sq. units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $3 \sqrt{14}$ sq. units**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given plane is $\dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{y}{3} + \dfrac{z}{6} = 1$

The coordinates of the points where the plane cuts the coordinate axes are $A(2, 0, 0)$, $B(0, 3, 0)$, and $C(0, 0, 6)$.

The vectors $\overrightarrow{AB}$ and $\overrightarrow{AC}$ are given by:
$\overrightarrow{AB} = -2\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} + 0\hat{k}$
$\overrightarrow{AC} = -2\hat{i} + 0\hat{j} + 6\hat{k}$

The area of $\triangle ABC$ is $\dfrac{1}{2} |\overrightarrow{AB} \times \overrightarrow{AC}|$.

$\overrightarrow{AB} \times \overrightarrow{AC} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ -2 & 3 & 0 \\ -2 & 0 & 6 \end{vmatrix} = 18\hat{i} + 12\hat{j} + 6\hat{k}$

$|\overrightarrow{AB} \times \overrightarrow{AC}| = \sqrt{18^2 + 12^2 + 6^2} = \sqrt{324 + 144 + 36} = \sqrt{504} = 6\sqrt{14}$

Area of $\triangle ABC = \dfrac{1}{2} \times 6\sqrt{14} = 3\sqrt{14}$ sq. units.

Answer: $3 \sqrt{14}$ sq. units

</details>

---

### Q41. MHT CET 2025 (26 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The mirror image of the point $\mathrm{P}(-1,2,-4)$ in the plane $x-\mathrm{y}-2 \mathrm{z}+1=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(3,-4,1)$
- **(B)** $(-3,4,0)$
- **(C)** $(4,1,0)$
- **(D)** $(2,-3,0)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $(-3,4,0)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the mirror image of the point $P(-1, 2, -4)$ in the plane $x - y - 2z + 1 = 0$ be $Q(x_1, y_1, z_1)$.

The formula for the mirror image of a point $(x_0, y_0, z_0)$ in the plane $ax + by + cz + d = 0$ is given by:

$\dfrac{x_1 - x_0}{a} = \dfrac{y_1 - y_0}{b} = \dfrac{z_1 - z_0}{c} = -2 \dfrac{ax_0 + by_0 + cz_0 + d}{a^2 + b^2 + c^2}$

Substituting the given values:

$\dfrac{x_1 - (-1)}{1} = \dfrac{y_1 - 2}{-1} = \dfrac{z_1 - (-4)}{-2} = -2 \left( \dfrac{1(-1) - 1(2) - 2(-4) + 1}{1^2 + (-1)^2 + (-2)^2} \right)$

$\dfrac{x_1 + 1}{1} = \dfrac{y_1 - 2}{-1} = \dfrac{z_1 + 4}{-2} = -2 \left( \dfrac{-1 - 2 + 8 + 1}{1 + 1 + 4} \right)$

$\dfrac{x_1 + 1}{1} = \dfrac{y_1 - 2}{-1} = \dfrac{z_1 + 4}{-2} = -2 \left( \dfrac{6}{6} \right) = -2$

Equating each term to $-2$:

$x_1 + 1 = -2 \Rightarrow x_1 = -3$

$y_1 - 2 = 2 \Rightarrow y_1 = 4$

$z_1 + 4 = 4 \Rightarrow z_1 = 0$

The mirror image is $(-3, 4, 0)$.

Answer: $(-3,4,0)$

</details>

### Q42. MHT CET 2025 (26 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the foot of the perpendicular drawn from the origin to a plane is $P(-1,-1,2)$, then equation of the plane is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+y-2 z+6=0$
- **(B)** $2 x+y+z+1=0$
- **(C)** $x+y+2 z-2=0$
- **(D)** $x-y-z+2=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $x+y-2 z+6=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $O(0,0,0)$ be the origin and $P(-1,-1,2)$ be the foot of the perpendicular to the plane.

The normal vector to the plane is $\overrightarrow{OP}$, which has direction ratios $(-1-0, -1-0, 2-0) = (-1, -1, 2)$.

The plane passes through the point $P(-1,-1,2)$.

The equation of a plane passing through $(x_1, y_1, z_1)$ with normal direction ratios $a, b, c$ is given by $a(x-x_1) + b(y-y_1) + c(z-z_1) = 0$.

Substituting the values, we get:

$-1(x - (-1)) - 1(y - (-1)) + 2(z - 2) = 0$

$-1(x + 1) - 1(y + 1) + 2(z - 2) = 0$

$-x - 1 - y - 1 + 2z - 4 = 0$

$-x - y + 2z - 6 = 0$

Multiplying the entire equation by $-1$, we obtain:

$x + y - 2z + 6 = 0$

Answer: $x+y-2 z+6=0$

</details>

### Q43. MHT CET 2025 (25 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the plane $\frac{x}{2}-\frac{y}{3}-\frac{z}{5}=1$ cuts the co-ordinate axes in point $A, B, C$ respectively, then the area of the triangle $A B C$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{17}{2}$ sq. units
- **(B)** $\frac{19}{2}$ sq. units
- **(C)** $\frac{11}{2}$ sq. units
- **(D)** $\frac{15}{2}$ sq. units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{19}{2}$ sq. units**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the plane can be written in intercept form as $\dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{y}{-3} + \dfrac{z}{-5} = 1$.

The intercepts on the coordinate axes are $a = 2$, $b = -3$, and $c = -5$.

The points where the plane cuts the axes are $A(2, 0, 0)$, $B(0, -3, 0)$, and $C(0, 0, -5)$.

The area of the triangle formed by the intercepts on the coordinate axes is given by $\dfrac{1}{2} \sqrt{(ab)^2 + (bc)^2 + (ca)^2}$.

Substituting the values of $a$, $b$, and $c$:

$ab = 2 \times (-3) = -6$

$bc = (-3) \times (-5) = 15$

$ca = (-5) \times 2 = -10$

Area $= \dfrac{1}{2} \sqrt{(-6)^2 + (15)^2 + (-10)^2}$

Area $= \dfrac{1}{2} \sqrt{36 + 225 + 100}$

Area $= \dfrac{1}{2} \sqrt{361}$

Area $= \dfrac{19}{2}$ sq. units.

Answer: $\frac{19}{2}$ sq. units

</details>

### Q44. MHT CET 2025 (25 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In 3-dimensional space, the equation $x^2-8 x+12=0$ represents ....

**Options:**

- **(A)** two straight lines
- **(B)** a pair of straight lines passing through the origin
- **(C)** 2 planes parallel to YZ-plane
- **(D)** 2 planes parallel to XZ-plane

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 2 planes parallel to YZ-plane**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given equation is $x^2 - 8x + 12 = 0$.

Factorizing the quadratic equation, we get:

$(x - 6)(x - 2) = 0$

$x = 6$ or $x = 2$

In 3-dimensional space, an equation of the form $x = k$ (where $k$ is a constant) represents a plane parallel to the YZ-plane.

Thus, $x = 6$ and $x = 2$ represent two planes parallel to the YZ-plane.

Answer: 2 planes parallel to YZ-plane

</details>

---

### Q45. MHT CET 2025 (25 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The length of the perpendicular from the point $\left(1, \frac{3}{2}, 2\right)$ to the plane $2 x-2 y+4 z+17=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{6}$ units
- **(B)** $3 \sqrt{3}$ units
- **(C)** $4 \sqrt{3}$ units
- **(D)** $2 \sqrt{6}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $2 \sqrt{6}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

The perpendicular distance from a point $(x_1, y_1, z_1)$ to the plane $ax + by + cz + d = 0$ is given by $p = \dfrac{|ax_1 + by_1 + cz_1 + d|}{\sqrt{a^2 + b^2 + c^2}}$.

Substituting the given values $(x_1, y_1, z_1) = \left(1, \dfrac{3}{2}, 2\right)$ and the plane equation $2x - 2y + 4z + 17 = 0$:

$p = \dfrac{\left|2(1) - 2\left(\dfrac{3}{2}\right) + 4(2) + 17\right|}{\sqrt{2^2 + (-2)^2 + 4^2}}$

$p = \dfrac{|2 - 3 + 8 + 17|}{\sqrt{4 + 4 + 16}}$

$p = \dfrac{24}{\sqrt{24}}$

$p = \sqrt{24} = 2\sqrt{6}$ units.

Answer: $2 \sqrt{6}$ units

</details>

---

### Q46. MHT CET 2025 (23 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let the plane passing through point $(2,1,-1)$ containing line joining the points $(1,3,2)$ and $(1,2,1)$ makes intercepts p,q,r on co-ordinate axes, then $\mathrm{p}+\mathrm{q}+\mathrm{r}=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0$
- **(B)** $3$
- **(C)** $2$
- **(D)** $-2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the three points be $A(2, 1, -1)$, $B(1, 3, 2)$, and $C(1, 2, 1)$.

The plane passes through these three points. We can find two vectors lying in the plane:
$\vec{AB} = (1 - 2)\hat{i} + (3 - 1)\hat{j} + (2 - (-1))\hat{k} = -\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$
$\vec{AC} = (1 - 2)\hat{i} + (2 - 1)\hat{j} + (1 - (-1))\hat{k} = -\hat{i} + \hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ to the plane is given by the cross product $\vec{AB} \times \vec{AC}$:
$\vec{n} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ -1 & 2 & 3 \\ -1 & 1 & 2 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(4 - 3) - \hat{j}(-2 - (-3)) + \hat{k}(-1 - (-2))$
$\vec{n} = \hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k}$

The equation of the plane passing through $C(1, 2, 1)$ with normal vector $(1, -1, 1)$ is:
$1(x - 1) - 1(y - 2) + 1(z - 1) = 0$
$x - 1 - y + 2 + z - 1 = 0$
$x - y + z = 0$

To find the intercepts on the coordinate axes, we substitute $y=0, z=0$ for the x-intercept, $x=0, z=0$ for the y-intercept, and $x=0, y=0$ for the z-intercept.
For the x-intercept $p$: $p - 0 + 0 = 0 \Rightarrow p = 0$
For the y-intercept $q$: $0 - q + 0 = 0 \Rightarrow q = 0$
For the z-intercept $r$: $0 - 0 + r = 0 \Rightarrow r = 0$

Therefore, the sum of the intercepts is:
$\mathrm{p} + \mathrm{q} + \mathrm{r} = 0 + 0 + 0 = 0$

Answer: $0$

</details>

---

### Q47. MHT CET 2025 (23 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the foot of the perpendicular drawn from the origin to a plane is $\mathrm{P}(2,-1,4)$, then the equation of the plane is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2 x+y+4 z-19=0$
- **(B)** $x+y+z-5=0$
- **(C)** $2 x-2 y-3 z+6=0$
- **(D)** $2 x-y+4 z-21=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $2 x-y+4 z-21=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the origin be $O(0, 0, 0)$ and the foot of the perpendicular be $P(2, -1, 4)$.

The normal vector to the plane is $\overrightarrow{OP} = 2\hat{i} - \hat{j} + 4\hat{k}$.

The direction ratios of the normal are $2, -1, 4$.

The plane passes through the point $P(2, -1, 4)$.

The equation of the plane is given by $a(x - x_1) + b(y - y_1) + c(z - z_1) = 0$.

Substituting the values, we get:

$2(x - 2) - 1(y + 1) + 4(z - 4) = 0$

$2x - 4 - y - 1 + 4z - 16 = 0$

$2x - y + 4z - 21 = 0$

Answer: $2 x-y+4 z-21=0$

</details>

### Q48. MHT CET 2025 (22 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The distance of the plane $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(\hat{i}-\hat{\mathrm{j}})+\lambda(\hat{i}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})+\mu(\hat{i}-2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$ from the origin is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{7}{\sqrt{38}}$ units
- **(B)** $\frac{1}{\sqrt{38}}$ units
- **(C)** $\frac{5}{\sqrt{38}}$ units
- **(D)** $\frac{2}{\sqrt{38}}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{7}{\sqrt{38}}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the plane is given in the form $\vec{r} = \vec{a} + \lambda\vec{b} + \mu\vec{c}$, where $\vec{a} = \hat{i} - \hat{j}$, $\vec{b} = \hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}$, and $\vec{c} = \hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$.

The normal vector to the plane is $\vec{n} = \vec{b} \times \vec{c}$.

$\vec{n} = (\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}) \times (\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k})$

$\vec{n} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & 1 & 1 \\ 1 & -2 & 3 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(3 - (-2)) - \hat{j}(3 - 1) + \hat{k}(-2 - 1) = 5\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} - 3\hat{k}$

The equation of the plane is $\vec{r} \cdot \vec{n} = \vec{a} \cdot \vec{n}$.

$\vec{a} \cdot \vec{n} = (\hat{i} - \hat{j}) \cdot (5\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} - 3\hat{k}) = 1(5) + (-1)(-2) + 0(-3) = 5 + 2 = 7$

The equation of the plane in Cartesian form is $5x - 2y - 3z - 7 = 0$.

The perpendicular distance of the plane from the origin $(0, 0, 0)$ is given by:

$d = \dfrac{|-7|}{\sqrt{5^2 + (-2)^2 + (-3)^2}} = \dfrac{7}{\sqrt{25 + 4 + 9}} = \dfrac{7}{\sqrt{38}}$ units.

Answer: $\frac{7}{\sqrt{38}}$ units

</details>

---

### Q49. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The distance of the point $P(3,8,2)$ from the line $\frac{x-1}{2}=\frac{y-3}{4}=\frac{z-2}{3}$ measured parallel to the plane $3 x+2 y-2 z+15=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 7 units
- **(B)** 6 units
- **(C)** 8 units
- **(D)** 10 units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 7 units**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the point on the given line be $Q$. The coordinates of $Q$ can be written as $(2\lambda+1, 4\lambda+3, 3\lambda+2)$.

The direction ratios of the line segment $PQ$ are $(2\lambda+1-3, 4\lambda+3-8, 3\lambda+2-2) = (2\lambda-2, 4\lambda-5, 3\lambda)$.

Since the distance is measured parallel to the plane $3x+2y-2z+15=0$, the line $PQ$ is parallel to the plane. Therefore, the normal to the plane is perpendicular to $PQ$.

The direction ratios of the normal to the plane are $(3, 2, -2)$.

Using the condition of perpendicularity:

$3(2\lambda-2) + 2(4\lambda-5) - 2(3\lambda) = 0$

$6\lambda - 6 + 8\lambda - 10 - 6\lambda = 0$

$8\lambda - 16 = 0 \Rightarrow \lambda = 2$

Substituting $\lambda = 2$, the coordinates of $Q$ are $(2(2)+1, 4(2)+3, 3(2)+2) = (5, 11, 8)$.

The distance $PQ$ is given by the distance formula:

$PQ = \sqrt{(5-3)^2 + (11-8)^2 + (8-2)^2}$

$PQ = \sqrt{2^2 + 3^2 + 6^2} = \sqrt{4 + 9 + 36} = \sqrt{49} = 7$ units.

Answer: 7 units

</details>

---

### Q50. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The distance between the line
$\bar{r}=3 \hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+\hat{k}+\lambda(\hat{i}-2\hat{j})$ and the plane $\bar{r} \cdot(2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k})=4$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}$ units
- **(B)** $\frac{3}{\sqrt{6}}$ units
- **(C)** $\frac{2}{\sqrt{6}}$ units
- **(D)** $\frac{5}{\sqrt{6}}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the given line is $\bar{r} = 3\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + \hat{k} + \lambda(\hat{i} - 2\hat{j})$.
The direction vector of the line is $\vec{b} = \hat{i} - 2\hat{j}$.

The equation of the given plane is $\bar{r} \cdot (2\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}) = 4$.
The normal vector to the plane is $\vec{n} = 2\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}$.

Taking the dot product of $\vec{b}$ and $\vec{n}$:
$\vec{b} \cdot \vec{n} = (1)(2) + (-2)(1) + (0)(1) = 2 - 2 = 0$
Since $\vec{b} \cdot \vec{n} = 0$, the line is parallel to the plane.

The distance between the line and the plane is the perpendicular distance from any point on the line to the plane. A point on the line is given by its position vector $\vec{a} = 3\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + \hat{k}$, which corresponds to the coordinates $(3, -2, 1)$.

The Cartesian equation of the plane is $2x + y + z - 4 = 0$.

The perpendicular distance $d$ from the point $(3, -2, 1)$ to the plane is:
$d = \dfrac{|2(3) + 1(-2) + 1(1) - 4|}{\sqrt{2^2 + 1^2 + 1^2}}$

$d = \dfrac{|6 - 2 + 1 - 4|}{\sqrt{4 + 1 + 1}}$

$d = \dfrac{|1|}{\sqrt{6}} = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{6}} \text{ units}$

Answer: $\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}$ units

</details>

---

### Q51. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the distance of the point $\mathrm{P}(1,-2,1)$ from the plane $x+2 \mathrm{y}-2 \mathrm{z}=\alpha$, where $\alpha>0$ is 5 units, then the foot of the perpendicular from P to the plane is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left(2, \frac{2}{3}, \frac{-10}{3}\right)$
- **(B)** $\left(\frac{8}{3}, \frac{7}{3}, \frac{-4}{3}\right)$
- **(C)** $\left(\frac{4}{3}, \frac{2}{3}, \frac{-8}{3}\right)$
- **(D)** $\left(\frac{8}{3}, \frac{4}{3}, \frac{-7}{3}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\left(\frac{8}{3}, \frac{4}{3}, \frac{-7}{3}\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The perpendicular distance of the point $P(1, -2, 1)$ from the plane $x + 2y - 2z - \alpha = 0$ is given by:

$d = \dfrac{|1(1) + 2(-2) - 2(1) - \alpha|}{\sqrt{1^2 + 2^2 + (-2)^2}} = 5$

$\dfrac{|1 - 4 - 2 - \alpha|}{\sqrt{9}} = 5$

$\dfrac{|-5 - \alpha|}{3} = 5$

$|-5 - \alpha| = 15$

$-5 - \alpha = 15 \quad \text{or} \quad -5 - \alpha = -15$

$\alpha = -20 \quad \text{or} \quad \alpha = 10$

Since it is given that $\alpha > 0$, we have $\alpha = 10$.

The equation of the plane is $x + 2y - 2z - 10 = 0$.

Let the foot of the perpendicular from $P(1, -2, 1)$ to the plane be $(x_2, y_2, z_2)$. The coordinates of the foot of the perpendicular are given by the formula:

$\dfrac{x_2 - x_1}{a} = \dfrac{y_2 - y_1}{b} = \dfrac{z_2 - z_1}{c} = -\dfrac{ax_1 + by_1 + cz_1 - d}{a^2 + b^2 + c^2}$

Substituting the values, we get:

$\dfrac{x_2 - 1}{1} = \dfrac{y_2 - (-2)}{2} = \dfrac{z_2 - 1}{-2} = -\dfrac{1(1) + 2(-2) - 2(1) - 10}{1^2 + 2^2 + (-2)^2}$

$\dfrac{x_2 - 1}{1} = \dfrac{y_2 + 2}{2} = \dfrac{z_2 - 1}{-2} = -\dfrac{-15}{9} = \dfrac{5}{3}$

Equating each term to $\dfrac{5}{3}$:

$x_2 - 1 = \dfrac{5}{3} \Rightarrow x_2 = \dfrac{8}{3}$

$y_2 + 2 = \dfrac{10}{3} \Rightarrow y_2 = \dfrac{4}{3}$

$z_2 - 1 = -\dfrac{10}{3} \Rightarrow z_2 = -\dfrac{7}{3}$

Therefore, the foot of the perpendicular is $\left(\dfrac{8}{3}, \dfrac{4}{3}, \dfrac{-7}{3}\right)$.

Answer: $\left(\frac{8}{3}, \frac{4}{3}, \frac{-7}{3}\right)$

</details>

---

### Q52. MHT CET 2025 (20 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane passing through the line of intersection of the planes $2 x-y+z-3=0$ and $4 x-3 y+5 z+9=0$ and parallel to the line $\frac{x+1}{2}=\frac{y+3}{4}=\frac{z-3}{5}$ is $\alpha x+\beta y+\gamma z+d=0$ (where $\alpha, \beta, \gamma, d$ are coprime integers and $\alpha > 0$). Then $\alpha+\beta+\gamma+\mathrm{d}=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $48$
- **(B)** $-48$
- **(C)** $84$
- **(D)** $45$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $-48$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of any plane passing through the line of intersection of the given planes is:

$(2x - y + z - 3) + \lambda (4x - 3y + 5z + 9) = 0$

$(2 + 4\lambda)x + (-1 - 3\lambda)y + (1 + 5\lambda)z + (-3 + 9\lambda) = 0$

Since this plane is parallel to the line $\dfrac{x+1}{2} = \dfrac{y+3}{4} = \dfrac{z-3}{5}$, the normal to the plane is perpendicular to the line. Therefore, the dot product of their direction ratios is zero:

$2(2 + 4\lambda) + 4(-1 - 3\lambda) + 5(1 + 5\lambda) = 0$

$4 + 8\lambda - 4 - 12\lambda + 5 + 25\lambda = 0$

$21\lambda + 5 = 0 \Rightarrow \lambda = -\dfrac{5}{21}$

Substituting $\lambda = -\dfrac{5}{21}$ into the equation of the plane, we get:

$(2x - y + z - 3) - \dfrac{5}{21}(4x - 3y + 5z + 9) = 0$

$21(2x - y + z - 3) - 5(4x - 3y + 5z + 9) = 0$

$42x - 21y + 21z - 63 - 20x + 15y - 25z - 45 = 0$

$22x - 6y - 4z - 108 = 0$

Dividing by $2$ to make the coefficients coprime integers:

$11x - 3y - 2z - 54 = 0$

Comparing this with $\alpha x + \beta y + \gamma z + d = 0$, we have:

$\alpha = 11$, $\beta = -3$, $\gamma = -2$, $d = -54$

These are coprime integers and $\alpha > 0$.

$\alpha + \beta + \gamma + d = 11 - 3 - 2 - 54 = -48$

Answer: $-48$

</details>

---

### Q53. MHT CET 2025 (20 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the angle between the planes $x-2 y+3 z-5=0$ and $x+\alpha y+2 z+7=0$ is $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{14}\right)$ then the difference between the values of $\alpha$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{12}{11}$
- **(B)** $\frac{62}{55}$
- **(C)** $\frac{31}{11}$
- **(D)** $\frac{8}{5}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{62}{55}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The normal vectors to the given planes are $\vec{n_1} = \hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$ and $\vec{n_2} = \hat{i} + \alpha\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$.

The angle $\theta$ between the planes is given by:

$\cos \theta = \dfrac{|\vec{n_1} \cdot \vec{n_2}|}{|\vec{n_1}| |\vec{n_2}|}$

Given $\theta = \cos^{-1}\left(\dfrac{1}{14}\right)$, we have $\cos \theta = \dfrac{1}{14}$.

Substituting the values:

$\dfrac{1}{14} = \dfrac{|(1)(1) + (-2)(\alpha) + (3)(2)|}{\sqrt{1^2 + (-2)^2 + 3^2} \sqrt{1^2 + \alpha^2 + 2^2}}$

$\dfrac{1}{14} = \dfrac{|7 - 2\alpha|}{\sqrt{14} \sqrt{\alpha^2 + 5}}$

Squaring both sides:

$\dfrac{1}{196} = \dfrac{(7 - 2\alpha)^2}{14(\alpha^2 + 5)}$

$\dfrac{1}{14} = \dfrac{49 - 28\alpha + 4\alpha^2}{\alpha^2 + 5}$

$\alpha^2 + 5 = 14(49 - 28\alpha + 4\alpha^2)$

$\alpha^2 + 5 = 686 - 392\alpha + 56\alpha^2$

$55\alpha^2 - 392\alpha + 681 = 0$

Let the roots of this quadratic equation be $\alpha_1$ and $\alpha_2$. The difference between the values of $\alpha$ is given by $\dfrac{\sqrt{D}}{a}$ where $D = b^2 - 4ac$.

$D = (-392)^2 - 4(55)(681)$

$D = 153664 - 149820 = 3844$

$\sqrt{D} = \sqrt{3844} = 62$

Therefore, the difference between the values of $\alpha$ is:

$|\alpha_1 - \alpha_2| = \dfrac{62}{55}$

Answer: $\frac{62}{55}$

</details>
