### Q54. MHT CET 2025 (19 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane passing through the line of intersection of the planes $x+y+z=1$ and $3 x+4 y+5 z=2$ and perpendicular to the XY- plane is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2 x+y-3=0$
- **(B)** $x-2 y+3=0$
- **(C)** $x-3 y-2=0$
- **(D)** $2 x-y+6=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $2 x+y-3=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the plane passing through the line of intersection of the given planes is

$(x+y+z-1) + \lambda(3x+4y+5z-2) = 0$

$(1+3\lambda)x + (1+4\lambda)y + (1+5\lambda)z - (1+2\lambda) = 0$

Since this plane is perpendicular to the XY-plane, its normal is perpendicular to the normal of the XY-plane. The normal to the XY-plane is along the Z-axis, which has direction ratios $(0, 0, 1)$.

Therefore, the dot product of their normals is zero:

$(1+3\lambda)(0) + (1+4\lambda)(0) + (1+5\lambda)(1) = 0$

$1+5\lambda = 0 \Rightarrow \lambda = -\dfrac{1}{5}$

Substituting $\lambda = -\dfrac{1}{5}$ in the equation of the plane, we get

$\left(1 - \dfrac{3}{5}\right)x + \left(1 - \dfrac{4}{5}\right)y + \left(1 - \dfrac{5}{5}\right)z - \left(1 - \dfrac{2}{5}\right) = 0$

$\dfrac{2}{5}x + \dfrac{1}{5}y - \dfrac{3}{5} = 0$

$2x + y - 3 = 0$

Answer: $2 x+y-3=0$

</details>

---

### Q55. MHT CET 2025 (19 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A plane passes through $(2,1,2)$ and $(1,2,1)$ and parallel to the line $2 x=3 y$ and $\mathrm{z}=1$, then the plane also passes through the point.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(-6,2,0)$
- **(B)** $(6,-2,0)$
- **(C)** $(-2,0,1)$
- **(D)** $(2,0,1)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $(-2,0,1)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given line is $2x = 3y$ and $z = 1$, which can be rewritten as $\dfrac{x}{3} = \dfrac{y}{2} = \dfrac{z-1}{0}$.

The direction ratios of the line are $(3, 2, 0)$.

The plane passes through the points $A(2, 1, 2)$ and $B(1, 2, 1)$.

The direction ratios of the line segment $AB$ are $(1 - 2, 2 - 1, 1 - 2) = (-1, 1, -1)$.

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ to the plane is perpendicular to both the given line and the line segment $AB$.

$\vec{n} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 3 & 2 & 0 \\ -1 & 1 & -1 \end{vmatrix} = \hat{i}(-2 - 0) - \hat{j}(-3 - 0) + \hat{k}(3 - (-2)) = -2\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} + 5\hat{k}$

The direction ratios of the normal are $(-2, 3, 5)$.

The equation of the plane passing through $A(2, 1, 2)$ is:

$-2(x - 2) + 3(y - 1) + 5(z - 2) = 0$

$-2x + 4 + 3y - 3 + 5z - 10 = 0$

$2x - 3y - 5z + 9 = 0$

Substituting the point $(-2, 0, 1)$ into the equation of the plane:

$2(-2) - 3(0) - 5(1) + 9 = -4 - 0 - 5 + 9 = 0$

The point $(-2, 0, 1)$ satisfies the equation of the plane.

Answer: $(-2,0,1)$

</details>

---

### Q56. MHT CET 2025 (19 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A plane passes through $(1,-2,1)$ and is perpendicular to the planes $2 x-2 \mathrm{y}+\mathrm{z}=0$ and $x-\mathrm{y}+2 \mathrm{z}=4$. The distance of the point $(1,2,2)$ from this plane is _______ units.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1$
- **(B)** $\sqrt{2}$
- **(C)** $2 \sqrt{2}$
- **(D)** $\sqrt{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $2 \sqrt{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the normal vector of the required plane be $\vec{n}$. Since the plane is perpendicular to the given planes, $\vec{n}$ is the cross product of their normal vectors $\vec{n}_1$ and $\vec{n}_2$.

$\vec{n}_1 = 2\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + \hat{k}$

$\vec{n}_2 = \hat{i} - \hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$

$\vec{n} = \vec{n}_1 \times \vec{n}_2 = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & -2 & 1 \\ 1 & -1 & 2 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(-4 + 1) - \hat{j}(4 - 1) + \hat{k}(-2 + 2) = -3\hat{i} - 3\hat{j}$

The direction ratios of the normal to the plane are proportional to $(1, 1, 0)$.

The equation of the plane passing through $(1, -2, 1)$ with normal direction ratios $(1, 1, 0)$ is:

$1(x - 1) + 1(y - (-2)) + 0(z - 1) = 0$

$x - 1 + y + 2 = 0$

$x + y + 1 = 0$

The perpendicular distance of the point $(1, 2, 2)$ from the plane $x + y + 1 = 0$ is:

$d = \dfrac{|1(1) + 1(2) + 1|}{\sqrt{1^2 + 1^2 + 0^2}}$

$d = \dfrac{|1 + 2 + 1|}{\sqrt{2}} = \dfrac{4}{\sqrt{2}} = 2\sqrt{2}$ units.

Answer: $2 \sqrt{2}$

</details>

### Q57. MHT CET 2025 (19 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The Cartesian equation of plane through $A(7,8,6)$ and parallel to the XY plane is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $z=7$
- **(B)** $z=8$
- **(C)** $z=6$
- **(D)** $z=4$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $z=6$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the XY plane is $z = 0$.

Any plane parallel to the XY plane has the equation of the form $z = c$, where $c$ is a constant.

Since the required plane passes through the point $A(7, 8, 6)$, the z-coordinate of the point must satisfy the equation of the plane.

Substituting $z = 6$, we get $c = 6$.

Thus, the Cartesian equation of the required plane is $z = 6$.

Answer: $z=6$

</details>

### Q58. MHT CET 2024 (16 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A plane makes positive intercepts of unit length on each of $X$ and $Y$ axis. If it passes through the point $(-1,1,2)$ and makes angle $\theta$ with the X -axis, then $\theta$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)$
- **(B)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{3}\right)$
- **(C)** $\sin ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{3}\right)$
- **(D)** $\sin ^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\sin ^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the equation of the plane in intercept form be $\dfrac{x}{a} + \dfrac{y}{b} + \dfrac{z}{c} = 1$.

Given that the plane makes positive intercepts of unit length on the X and Y axes, we have $a = 1$ and $b = 1$.

The equation of the plane becomes:
$x + y + \dfrac{z}{c} = 1$

Since the plane passes through the point $(-1, 1, 2)$, we substitute these coordinates into the equation:
$-1 + 1 + \dfrac{2}{c} = 1$

$\dfrac{2}{c} = 1 \Rightarrow c = 2$

Substituting $c = 2$ back into the equation of the plane, we get:
$x + y + \dfrac{z}{2} = 1$

$2x + 2y + z = 2$

The normal vector to the plane is $\vec{n} = 2\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + \hat{k}$.
The direction vector of the X-axis is $\vec{u} = \hat{i}$.

The angle $\theta$ between a plane and a line is given by:
$\sin \theta = \dfrac{|\vec{n} \cdot \vec{u}|}{|\vec{n}| |\vec{u}|}$

Calculating the dot product and magnitudes:
$\vec{n} \cdot \vec{u} = (2)(1) + (2)(0) + (1)(0) = 2$
$|\vec{n}| = \sqrt{2^2 + 2^2 + 1^2} = \sqrt{4 + 4 + 1} = 3$
$|\vec{u}| = \sqrt{1^2 + 0^2 + 0^2} = 1$

Therefore,
$\sin \theta = \dfrac{2}{3 \times 1} = \dfrac{2}{3}$

$\theta = \sin^{-1}\left(\dfrac{2}{3}\right)$

Answer: $\sin ^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)$

</details>

---

### Q59. MHT CET 2024 (16 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The length of the projection of the line segment joining the points $(5,-1,4)$ and $(4,-1,3)$ on the plane $x+y+z=7$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}$ units
- **(B)** $\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}$ units
- **(C)** $\frac{2}{3}$ units
- **(D)** $\frac{\sqrt{2}}{3}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the given points be $A(5, -1, 4)$ and $B(4, -1, 3)$.

The vector joining $A$ and $B$ is $\overrightarrow{AB} = (4 - 5)\hat{i} + (-1 - (-1))\hat{j} + (3 - 4)\hat{k} = -\hat{i} - \hat{k}$.

The length of the line segment $AB$ is $|\overrightarrow{AB}| = \sqrt{(-1)^2 + 0^2 + (-1)^2} = \sqrt{2}$.

The normal vector to the given plane $x + y + z = 7$ is $\vec{n} = \hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}$.

The magnitude of the normal vector is $|\vec{n}| = \sqrt{1^2 + 1^2 + 1^2} = \sqrt{3}$.

The length of the projection of $\overrightarrow{AB}$ along the normal $\vec{n}$ is given by $d = \dfrac{|\overrightarrow{AB} \cdot \vec{n}|}{|\vec{n}|}$.

$d = \dfrac{|(-1)(1) + (0)(1) + (-1)(1)|}{\sqrt{3}} = \dfrac{2}{\sqrt{3}}$.

The length of the projection of the line segment on the plane is $l = \sqrt{|\overrightarrow{AB}|^2 - d^2}$.

$l = \sqrt{(\sqrt{2})^2 - \left(\dfrac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\right)^2} = \sqrt{2 - \dfrac{4}{3}} = \sqrt(\dfrac{2}{3})$.

Answer: $\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}$ units

</details>

---

### Q60. MHT CET 2024 (16 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the distance between the plane $\mathrm{A} x-2 y+\mathrm{z}=\mathrm{d}$ and the plane containing the lines $\frac{x-1}{2}=\frac{y-2}{3}=\frac{z-3}{4}$ and $\frac{x-2}{3}=\frac{y-3}{4}=\frac{z-4}{5}$ is $\sqrt{6}$ units, then $|d|$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1
- **(B)** 2
- **(C)** $\sqrt{6}$
- **(D)** 6

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 6**

**Detailed Solution:**

The direction ratios of the given lines are $\vec{b}_1 = (2, 3, 4)$ and $\vec{b}_2 = (3, 4, 5)$.

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ to the plane containing these two lines is given by the cross product of their direction vectors:

$\vec{n} = \vec{b}_1 \times \vec{b}_2 = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & 3 & 4 \\ 3 & 4 & 5 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(15 - 16) - \hat{j}(10 - 12) + \hat{k}(8 - 9) = -\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - \hat{k}$

The direction ratios of the normal are $(-1, 2, -1)$, which can be written as $(1, -2, 1)$.

The plane contains the point $(1, 2, 3)$ from the first line. The equation of the plane is:

$1(x - 1) - 2(y - 2) + 1(z - 3) = 0$

$x - 1 - 2y + 4 + z - 3 = 0$

$x - 2y + z = 0$

The given plane is $Ax - 2y + z = d$. Since the distance between the two planes is a non-zero constant ($\sqrt{6}$ units), the planes must be parallel. Comparing the normal vectors, we get $A = 1$.

The distance between the parallel planes $x - 2y + z = 0$ and $x - 2y + z - d = 0$ is given by:

$\text{Distance} = \dfrac{|d - 0|}{\sqrt{1^2 + (-2)^2 + 1^2}} = \dfrac{|d|}{\sqrt{6}}$

Given that the distance is $\sqrt{6}$ units:

$\dfrac{|d|}{\sqrt{6}} = \sqrt{6}$

$|d| = 6$

Answer: 6

</details>

---

### Q61. MHT CET 2024 (16 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane, passing through the intersection of the planes $x+y+z=1$ and $2 x+3 y-z+4=0$ and parallel to $Y$-axis is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+4 z-1=0$
- **(B)** $x+4 z-7=0$
- **(C)** $x-4 z+7=0$
- **(D)** $x-4 z+1=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $x+4 z-7=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the plane passing through the intersection of the planes $x+y+z-1=0$ and $2x+3y-z+4=0$ is given by:

$(x+y+z-1) + \lambda(2x+3y-z+4) = 0$

$(1+2\lambda)x + (1+3\lambda)y + (1-\lambda)z + (4\lambda-1) = 0$

Since the required plane is parallel to the $Y$-axis, its normal is perpendicular to the $Y$-axis. The direction ratios of the $Y$-axis are $(0, 1, 0)$.

Taking the dot product of the normal vector and the direction vector of the $Y$-axis as zero:

$0(1+2\lambda) + 1(1+3\lambda) + 0(1-\lambda) = 0$

$1+3\lambda = 0 \Rightarrow \lambda = -\dfrac{1}{3}$

Substituting $\lambda = -\dfrac{1}{3}$ in the equation of the plane, we get:

$\left(1 - \dfrac{2}{3}\right)x + \left(1 - 1\right)y + \left(1 + \dfrac{1}{3}\right)z + \left(-\dfrac{4}{3} - 1\right) = 0$

$\dfrac{1}{3}x + \dfrac{4}{3}z - \dfrac{7}{3} = 0$

$x + 4z - 7 = 0$

Answer: $x+4 z-7=0$

</details>

---

### Q62. MHT CET 2024 (11 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The distance of the point $(1,-5,9)$ from the plane $x-y+z=5$ measured along the line $x=y=z$ is ______ units.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3 \sqrt{10}$
- **(B)** $10 \sqrt{3}$
- **(C)** $\frac{10}{\sqrt{3}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{20}{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $10 \sqrt{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the line passing through the point $P(1, -5, 9)$ and parallel to the line $x=y=z$ (which has direction ratios $1, 1, 1$) is:

$\dfrac{x-1}{1} = \dfrac{y+5}{1} = \dfrac{z-9}{1} = r$

Any point on this line is of the form $Q(r+1, r-5, r+9)$.

Since the point $Q$ lies on the plane $x-y+z=5$, we substitute its coordinates into the equation of the plane:

$(r+1) - (r-5) + (r+9) = 5$

$r + 1 - r + 5 + r + 9 = 5$

$r + 15 = 5$

$r = -10$

Substituting $r = -10$, the coordinates of the point of intersection $Q$ are $(-9, -15, -1)$.

The required distance is the distance between $P(1, -5, 9)$ and $Q(-9, -15, -1)$:

$PQ = \sqrt{(-9-1)^2 + (-15 - (-5))^2 + (-1-9)^2}$

$PQ = \sqrt{(-10)^2 + (-10)^2 + (-10)^2}$

$PQ = \sqrt{100 + 100 + 100} = \sqrt{300} = 10\sqrt{3}$ units.

Answer: $10 \sqrt{3}$

</details>

### Q63. MHT CET 2024 (11 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A variable plane passes through the fixed point $(3,2,1)$ and meets $X, Y$ and $Z$ axes at points $A$, B and C respectively. A plane is drawn parallel to YZ - plane through A , a second plane is drawn parallel to ZX -plan through B, a third plane is drawn parallel to XY - plane through C . Then locus of the point of intersection of these three planes, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{1}{x}+\frac{1}{y}+\frac{1}{z}=\frac{11}{6}$
- **(B)** $\frac{x}{3}+\frac{y}{2}+\frac{z}{1}=1$
- **(C)** $\frac{3}{x}+\frac{2}{y}+\frac{1}{z}=1$
- **(D)** $x+y+z=6$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{3}{x}+\frac{2}{y}+\frac{1}{z}=1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the equation of the variable plane be $\dfrac{x}{a} + \dfrac{y}{b} + \dfrac{z}{c} = 1$.

Since the plane passes through the fixed point $(3, 2, 1)$, we have:

$\dfrac{3}{a} + \dfrac{2}{b} + \dfrac{1}{c} = 1$

The plane meets the coordinate axes at the points $A(a, 0, 0)$, $B(0, b, 0)$, and $C(0, 0, c)$.

The equation of the plane passing through $A$ and parallel to the YZ-plane is $x = a$.

The equation of the plane passing through $B$ and parallel to the ZX-plane is $y = b$.

The equation of the plane passing through $C$ and parallel to the XY-plane is $z = c$.

Let the point of intersection of these three planes be $(x, y, z)$. Then we have $x = a$, $y = b$, and $z = c$.

Substituting these values into the condition $\dfrac{3}{a} + \dfrac{2}{b} + \dfrac{1}{c} = 1$, we get the locus of the point of intersection as:

$\dfrac{3}{x} + \dfrac{2}{y} + \dfrac{1}{z} = 1$

Answer: $\frac{3}{x}+\frac{2}{y}+\frac{1}{z}=1$

</details>

### Q64. MHT CET 2024 (11 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The perpendicular distance of the origin from the plane $2 x+y-2 z-18=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 18 units
- **(B)** 9 units
- **(C)** 6 units
- **(D)** 4 units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 6 units**

**Detailed Solution:**

The perpendicular distance of a point $(x_1, y_1, z_1)$ from the plane $ax + by + cz + d = 0$ is given by $p = \dfrac{|ax_1 + by_1 + cz_1 + d|}{\sqrt{a^2 + b^2 + c^2}}$.

Here, the point is the origin $(0, 0, 0)$ and the equation of the plane is $2x + y - 2z - 18 = 0$.

Substituting the values, we get:

$p = \dfrac{|2(0) + 1(0) - 2(0) - 18|}{\sqrt{2^2 + 1^2 + (-2)^2}}$

$p = \dfrac{|-18|}{\sqrt{4 + 1 + 4}}$

$p = \dfrac{18}{\sqrt{9}}$

$p = \dfrac{18}{3} = 6$ units.

Answer: 6 units

</details>

### Q65. MHT CET 2024 (10 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane through the point $(2,-1,-3)$ and parallel to the lines $\dfrac{x-1}{3}=\dfrac{y+2}{2}=\dfrac{z}{-4}$ and $\dfrac{x}{2}=\dfrac{y-1}{-3}=\dfrac{z-2}{2}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $8 x+y-13 z+27=0$
- **(B)** $2 x+y+z=0$
- **(C)** $3 x-y-z-10=0$
- **(D)** $8 x+14 y+13 z+37=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $8 x+14 y+13 z+37=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The plane is parallel to the given lines, so its normal vector is perpendicular to the direction vectors of both lines. The direction ratios of the lines are $3, 2, -4$ and $2, -3, 2$.

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ to the plane is given by the cross product of these direction vectors:

$\vec{n} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 3 & 2 & -4 \\ 2 & -3 & 2 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(4 - 12) - \hat{j}(6 - (-8)) + \hat{k}(-9 - 4)$

$\vec{n} = -8\hat{i} - 14\hat{j} - 13\hat{k}$

The direction ratios of the normal are proportional to $8, 14, 13$.

The equation of the plane passing through the point $(2, -1, -3)$ with normal direction ratios $8, 14, 13$ is:

$8(x - 2) + 14(y + 1) + 13(z + 3) = 0$

$8x - 16 + 14y + 14 + 13z + 39 = 0$

$8x + 14y + 13z + 37 = 0$

Answer: $8 x+14 y+13 z+37=0$

</details>

---

### Q66. MHT CET 2024 (10 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The vector equation of the plane passing through the point $\mathrm{A}(1,2,-1)$ and parallel to the vectors $2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}-\hat{k}$ and $\hat{i}-\hat{j}+3 \hat{k}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})=-9$
- **(B)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{k}})=9$
- **(C)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{k}})=9$
- **(D)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})=-9$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})=-9$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The normal vector to the plane is given by the cross product of the vectors parallel to the plane.

$\vec{n} = (2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - \hat{k}) \times (\hat{i} - \hat{j} + 3\hat{k})$

$\vec{n} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & 1 & -1 \\ 1 & -1 & 3 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(3 - 1) - \hat{j}(6 + 1) + \hat{k}(-2 - 1) = 2\hat{i} - 7\hat{j} - 3\hat{k}$

The plane passes through the point $A(1, 2, -1)$, so its position vector is $\vec{a} = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - \hat{k}$.

The vector equation of the plane is $\vec{r} \cdot \vec{n} = \vec{a} \cdot \vec{n}$.

$\vec{a} \cdot \vec{n} = (\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - \hat{k}) \cdot (2\hat{i} - 7\hat{j} - 3\hat{k})$

$\vec{a} \cdot \vec{n} = (1)(2) + (2)(-7) + (-1)(-3) = 2 - 14 + 3 = -9$

Therefore, the equation of the plane is $\vec{r} \cdot (2\hat{i} - 7\hat{j} - 3\hat{k}) = -9$.

Answer: $\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})=-9$

</details>

### Q67. MHT CET 2024 (10 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Equation of the plane, through the points $(-1,2,-2)$ and $(-1,3,2)$ and perpendicular to $y z$ - plane, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4 y+z=10$
- **(B)** $4 y-z+10=0$
- **(C)** $4 y-z=10$
- **(D)** $4 y+z+10=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $4 y-z=10$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the equation of the plane be $a(x - x_1) + b(y - y_1) + c(z - z_1) = 0$.

Since the plane passes through $(-1, 2, -2)$, the equation becomes:

$a(x + 1) + b(y - 2) + c(z + 2) = 0$

The plane also passes through $(-1, 3, 2)$, so this point must satisfy the equation:

$a(-1 + 1) + b(3 - 2) + c(2 + 2) = 0$

$b + 4c = 0 \Rightarrow b = -4c$

The plane is perpendicular to the $yz$-plane. The normal to the $yz$-plane is the $x$-axis, which has direction ratios $(1, 0, 0)$. Since the required plane is perpendicular to the $yz$-plane, their normals are perpendicular:

$a(1) + b(0) + c(0) = 0 \Rightarrow a = 0$

Substituting $a = 0$ and $b = -4c$ into the equation of the plane, we get:

$0(x + 1) - 4c(y - 2) + c(z + 2) = 0$

Dividing by $-c$ (assuming $c \neq 0$):

$4(y - 2) - (z + 2) = 0$

$4y - 8 - z - 2 = 0$

$4y - z = 10$

Answer: $4 y-z=10$

</details>

### Q68. MHT CET 2024 (09 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let L be the line of intersection of the planes $2 x+3 y+z=1$ and $x+3 y+2 z=2$. If L makes an angle $\alpha$ with the positive X -axis, then $\cos \alpha$ equals

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1
- **(B)** $\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{1}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The normal vectors to the given planes are $\vec{n_1} = 2\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} + \hat{k}$ and $\vec{n_2} = \hat{i} + 3\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$.

The direction vector $\vec{v}$ of the line of intersection $L$ is perpendicular to both normal vectors, given by their cross product:

$\vec{v} = \vec{n_1} \times \vec{n_2} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & 3 & 1 \\ 1 & 3 & 2 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{v} = \hat{i}(6 - 3) - \hat{j}(4 - 1) + \hat{k}(6 - 3) = 3\hat{i} - 3\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$

The direction ratios of the line $L$ are proportional to $(3, -3, 3)$, which simplifies to $(1, -1, 1)$.

The angle $\alpha$ is the angle between the line $L$ and the positive X-axis (which has the direction vector $\hat{i}$).

$\cos \alpha = \dfrac{\vec{v} \cdot \hat{i}}{|\vec{v}| |\hat{i}|} = \dfrac{3}{\sqrt{3^2 + (-3)^2 + 3^2} \sqrt{1^2}}$

$\cos \alpha = \dfrac{3}{\sqrt{27}} = \dfrac{3}{3\sqrt{3}} = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}$

Answer: $\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}$

</details>

---

### Q69. MHT CET 2024 (09 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A plane which is perpendicular to two planes $2 x-2 y+z=0$ and $x-y+2 z=4$, passes through $(1,2,1)$. The distance of the plane from the point $(2,3,4)$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{\dfrac{2}{5}}$ units
- **(B)** $\sqrt{2}$ units
- **(C)** $\dfrac{2}{\sqrt{5}}$ units
- **(D)** $\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{5}}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\sqrt{2}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the normal vectors of the given planes be $\vec{n}_1$ and $\vec{n}_2$.

$\vec{n}_1 = 2\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + \hat{k}$

$\vec{n}_2 = \hat{i} - \hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$

Since the required plane is perpendicular to both given planes, its normal vector $\vec{n}$ is parallel to the cross product of $\vec{n}_1$ and $\vec{n}_2$.

$\vec{n} = \vec{n}_1 \times \vec{n}_2 = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & -2 & 1 \\ 1 & -1 & 2 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(-4 + 1) - \hat{j}(4 - 1) + \hat{k}(-2 + 2) = -3\hat{i} - 3\hat{j}$

The direction ratios of the normal to the required plane are proportional to $(1, 1, 0)$.

The equation of the plane passing through $(1, 2, 1)$ with normal vector $\hat{i} + \hat{j}$ is:

$1(x - 1) + 1(y - 2) + 0(z - 1) = 0$

$x + y - 3 = 0$

The perpendicular distance $d$ of the point $(2, 3, 4)$ from the plane $x + y - 3 = 0$ is given by:

$d = \dfrac{|2 + 3 - 3|}{\sqrt{1^2 + 1^2 + 0^2}}$

$d = \dfrac{2}{\sqrt{2}} = \sqrt{2}$ units

Answer: $\sqrt{2}$ units

</details>

---

### Q70. MHT CET 2024 (09 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The distance of the point $(1,3,-7)$ from the plane passing through the point $(1,-1,-1)$ having normal perpendicular to both the lines $\dfrac{x-1}{1}=\dfrac{y+2}{-2}=\dfrac{z-4}{3}$ and $\dfrac{x-2}{2}=\dfrac{y+1}{-1}=\dfrac{z+7}{-1}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{10}{\sqrt{83}}$ units.
- **(B)** $\dfrac{5}{\sqrt{83}}$ units.
- **(C)** $\dfrac{10}{\sqrt{74}}$ units.
- **(D)** $\dfrac{20}{\sqrt{74}}$ units.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\dfrac{10}{\sqrt{83}}$ units.**

**Detailed Solution:**

The direction vectors of the given lines are $\vec{b_1} = \hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$ and $\vec{b_2} = 2\hat{i} - \hat{j} - \hat{k}$.

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ to the plane is perpendicular to both lines, so it is given by their cross product:

$\vec{n} = \vec{b_1} \times \vec{b_2} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & -2 & 3 \\ 2 & -1 & -1 \end{vmatrix} = 5\hat{i} + 7\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$

The equation of the plane passing through the point $(1, -1, -1)$ with normal vector $\vec{n}$ is:

$5(x - 1) + 7(y + 1) + 3(z + 1) = 0$

$5x + 7y + 3z + 5 = 0$

The perpendicular distance $d$ of the point $(1, 3, -7)$ from this plane is:

$d = \dfrac{|5(1) + 7(3) + 3(-7) + 5|}{\sqrt{5^2 + 7^2 + 3^2}}$

$d = \dfrac{|5 + 21 - 21 + 5|}{\sqrt{25 + 49 + 9}}$

$d = \dfrac{10}{\sqrt{83}}$ units.

Answer: $\dfrac{10}{\sqrt{83}}$ units.

</details>

### Q71. MHT CET 2024 (09 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane, passing through the point $(1,1,1)$ and perpendicular to the planes $2 x+y-2 z=5$ and $3 x-6 y-2 z=7$, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $14 x+2 y-15 z=1$
- **(B)** $14 x-2 y+15 z=27$
- **(C)** $14 x+2 y+15 z=31$
- **(D)** $-14 x+2 y+15 z=3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $14 x+2 y+15 z=31$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the normal vector of the required plane be $\vec{n}$. Since the plane is perpendicular to the given planes, its normal vector is perpendicular to the normal vectors of the given planes.

The normal vectors of the given planes are $\vec{n}_1 = 2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}$ and $\vec{n}_2 = 3\hat{i} - 6\hat{j} - 2\hat{k}$.

$\vec{n} = \vec{n}_1 \times \vec{n}_2 = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & 1 & -2 \\ 3 & -6 & -2 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(-2 - 12) - \hat{j}(-4 + 6) + \hat{k}(-12 - 3)$

$\vec{n} = -14\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} - 15\hat{k}$

Taking the direction ratios of the normal as $14, 2, 15$, the equation of the plane passing through $(1, 1, 1)$ is:

$14(x - 1) + 2(y - 1) + 15(z - 1) = 0$

$14x + 2y + 15z = 31$

Answer: $14 x+2 y+15 z=31$

</details>

### Q72. MHT CET 2024 (04 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane, passing through the point $(-1,2,-3)$ and parallel to the lines. $\dfrac{x-1}{3}=\dfrac{y-2}{2}=\dfrac{z}{-4}$ and $\dfrac{x}{2}=\dfrac{y-1}{-3}=\dfrac{z-2}{2}$, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $8 x-14 y-13 z-3=0$
- **(B)** $8 x-14 y+13 z+75=0$
- **(C)** $8 x+14 y+13 z+19=0$
- **(D)** $8 x+14 y-13 z-59=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $8 x+14 y+13 z+19=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The direction ratios of the given lines are $\vec{b}_1 = 3\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - 4\hat{k}$ and $\vec{b}_2 = 2\hat{i} - 3\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$.

Since the plane is parallel to both lines, its normal vector $\vec{n}$ is perpendicular to both $\vec{b}_1$ and $\vec{b}_2$.

$\vec{n} = \vec{b}_1 \times \vec{b}_2 = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 3 & 2 & -4 \\ 2 & -3 & 2 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(4 - 12) - \hat{j}(6 - (-8)) + \hat{k}(-9 - 4)$

$\vec{n} = -8\hat{i} - 14\hat{j} - 13\hat{k}$

The direction ratios of the normal to the plane can be taken as $(8, 14, 13)$.

The equation of the plane passing through $(-1, 2, -3)$ is

$8(x - (-1)) + 14(y - 2) + 13(z - (-3)) = 0$

$8(x + 1) + 14(y - 2) + 13(z + 3) = 0$

$8x + 8 + 14y - 28 + 13z + 39 = 0$

$8x + 14y + 13z + 19 = 0$

Answer: $8 x+14 y+13 z+19=0$

</details>

### Q73. MHT CET 2024 (04 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let P be a plane passing through the points $(2,1,0),(4,1,1)$ and $(5,0,1)$ and $R$ be the point $(2,1,6)$. Then image of $R$ in the plane $P$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(6,5,2)$
- **(B)** $(4,3,2)$
- **(C)** $(6,5,-2)$
- **(D)** $(3,4,-2)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $(6,5,-2)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the given points be $A(2,1,0)$, $B(4,1,1)$ and $C(5,0,1)$.

The vectors $\overrightarrow{AB}$ and $\overrightarrow{AC}$ are given by:

$\overrightarrow{AB} = (4-2)\hat{i} + (1-1)\hat{j} + (1-0)\hat{k} = 2\hat{i} + \hat{k}$

$\overrightarrow{AC} = (5-2)\hat{i} + (0-1)\hat{j} + (1-0)\hat{k} = 3\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k}$

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ to the plane is:

$\vec{n} = \overrightarrow{AB} \times \overrightarrow{AC} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & 0 & 1 \\ 3 & -1 & 1 \end{vmatrix} = \hat{i}(0 - (-1)) - \hat{j}(2 - 3) + \hat{k}(-2 - 0) = \hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}$

The equation of the plane passing through $A(2,1,0)$ with normal vector $\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}$ is:

$1(x - 2) + 1(y - 1) - 2(z - 0) = 0$

$x + y - 2z - 3 = 0$

Let the image of the point $R(2,1,6)$ in the plane be $(x', y', z')$. Using the formula for the image of a point in a plane:

$\dfrac{x' - 2}{1} = \dfrac{y' - 1}{1} = \dfrac{z' - 6}{-2} = -2 \dfrac{1(2) + 1(1) - 2(6) - 3}{1^2 + 1^2 + (-2)^2}$

$\dfrac{x' - 2}{1} = \dfrac{y' - 1}{1} = \dfrac{z' - 6}{-2} = -2 \left(\dfrac{2 + 1 - 12 - 3}{6}\right)$

$\dfrac{x' - 2}{1} = \dfrac{y' - 1}{1} = \dfrac{z' - 6}{-2} = -2 \left(\dfrac{-12}{6}\right) = 4$

Solving for $x', y', z'$:

$x' = 2 + 4 = 6$

$y' = 1 + 4 = 5$

$z' = 6 - 8 = -2$

The image of $R$ in the plane is $(6, 5, -2)$.

Answer: $(6,5,-2)$

</details>

---

### Q74. MHT CET 2024 (04 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the points $(1,-1, \lambda)$ and $(-3,0,1)$ are equidistant from the plane $3 x-4 y-12 z+13=0$, then the sum of all possible values of $\lambda$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{7}{3}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{10}{3}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{4}{3}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{5}{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\dfrac{10}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The distance of a point $(x_1, y_1, z_1)$ from the plane $Ax + By + Cz + D = 0$ is given by $\dfrac{|Ax_1 + By_1 + Cz_1 + D|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2 + C^2}}$.

The distance of the point $(1, -1, \lambda)$ from the plane $3x - 4y - 12z + 13 = 0$ is:

$d_1 = \dfrac{|3(1) - 4(-1) - 12(\lambda) + 13|}{\sqrt{3^2 + (-4)^2 + (-12)^2}} = \dfrac{|20 - 12\lambda|}{13}$

The distance of the point $(-3, 0, 1)$ from the plane is:

$d_2 = \dfrac{|3(-3) - 4(0) - 12(1) + 13|}{13} = \dfrac{|-8|}{13} = \dfrac{8}{13}$

Since the points are equidistant from the plane, $d_1 = d_2$:

$\dfrac{|20 - 12\lambda|}{13} = \dfrac{8}{13}$

$|20 - 12\lambda| = 8$

This gives two possible cases:

$20 - 12\lambda = 8 \Rightarrow 12\lambda = 12 \Rightarrow \lambda = 1$

$20 - 12\lambda = -8 \Rightarrow 12\lambda = 28 \Rightarrow \lambda = \dfrac{7}{3}$

The sum of all possible values of $\lambda$ is $1 + \dfrac{7}{3} = \dfrac{10}{3}$.

Answer: $\dfrac{10}{3}$

</details>

---

### Q75. MHT CET 2024 (04 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A plane which is perpendicular to two planes $2 x-2 y+z=0$ and $x-y+2 z=4$, passes through $(1,-2,1)$. The distance of the plane from the point $(1,2,2)$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0 units
- **(B)** 1 units
- **(C)** $\sqrt{2}$ units
- **(D)** $2 \sqrt{2}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $2 \sqrt{2}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the normal vectors of the given planes be $\vec{n}_1$ and $\vec{n}_2$.

$\vec{n}_1 = 2\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + \hat{k}$

$\vec{n}_2 = \hat{i} - \hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ of the required plane is perpendicular to both $\vec{n}_1$ and $\vec{n}_2$.

$\vec{n} = \vec{n}_1 \times \vec{n}_2 = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & -2 & 1 \\ 1 & -1 & 2 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(-4 + 1) - \hat{j}(4 - 1) + \hat{k}(-2 + 2) = -3\hat{i} - 3\hat{j}$

The direction ratios of the normal to the required plane are proportional to $(1, 1, 0)$.

The equation of the plane passing through $(1, -2, 1)$ is:

$1(x - 1) + 1(y - (-2)) + 0(z - 1) = 0$

$x + y + 1 = 0$

The perpendicular distance of this plane from the point $(1, 2, 2)$ is:

$d = \dfrac{|1(1) + 1(2) + 1|}{\sqrt{1^2 + 1^2}}$

$d = \dfrac{4}{\sqrt{2}} = 2\sqrt{2}$ units.

Answer: $2 \sqrt{2}$ units

</details>

### Q76. MHT CET 2024 (04 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let the vectors $\overline{\mathrm{a}}, \overline{\mathrm{b}}, \overline{\mathrm{c}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{d}}$ be such that $(\overline{\mathrm{a}} \times \overline{\mathrm{b}}) \times(\overline{\mathrm{c}} \times \overline{\mathrm{d}})=\overline{0}$. Let $\mathrm{P}_1$ and $\mathrm{P}_2$ be the planes determined by the pair of vectors $\stackrel{\rightharpoonup}{\mathrm{a}}, \overline{\mathrm{b}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{c}}, \overline{\mathrm{d}}$ respectively, then the angle between $P_1$ and $P_2$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0
- **(B)** $\dfrac{\pi}{4}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{\pi}{3}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{\pi}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 0**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\vec{n}_1$ be the normal vector to the plane $P_1$ containing vectors $\vec{a}$ and $\vec{b}$.

$\vec{n}_1 = \vec{a} \times \vec{b}$

Let $\vec{n}_2$ be the normal vector to the plane $P_2$ containing vectors $\vec{c}$ and $\vec{d}$.

$\vec{n}_2 = \vec{c} \times \vec{d}$

It is given that $(\vec{a} \times \vec{b}) \times (\vec{c} \times \vec{d}) = \vec{0}$.

Substituting the normal vectors, we get:

$\vec{n}_1 \times \vec{n}_2 = \vec{0}$

This implies that the normal vectors $\vec{n}_1$ and $\vec{n}_2$ are parallel.

Since the normals to the planes are parallel, the planes $P_1$ and $P_2$ are also parallel.

Thus, the angle between the planes $P_1$ and $P_2$ is $0$.

Answer: 0

</details>

### Q77. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Equation of the plane containing the straight line $\dfrac{x}{2}=\dfrac{y}{3}=\dfrac{z}{4}$ and perpendicular to the plane containing the straight lines $\dfrac{x}{3}=\dfrac{y}{4}=\dfrac{z}{2}$ and $\dfrac{x}{4}=\dfrac{y}{2}=\dfrac{z}{3}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+2 y-2 z=0$
- **(B)** $3 x+2 y-2 z=0$
- **(C)** $\quad x-2 y+z=0$
- **(D)** $5 x+2 y-4 z=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\quad x-2 y+z=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the plane containing the lines $\dfrac{x}{3}=\dfrac{y}{4}=\dfrac{z}{2}$ and $\dfrac{x}{4}=\dfrac{y}{2}=\dfrac{z}{3}$ be $P_1$.

The direction vectors of these lines are $\vec{d}_2 = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$ and $\vec{d}_3 = 4\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$.

The normal vector to the plane $P_1$ is given by their cross product:
$\vec{n}_1 = \vec{d}_2 \times \vec{d}_3 = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 3 & 4 & 2 \\ 4 & 2 & 3 \end{vmatrix} = \hat{i}(12 - 4) - \hat{j}(9 - 8) + \hat{k}(6 - 16) = 8\hat{i} - \hat{j} - 10\hat{k}$

Let the required plane be $P$. It contains the line $\dfrac{x}{2}=\dfrac{y}{3}=\dfrac{z}{4}$, which passes through the origin $(0,0,0)$ and has the direction vector $\vec{d}_1 = 2\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} + 4\hat{k}$.

Since plane $P$ is perpendicular to plane $P_1$, its normal vector $\vec{n}$ must be perpendicular to both $\vec{d}_1$ and $\vec{n}_1$.

$\vec{n} = \vec{d}_1 \times \vec{n}_1 = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & 3 & 4 \\ 8 & -1 & -10 \end{vmatrix} = \hat{i}(-30 + 4) - \hat{j}(-20 - 32) + \hat{k}(-2 - 24) = -26\hat{i} + 52\hat{j} - 26\hat{k}$

Dividing by $-26$, the direction ratios of the normal to the required plane are $(1, -2, 1)$.

Since the plane $P$ passes through the origin $(0,0,0)$, its equation is:
$1(x - 0) - 2(y - 0) + 1(z - 0) = 0$

$x - 2y + z = 0$

Answer: $\quad x-2 y+z=0$

</details>

---

### Q78. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane passing through the point $(1, 1, 1)$ and perpendicular to the planes $2x - y + 2z = 5$ and $3x + 6y + 2z = 7$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** \(14 x+2 y-15 z=1\)
- **(B)** \(14 x-2 y+15 z=27\)
- **(C)** \(14 x+2 y+15 z=31\)
- **(D)** \(-14 x+2 y+15 z=3\)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) \(-14 x+2 y+15 z=3\)**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the normal vector of the required plane be $\vec{n}$. Since the required plane is perpendicular to the given planes, its normal vector will be perpendicular to the normal vectors of the given planes.

The normal vectors of the given planes are $\vec{n}_1 = 2\hat{i} - \hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$ and $\vec{n}_2 = 3\hat{i} + 6\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$.

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ is given by their cross product:

$\vec{n} = \vec{n}_1 \times \vec{n}_2 = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & -1 & 2 \\ 3 & 6 & 2 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(-2 - 12) - \hat{j}(4 - 6) + \hat{k}(12 - (-3))$

$\vec{n} = -14\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 15\hat{k}$

The equation of the plane passing through $(1, 1, 1)$ with normal vector $\vec{n}$ is:

$-14(x - 1) + 2(y - 1) + 15(z - 1) = 0$

$-14x + 14 + 2y - 2 + 15z - 15 = 0$

$-14x + 2y + 15z = 3$

Answer: \(-14 x+2 y+15 z=3\)

</details>

### Q79. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Equation of the plane containing the straight line $\dfrac{x}{3}=\dfrac{y}{2}=\dfrac{z}{4}$ and perpendicular to the plane containing the straight lines $\dfrac{x}{4}=\dfrac{y}{3}=\dfrac{z}{2}$ and $\dfrac{\dot{x}}{2}=\dfrac{y}{-4}=\dfrac{z}{3}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\quad 6 x-67 y-29 \mathrm{z}=0$
- **(B)** $6 x+67 y-29 z=0$
- **(C)** $\quad 6 x-67 y+29 z=0$
- **(D)** $6 x+67 y+29 z=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\quad 6 x-67 y+29 z=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the direction vectors of the given lines be $\vec{d_1} = 3\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 4\hat{k}$, $\vec{d_2} = 4\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$, and $\vec{d_3} = 2\hat{i} - 4\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$.

The normal vector $\vec{n_1}$ to the plane containing the lines with direction vectors $\vec{d_2}$ and $\vec{d_3}$ is given by their cross product:

$\vec{n_1} = \vec{d_2} \times \vec{d_3} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 4 & 3 & 2 \\ 2 & -4 & 3 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n_1} = \hat{i}(9 - (-8)) - \hat{j}(12 - 4) + \hat{k}(-16 - 6) = 17\hat{i} - 8\hat{j} - 22\hat{k}$

The required plane contains the first line (which passes through the origin and has direction $\vec{d_1}$) and is perpendicular to the plane with normal $\vec{n_1}$. Therefore, the normal vector $\vec{n}$ of the required plane is perpendicular to both $\vec{d_1}$ and $\vec{n_1}$:

$\vec{n} = \vec{d_1} \times \vec{n_1} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 3 & 2 & 4 \\ 17 & -8 & -22 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(-44 - (-32)) - \hat{j}(-66 - 68) + \hat{k}(-24 - 34)$

$\vec{n} = -12\hat{i} + 134\hat{j} - 58\hat{k}$

Dividing by $-2$ to simplify the direction ratios, we get the normal vector as $6\hat{i} - 67\hat{j} + 29\hat{k}$.

Since the required plane contains the first line, it must pass through the origin $(0,0,0)$. The equation of the plane is:

$6(x - 0) - 67(y - 0) + 29(z - 0) = 0$

$6x - 67y + 29z = 0$

Answer: $\quad 6 x-67 y+29 z=0$

</details>

---

### Q80. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Let $P$ be the image of the point $(3,1,7)$ with respect to the plane $x-y+z=3$. Then the equation of the plane passing through P and containing the straight line $\dfrac{x}{1}=\dfrac{y}{2}=\dfrac{z}{1}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-4 y-x+7 \mathrm{z}=0$
- **(B)** $x-4 y-7 z=0$
- **(C)** $x-4 y+7 z=0$
- **(D)** $\quad x+4 y+7 z=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $x-4 y+7 z=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $P(x_1, y_1, z_1)$ be the image of the point $(3,1,7)$ with respect to the plane $x-y+z-3=0$.

Using the formula for the image of a point:

$\dfrac{x_1 - 3}{1} = \dfrac{y_1 - 1}{-1} = \dfrac{z_1 - 7}{1} = -2 \dfrac{1(3) - 1(1) + 1(7) - 3}{1^2 + (-1)^2 + 1^2}$

$\dfrac{x_1 - 3}{1} = \dfrac{y_1 - 1}{-1} = \dfrac{z_1 - 7}{1} = -2 \left(\dfrac{6}{3}\right) = -4$

Solving for $x_1, y_1, z_1$:

$x_1 = 3 - 4 = -1$

$y_1 = 1 + 4 = 5$

$z_1 = 7 - 4 = 3$

Thus, the point $P$ is $(-1, 5, 3)$.

The required plane contains the line $\dfrac{x}{1}=\dfrac{y}{2}=\dfrac{z}{1}$, which passes through the origin $(0,0,0)$ and has direction ratios $(1, 2, 1)$.

Since the plane contains the origin, its equation is of the form $ax + by + cz = 0$.

The normal vector to the plane is perpendicular to both the line's direction vector $\vec{d} = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + \hat{k}$ and the position vector of $P$, $\overrightarrow{OP} = -\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$.

The normal vector $\vec{n}$ is given by the cross product:

$\vec{n} = \vec{d} \times \overrightarrow{OP} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & 2 & 1 \\ -1 & 5 & 3 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i}(6 - 5) - \hat{j}(3 + 1) + \hat{k}(5 + 2)$

$\vec{n} = \hat{i} - 4\hat{j} + 7\hat{k}$

The equation of the plane passing through the origin with normal vector $\hat{i} - 4\hat{j} + 7\hat{k}$ is:

$1(x - 0) - 4(y - 0) + 7(z - 0) = 0$

$x - 4y + 7z = 0$

Answer: $x-4 y+7 z=0$

</details>

---

### Q81. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of the plane, passing through the mid point of the line segment of join of the points $\mathrm{P}(1,2,5)$ and $Q(3,4,3)$ and perpendicular to it, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\quad x+y-\mathrm{z}+1=0$
- **(B)** $x+y-z-1=0$
- **(C)** $x+y+z+1=0$
- **(D)** $\quad x-y-z+1=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $x+y-z-1=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The midpoint of the line segment joining $P(1,2,5)$ and $Q(3,4,3)$ is given by $\left(\dfrac{1+3}{2}, \dfrac{2+4}{2}, \dfrac{5+3}{2}\right) = (2,3,4)$.

The normal vector to the plane is parallel to the vector $\overrightarrow{PQ}$.

Direction ratios of $\overrightarrow{PQ}$ are $(3-1, 4-2, 3-5) = (2, 2, -2)$.

Taking the direction ratios of the normal to the plane as $(1, 1, -1)$.

The equation of the plane passing through $(2,3,4)$ with normal direction ratios $(1, 1, -1)$ is

$1(x-2) + 1(y-3) - 1(z-4) = 0$

$x + y - z - 2 - 3 + 4 = 0$

$x + y - z - 1 = 0$

Answer: $x+y-z-1=0$

</details>

---

### Q82. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The Cartesian equation of the plane, passing through the points $(3,1,1),(1,2,3)$ and $(-1,4,2)$, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5 x+6 y-2 z-23=0$
- **(B)** $-5 x+6 y+2 z+23=0$
- **(C)** $5 x+6 y+2 z-23=0$
- **(D)** $5 x-6 y+2 z-23 \doteq 0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $5 x+6 y+2 z-23=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the plane passing through three non-collinear points $(x_1, y_1, z_1)$, $(x_2, y_2, z_2)$, and $(x_3, y_3, z_3)$ is given by the determinant:

$\begin{vmatrix} x - x_1 & y - y_1 & z - z_1 \\ x_2 - x_1 & y_2 - y_1 & z_2 - z_1 \\ x_3 - x_1 & y_3 - y_1 & z_3 - z_1 \end{vmatrix} = 0$

Substituting the given points $(3,1,1)$, $(1,2,3)$, and $(-1,4,2)$:

$\begin{vmatrix} x - 3 & y - 1 & z - 1 \\ 1 - 3 & 2 - 1 & 3 - 1 \\ -1 - 3 & 4 - 1 & 2 - 1 \end{vmatrix} = 0$

$\begin{vmatrix} x - 3 & y - 1 & z - 1 \\ -2 & 1 & 2 \\ -4 & 3 & 1 \end{vmatrix} = 0$

Expanding the determinant along the first row:

$(x - 3)[(1)(1) - (2)(3)] - (y - 1)[(-2)(1) - (2)(-4)] + (z - 1)[(-2)(3) - (1)(-4)] = 0$

$(x - 3)(1 - 6) - (y - 1)(-2 + 8) + (z - 1)(-6 + 4) = 0$

$-5(x - 3) - 6(y - 1) - 2(z - 1) = 0$

$-5x + 15 - 6y + 6 - 2z + 2 = 0$

$-5x - 6y - 2z + 23 = 0$

Multiplying by $-1$, we get the equation of the plane as:

$5x + 6y + 2z - 23 = 0$

Answer: $5 x+6 y+2 z-23=0$

</details>

---

### Q83. MHT CET 2023 (14 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The mirror image of $\mathrm{P}(2,4,-1)$ in the plane $x-y+2 z-2=0$ is $(\mathrm{a}, \mathrm{b}, \mathrm{c})$, then the value of $\mathrm{a}+\mathrm{b}+\mathrm{c}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 4
- **(B)** 5
- **(C)** 7
- **(D)** 9

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 9**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/J7VngjQHu_hT9QDgWE4BiuG2SAwbsNT8GjA1Fo3_oTY.original.fullsize.png">

The d.r.s. of the normal to the plane are $1,-1,2$.
$\therefore \quad$ The equation of line PM is
$\begin{aligned}
& \frac{x-2}{1}=\frac{y-4}{-1}=\frac{\mathrm{z}+1}{2}=\lambda(\text { say }) \\
& \Rightarrow x=\lambda+2, y=-\lambda+4, \mathrm{z}=2 \lambda-1 \\
& \text { Let } \mathrm{M} \equiv(\lambda+2,-\lambda+4,2 \lambda-1)
\end{aligned}$
$\begin{array}{ll}
\therefore \quad & \text { Equation of plane becomes } \\
& 1(\lambda+2)-1(-\lambda+4)+2(2 \lambda-1)-2=0 \\
& \Rightarrow \lambda=1 \\
\therefore \quad & M \equiv(3,3,1)
\end{array}$
Since $M$ is the mid-point of $P Q$.
$\begin{aligned}
& \therefore \quad \frac{2+\mathrm{a}}{2}=3, \frac{4+\mathrm{b}}{2}=3, \frac{-1+\mathrm{c}}{2}=1 \\
& \Rightarrow \mathrm{a}=4, \mathrm{~b}=2, \mathrm{c}=3 \\
& \Rightarrow \mathrm{a}+\mathrm{b}+\mathrm{c}=9
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q84. MHT CET 2023 (14 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Equation of the plane passing through $(1,-1,2)$ and perpendicular to the planes $x+2 y-2 z=4$ and $3 x+2 y+z=6$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $6 x-7 y-4 z-5=0$
- **(B)** $6 x+7 y-4 z+5=0$
- **(C)** $6 x-7 y+4 z+5=0$
- **(D)** $6 x+7 y+4 z-5=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $6 x-7 y-4 z-5=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of plane passing through $(1,-1,2)$ is $\mathrm{a}(x-1)+\mathrm{b}(y+1)+\mathrm{c}(\mathrm{z}-2)=0$

Since plane (i) is perpendicular to the planes $x+2 y-2 z=4$ and $3 x+2 y+z=6$
$$
\begin{array}{ll}
\therefore \quad & a+2 b-2 c=0 \\
& \text { and } 3 a+2 b+c=0 \\
& \Rightarrow \frac{a}{6}=\frac{b}{-7}=\frac{c}{-4}
\end{array}
$$
$\therefore \quad$ The equation of the required plane is
$$
\begin{aligned}
& 6(x-1)-7(y+1)-4(z-2)=0 \\
& \Rightarrow 6 x-7 y-4 z-5=0
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q85. MHT CET 2023 (13 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A plane is parallel to two lines whose direction ratios are $2,0,-2$ and $-2,2,0$ and it contains the point $(2,2,2)$. If it cuts co-ordinate axes at $\mathrm{A}, \mathrm{B}, \mathrm{C}$ then the volume of tetrahedron $\mathrm{OABC}$ (in cubic units) is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 216
- **(B)** 6
- **(C)** 36
- **(D)** 9

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 36**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of plane passing through $(2,2,2)$
is $\mathrm{a}(x-2)+\mathrm{b}(y-2)+\mathrm{c}(\mathrm{z}-2)=0$
Also, $2 \mathrm{a}-2 \mathrm{c}=0$
$-2 a+2 b=0$
$\Rightarrow \mathrm{a}=\mathrm{b}=\mathrm{c}$
$\begin{aligned} & x+y+z-6=0 \\ & \Rightarrow \frac{x}{6}+\frac{y}{6}+\frac{z}{6}=1\end{aligned}$
It cuts the co-ordinate axes at $\mathrm{A}(6,0,0)$, $\mathrm{B}(0,6,0)$ and $\mathrm{C}(0,0,6)$
$\therefore \quad \overline{\mathrm{a}}=6 \hat{\mathrm{i}}, \overline{\mathrm{b}}=6 \hat{\mathrm{j}}, \overline{\mathrm{c}}=6 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
$\therefore \quad$ Volume of tetrahedron $=\frac{1}{6}\left[\begin{array}{lll}-\bar{a} & \bar{b} & \bar{c}\end{array}\right]$
$\begin{aligned} & =\frac{1}{6}\left|\begin{array}{lll}6 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 6 & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & 6\end{array}\right| \\ & =36 \text { cubic units }\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q86. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Equation of plane containing the line $\frac{x}{2}=\frac{y}{3}=\frac{z}{4}$ and perpendicular to the plane containing the lines $\frac{x}{3}=\frac{y}{4}=\frac{z}{2}$ and $\frac{x}{4}=\frac{y}{2}=\frac{z}{3}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+2 y+z=0$
- **(B)** $x+2 y-z=0$
- **(C)** $x-2 y+z=0$
- **(D)** $x-2 y-\mathrm{z}=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $x-2 y+z=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of the plane containing $\frac{x}{3}=\frac{y}{4}=\frac{z}{2}$ and $\frac{x}{4}=\frac{y}{2}=\frac{z}{3}$ is
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \left|\begin{array}{ccc}
x & y & z \\
3 & 4 & 2 \\
4 & 2 & 3
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
& \Rightarrow 8 x-y-10 z=0
\end{aligned}
$$

Now required plane is perpendicular to this plane.

Consider option (C)
$$
(8)(1)+(-1)(-2)+(-10)(1)=0
$$

</details>

### Q87. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of the plane through $(-1,1,2)$ whose normal makes equal acute angles with co-ordinate axes is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+y+z-3=0$
- **(B)** $x+y+z-2=0$
- **(C)** $x+y-z-2=0$
- **(D)** $x-y+z-3=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $x+y+z-2=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Note that $(-1,1,2)$ is satisfied by only option (B) Alternate Method:
Let $A \equiv(-1,1,2)$
$$
\begin{aligned}
\therefore \quad \bar{a} & =-\hat{i}+\hat{j}+2 \hat{k} \\
& \bar{n}=\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}
\end{aligned}
$$
$\therefore \quad$ equation of plane is $\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{n}}=\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{n}}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \Rightarrow \overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})=(-\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{k}}) \cdot(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}) \\
& \Rightarrow \overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})=2 \\
& \Rightarrow(x \hat{\mathrm{i}}+y \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\mathrm{z} \hat{\mathrm{k}}) \cdot(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})=2 \\
& \Rightarrow x+y+\mathrm{z}-2=0
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q88. MHT CET 2023 (11 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A plane is parallel to two lines, whose direction ratios are $1,0,-1$ and $-1,1,0$ and it contains the point $(1,1,1)$. If it cuts co-ordinate axes (X, Y, Z - axes resp.) at $\mathrm{A}, \mathrm{B}, \mathrm{C}$, then the volume of the tetrahedron $\mathrm{OABC}$ is cu. units.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $9$
- **(B)** $\frac{9}{4}$
- **(C)** $\frac{9}{2}$
- **(D)** $27$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{9}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of the plane passing through $(1,1,1)$ is given as
$$
\mathrm{a}(x-1)+\mathrm{b}(y-1)+\mathrm{c}(\mathrm{z}-1)=0
$$

As the plane is parallel to the lines having direction ratios $1,0,-1$ and $-1,1,0$, we get
$$
\begin{aligned}
& a-c=0 \text { and }-a+b=0 \\
& \Rightarrow a=b=c
\end{aligned}
$$
$\therefore \quad$ From (i) and (ii), we get
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \therefore \quad x-1+y-1+z-1=0 \\
& \therefore \quad x+y+z=3 \quad \Rightarrow \frac{x}{3}+\frac{y}{3}+\frac{z}{3}=1
\end{aligned}
$$
$\therefore \quad$ Co-ordinates of A, B, C are $(3,0,0),(0,3,0)$ and $(0,0,3)$ respectively.
$$
\begin{aligned}
\therefore \quad \text { Volume of tetrahedron OABC } & =\frac{1}{6}\left|\begin{array}{lll}
3 & 0 & 0 \\
0 & 3 & 0 \\
0 & 0 & 3
\end{array}\right| \\
& =\frac{1}{6} \times 27 \\
& =\frac{9}{2} \text { cu. units }
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q89. MHT CET 2023 (10 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Two lines $\frac{x-3}{1}=\frac{y+1}{3}=\frac{z-6}{-1} \quad$ and $\frac{x+5}{7}=\frac{y-2}{-6}=\frac{z-3}{4} \quad$ intersect at the point $\mathrm{R}$. Then reflection of $\mathrm{R}$ in the $x y$-plane has co-ordinates

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(2,-4,-7)$
- **(B)** $(2,-4,7)$
- **(C)** $(-2,4,7)$
- **(D)** $(2,4,7)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $(2,-4,-7)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\frac{x-3}{1}=\frac{y+1}{3}=\frac{z-6}{-1}=\lambda$
$\Rightarrow x=3+\lambda, y=3 \lambda-1, \mathrm{z}=-\lambda+6$
Let $\frac{x+5}{7}=\frac{y-2}{-6}=\frac{z-3}{4}=\mu$
$\Rightarrow x=7 \mu-5, y=-6 \mu+2, \mathrm{z}=4 \mu+3$
Both the given lines intersect each other.
$\begin{aligned}
& \text { So, } \lambda+3=7 \mu-5 \\
& \Rightarrow 7 \mu-\lambda=8...(i)
\end{aligned}$
Also, $3 \lambda-1=-6 \mu+2$
$\Rightarrow 6 \mu+3 \lambda=3$...(ii)
From (i) and (ii), we get
$\begin{aligned}
& \mu=1, \lambda=-1 \\
& \text { i.e., } x=2, y=-4, \mathrm{z}=7
\end{aligned}$
$\therefore \quad$ Co-ordinates of the intersection of the given lines are $\mathrm{R}(2,-4,7)$
Hence, reflection of $\mathrm{R}$ in the $x y$-plane is $(2,-4,-7)$.

</details>

---

### Q90. MHT CET 2023 (10 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The perpendicular distance of the origin from the plane $x-3 y+4 z-6=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $6$
- **(B)** $\frac{6}{\sqrt{26}}$
- **(C)** $\frac{1}{\sqrt{26}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{3}{\sqrt{26}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{6}{\sqrt{26}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Length of perpendicular from point $\mathrm{O}(0,0,0)$ to plane $x-3 y+4 z-6=0$ is given by
$\begin{aligned}
d & =\left|\frac{0(1)+0(-3)+0(4)-6}{\sqrt{(1)^2+(-3)^2+(4)^2}}\right| \\
& =\left|\frac{-6}{\sqrt{1+9+16}}\right|=\frac{6}{\sqrt{26}}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q91. MHT CET 2023 (10 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The plane through the intersection of planes $x+y+z=1$ and $2 x+3 y-z+4=0$ and parallel to Y-axis also passes through the point

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(3,3,-1)$
- **(B)** $(-3,0,1)$
- **(C)** $(3,2,1)$
- **(D)** $(-3,0,-1)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $(3,2,1)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of plane through the intersection of given planes is
$\begin{aligned}
& (x+y+z-1)+\lambda(2 x+3 y-z+4)=0 \quad \ldots \text { (i) } \\
& \Rightarrow(1+2 \lambda) x+(1+3 \lambda) y+(1-\lambda) z+4 \lambda-1=0
\end{aligned}$
Since the plane is parallel to $\mathrm{Y}$-axis.
$\begin{aligned}
\therefore \quad & 1+3 \lambda=0 \\
& \Rightarrow \lambda=\frac{-1}{3}
\end{aligned}$
Substituting $\lambda=\frac{-1}{3}$ in (i), we get
$\begin{aligned}
& (x+y+z-1)-\frac{1}{3}(2 x+3 y-z+4)=0 \\
& \Rightarrow x+4 z-7=0
\end{aligned}$
Point $(3,2,1)$ satisfies this equation.

</details>

### Q92. MHT CET 2023 (10 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\mathrm{P}$ be a plane passing through the points $(2,1,0),(4,1,1)$ and $(5,0,1)$ and $R$ be the point $(2,1,6)$. Then image of $\mathrm{R}$ in the plane $\mathrm{P}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(6,5,2)$
- **(B)** $(4,3,2)$
- **(C)** $(6,5,-2)$
- **(D)** $(3,4,-2)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $(6,5,-2)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of the plane passing through $(2,1,0)$, $(4,1,1)$ and $(5,0,1)$ is
$\begin{aligned}
& \left|\begin{array}{lll}
x-2 & y-1 & z-0 \\
4-2 & 1-1 & 1-0 \\
5-2 & 0-1 & 1-0
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
& \Rightarrow x+y-2 z=3
\end{aligned}$
$\mathrm{R}^{\prime}(x, y, \mathrm{z})$ is image of $\mathrm{R}(2,1,6)$ w:r.t. to plane
$\begin{aligned}
& x+y-2 z=3 \\
& \frac{x-2}{1}=\frac{y-1}{1}=\frac{z-6}{-2}=\frac{-2[2+1-2(6)-3]}{1+1+4} \\
& \Rightarrow \frac{x-2}{1}=\frac{y-1}{1}=\frac{z-6}{-2}=4 \\
& \Rightarrow x=6, y=5, \mathrm{z}=-2 \\
\therefore \quad & \mathrm{R}^{\prime}(x, y, \mathrm{z}) \equiv(6,5,-2)
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q93. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of a plane, containing the line of intersection of the planes $2 x-y-4=0$ and $y+2 z-4=0$ and passing through the point $(2,1,0)$, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3 x-2 y+z=4$
- **(B)** $3 x+2 y+z=4$
- **(C)** $3 x-2 y-z=4$
- **(D)** $3 x+2 y-z=-4$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $3 x-2 y-z=4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of plane passing through the intersection of given planes is
$2 x-y-4+\lambda(y+2 z-4)=0$
Since, the plane passes through $(2,1,0)$
$\begin{aligned}
& 2(2)-1-4+\lambda(1+2(0)-4)=0 \\
& 4-1-4-3 \lambda=0 \\
& -1-3 \lambda=0 \\
& \lambda=\frac{-1}{3}
\end{aligned}$
Substituting $\lambda=\frac{-1}{3}$ in equation (i), we get
$\begin{aligned}
& 2 x-y-4-\frac{1}{3}(y+2 z-4)=0 \\
& 6 x-3 y-12-y-2 z+4=0 \\
& 6 x-4 y-2 z-8=0 \\
& 3 x-2 y-z=4
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q94. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The shortest distance between the lines $\frac{x-1}{2}=\frac{y-2}{3}=\frac{z-3}{4}$ and $\frac{x-2}{3}=\frac{y-4}{4}=\frac{z-5}{5}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{1}{\sqrt{14}}$ units.
- **(B)** $\frac{1}{\sqrt{5}}$ units.
- **(C)** $\frac{1}{\sqrt{11}}$ units.
- **(D)** $\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}$ units.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}$ units.**

**Detailed Solution:**

The lines are
$\frac{x-1}{2}=\frac{y-2}{3}=\frac{z-3}{4} \text { and } \frac{x-2}{3}=\frac{y-4}{4}=\frac{z-5}{5}$
Comparing equations with
$\frac{x-x_1}{\mathrm{a}_1}=\frac{y-y_1}{\mathrm{~b}_1}=\frac{\mathrm{z}-\mathrm{z}_1}{\mathrm{c}_1} \text { and } \frac{x-x_2}{\mathrm{a}_2}=\frac{y-y_2}{\mathrm{~b}_2}=\frac{\mathrm{z}-\mathrm{z}_2}{\mathrm{c}_2} \text {, }$
we get
$\begin{aligned}
& x_1=1, y_1=2, \mathrm{z}_1=3 \quad x_2=2, y_2=4, \mathrm{z}_2=5 \\
& \mathrm{a}_1=2, \mathrm{~b}_1=3, \mathrm{c}_1=4 \quad \mathrm{a}_2=3, \mathrm{~b}_2=4, \mathrm{c}_2=5 \\
& \left|\begin{array}{ccc}
x_2-x_1 & y_2-y_1 & z_2-z_1 \\
\mathrm{a}_1 & \mathrm{~b}_1 & \mathrm{c}_1 \\
\mathrm{a}_2 & \mathrm{~b}_2 & \mathrm{c}_2
\end{array}\right|=\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
1 & 2 & 2 \\
2 & 3 & 4 \\
3 & 4 & 5
\end{array}\right| \\
& =1(15-16)-2(10-12)+2(8-9) \\
& =1 \\
& \therefore \quad \sqrt{\left(a_1 b_2-a_2 b_1\right)^2+\left(b_1 c_2-b_2 c_1\right)^2+\left(c_1 a_2-c_2 a_1\right)^2} \\
& =\sqrt{(2 \times 4-3 \times 3)^2+(3 \times 5-4 \times 4)^2+(4 \times 3-5 \times 2)^2} \\
& =\sqrt{1+1+4} \\
& =\sqrt{6} \\
&
\end{aligned}$
Shortest distance between line is $\mathrm{d}$
$\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{d} & =\frac{\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
x_2-x_1 & y_2-y_1 & \mathrm{z}_2-\mathrm{z}_1 \\
\mathrm{a}_1 & \mathrm{~b}_1 & \mathrm{c}_1 \\
\mathrm{a}_2 & \mathrm{~b}_2 & \mathrm{c}_2
\end{array}\right|}{\sqrt{\left(\mathrm{a}_1 \mathrm{~b}_2-\mathrm{a}_2 \mathrm{~b}_1\right)^2+\left(\mathrm{b}_1 \mathrm{c}_2-\mathrm{b}_2 \mathrm{c}_1\right)^2+\sqrt{\left(\mathrm{c}_1 \mathrm{a}_2-\mathrm{c}_2 \mathrm{a}_1\right)^2}}} \\
& =\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q95. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A vector $\vec{n}$ is inclined to $X$-axis at $45^{\circ}$, $\mathrm{Y}$-axis at $60^{\circ}$ and at an acute angle to $\mathrm{Z}$-axis. If $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{n}}$ is normal to a plane passing through the point $(-\sqrt{2}, 1,1)$, then equation of the plane is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{2} x+y+z=0$
- **(B)** $x+\sqrt{2} y+z=1$
- **(C)** $-\sqrt{2} x+y+2 z=5$
- **(D)** $x+y+\sqrt{2} z=1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\sqrt{2} x+y+z=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\vec{n}$ be inclined at angles $\alpha, \beta, \gamma$ to $\mathrm{X}, \mathrm{Y}, \mathrm{Z}$ axes respectively.
$\begin{array}{ll} 
& \alpha=45^{\circ}, \beta=60^{\circ}, \gamma=? \\
& \cos ^2 \alpha+\cos ^2 \beta+\cos ^2 \gamma=1 \\
\therefore \quad & \frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{4}+\cos ^2 \gamma=1 \\
\therefore \quad & \cos ^2 \gamma=\frac{1}{4} \\
\therefore \quad & \gamma=60^{\circ} \\
& \overrightarrow{\mathrm{n}}=\cos \alpha \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\cos \beta \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\cos \gamma \hat{\mathrm{k}} \\
\therefore \quad & \overrightarrow{\mathrm{n}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}} \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\frac{1}{2} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{1}{2} \hat{\mathrm{k}}
\end{array}$
$\therefore \quad$ Equation of the required plane is
$\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(x+\sqrt{2})+\frac{1}{2}(y-1)+\frac{1}{2}(z-1)=0$
i.e., $\sqrt{2} x+y+z=0$

</details>

### Q96. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The foot of the perpendicular drawn from the origin to the plane is $(4,-2,5)$, then the Cartesian equation of the plane is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 4x-2y+5z=45
- **(B)** -4x+2y+5z=45
- **(C)** 4x-2y+5z+45=0
- **(D)** 4x+2y-5z+45=0

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 4x-2y+5z=45**

**Detailed Solution:**

Substitute $x=4, y=-2, \mathrm{z}=5$ in all options.
$\therefore \quad$ For option A
$\begin{aligned}
4 x-2 y+5 z & =4(4)-2(-2)+5(5) \\
& =16+4+25 \\
& =45
\end{aligned}$
$\therefore \quad$ Only option A is satisfied by $(4,-2,5)$

</details>

### Q97. MHT CET 2022 (11 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The line drawn $(4,-1,2)$ and $(-3,2,3)$ meets the plane at right angles at the point $(-10,5,4)$ then the equation of plane is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2 x-y-z+29=0$
- **(B)** $7 x-3 y-z+89=0$
- **(C)** $x-y+z+11=0$
- **(D)** $x+y+z+1=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $7 x-3 y-z+89=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

d.r's of normal to the plane are $ < 4-(-3),-1-2$, $2-3>^0 < 7,-3,-1>$
and it passes through $(-10,5,4)$. Hence the required equation is
$\begin{aligned}
& 7(x-(-10))-3(y-5)-1(z-4)=0 \\
& \Rightarrow 7 x-3 y-z+89=0
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q98. MHT CET 2022 (10 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\bar{n}$ be a vector of magnitude $3 \sqrt{3}$ such that it makes equal acute angles with the co-ordinate axes. Then the vactor equation of a plane passing through $(1,-1,2)$ and normal to $\bar{n}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\bar{r} \cdot(\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k})=3$
- **(B)** $\bar{r} \cdot(3 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})=12$
- **(C)** $\bar{r} \cdot(3 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})=1$
- **(D)** $\bar{r} \cdot(3 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})=6$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\bar{r} \cdot(3 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})=6$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \vec{r} \cdot \vec{n}=\vec{a} \cdot \vec{n} \\ & \Rightarrow \vec{r} \cdot(\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k})=(\hat{i}-\hat{j}+2 \widehat{k}) \cdot(\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}) \\ & \Rightarrow \vec{r} \cdot(\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k})=2 \\ & \Rightarrow \vec{r} \cdot(3 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})=6\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q99. MHT CET 2022 (10 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane through the intersection of the planes $x+y+z=1$ and $2 x+3 y-z+4=0$ and parallel to $x-a x i s$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3 y+z-6=0$
- **(B)** $3 y-z+6=0$
- **(C)** $y-3 z+6=0$
- **(D)** $y+3 z-6=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $y-3 z+6=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Required equation is $(x+y+z-1)+\lambda(2 x+3 y-z+4)=0$
$$
\Rightarrow(1+2 \lambda) x+(1+3 \lambda) y+(1-\lambda) z+(4 \lambda-1)=0
$$
But plane is parallel to the $\mathrm{x}$-axis
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \Rightarrow \lambda=-\frac{1}{2} \\
& \Rightarrow-\frac{1}{2} y+\frac{3}{2} z-3=0 \\
& \Rightarrow y-3 z+6=0
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q100. MHT CET 2022 (08 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The foot of the perpendicular drawn from the origin to the plane is $(4,-2,-5)$. Hence, the equation of the plane is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4 x-2 y+5 z=-5$
- **(B)** $4 x-2 y-5 z=45$
- **(C)** $4 x+2 y-5 z=37$
- **(D)** $4 x+2 y+5 z+13=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $4 x-2 y-5 z=45$**

**Detailed Solution:**

D.r's of normal to the plane $\langle 4-0,-2-0,-5-0\rangle \equiv\langle 4,-2,-5\rangle$
Hence equation of the plane $4 x-2 y-5 z=\lambda$
But it passes through $(4,-2,-5)$
$\begin{aligned}
& \Rightarrow 4 \times 4-2 \times(-2)-5 \times(-5)=\lambda \\
& \Rightarrow \lambda=45
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q101. MHT CET 2022 (08 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of the plane passing through the points $(1,2,3)$, $(-1,4,2)$ and $(3,1,1)$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5 x+6 y+2 z-23=0$
- **(B)** $5 x+y+2 z-13=0$
- **(C)** $5 x+y+12 z-43=0$
- **(D)** $5 x+y+12 z-43=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $5 x+6 y+2 z-23=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The required equation of plane
$\begin{aligned}
& \left|\begin{array}{ccc}
x-1 & y-2 & z-3 \\
-1-1 & 4-2 & 2-3 \\
3-1 & 1-2 & 1-3
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
& \Rightarrow\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
x-1 & y-2 & z-3 \\
-2 & 2 & -1 \\
2 & -1 & -2
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
& \Rightarrow-5(x-1)-6(y-2)-2(z-3)=0 \\
& \Rightarrow 5 x+6 y+2 z-23=0
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q102. MHT CET 2022 (08 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the planes $\bar{r} \cdot(2 \hat{i}-\lambda \hat{j}+\hat{k})=3$ and $\bar{r} \cdot(4 \hat{i}-\hat{j}+\mu \hat{k})=5$ are paralle, then values of $\lambda$ and $\mu$ are respectively

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{1}{2}, 2$
- **(B)** $\frac{-1}{2}, 2$
- **(C)** $\frac{1}{2},-2$
- **(D)** $\frac{-1}{2},-2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{1}{2}, 2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The two planes are parallel hence
$\begin{aligned}
& \frac{2}{4}=\frac{-\lambda}{-1}=\frac{1}{\mu} \\
& \Rightarrow \lambda=\frac{1}{2} \text { and } \mu=2
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q103. MHT CET 2022 (07 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The length of the perpendicular from the origin to the plane $\bar{r}$. $(3 \hat{i}-4 \hat{j}+12 \hat{k})=8$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 8 units
- **(B)** $\frac{13}{8}$
- **(C)** $\frac{8}{13}$
- **(D)** 13 units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{8}{13}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The required length of perpendicular $=\frac{8}{\sqrt{3^2+(-4)^2+12^2}}=\frac{8}{13}$

### Q104. MHT CET 2022 (06 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of the lines passing through the point $(3,2)$ and making an acute angle of $45^{\circ}$ with the lien $x-2 y-3=0$ are

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+2 y-7=0,2 x-y-4=0$
- **(B)** $3 x+y-11=0, x+3 y-9=0$
- **(C)** $3 x-y-7=0, x+3 y-9=0$
- **(D)** $3 x+y-11=0, x+3 y+9=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $3 x-y-7=0, x+3 y-9=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the slope of required line by $\mathrm{m}$ then $\tan 45^{\circ}=\left|\frac{m-\frac{1}{2}}{1+m \times \frac{1}{2}}\right|$
$\begin{aligned} & \Rightarrow \pm 1=\frac{m-\frac{1}{2}}{1+m \times \frac{1}{2}} \\ & \Rightarrow m=3 \text { or } m=-\frac{1}{3}\end{aligned}$
So, required equation
$\begin{aligned} & (y-2)=3(x-3) \text { or }(y-2)=\frac{-1}{3}(x-3) \\ & \Rightarrow 3 x-y-7=0 \text { or } x+3 y-9=0\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q105. MHT CET 2022 (06 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A tetrahedron has vertices $\mathrm{P}(1,2,1), \mathrm{Q}(2,1,3), \mathrm{R}(-1,1,2)$ and $\mathrm{O}(0,0,0)$. Then the angle between the faces OPQ and PQR is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{17}{35}\right)$
- **(B)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{19}{31}\right)$
- **(C)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{19}{35}\right)$
- **(D)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{17}{31}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{19}{35}\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of OPQ is $\left|\begin{array}{lll}x & y & z \\ 1 & 2 & 1 \\ 2 & 1 & 3\end{array}\right|=0$
$\Rightarrow 5 x-y-3 z=0$
equation of PQR is $\left|\begin{array}{ccc}x-1 & y-2 & z-1 \\ 2-1 & 1-2 & 3-1 \\ -1-1 & 1-2 & 2-1\end{array}\right|=0$
$\Rightarrow x-5 y-3 z+12=0$
Angle between the planes
$\begin{aligned} & \theta=\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{5 \times 1+(-1) \times(-5)+(-3) \times(-3)}{\sqrt{5^2+(-1)^2+(-3)^2} \cdot \sqrt{1^2+(-5)^2+(-3)^2}}\right) \\ & \Rightarrow \theta=\cos ^{-1} \frac{19}{\sqrt{35} \cdot \sqrt{35}}=\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{19}{35}\right)\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q107. MHT CET 2022 (06 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The cartesian equation of a line passing through $(1,2,3)$ and parallel to planes $x-y+2 z=5$ and $3 x+y+z=6$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{x-1}{-3}=\frac{y-2}{-5}=\frac{z-3}{4}$
- **(B)** $\frac{x-1}{-3}=\frac{y-2}{5}=\frac{z-3}{4}$
- **(C)** $\frac{x-1}{13}=\frac{y-2}{-1}=\frac{z-3}{1}$
- **(D)** $\frac{x-1}{3}=\frac{y-2}{1}=\frac{z-3}{1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{x-1}{-3}=\frac{y-2}{5}=\frac{z-3}{4}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Required equation
$\begin{aligned} & \frac{x-1}{(-1) \times 1-1 \times 2}=\frac{y-2}{3 \times 2-1 \times 1}=\frac{z-3}{1 \times 1-3 \times(-1)} \\ & \Rightarrow \frac{x-1}{-3}=\frac{y-2}{5}=\frac{z-3}{4}\end{aligned}$

</details>

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane passing through the point $(2,2,1)$ and the intersection of the planes $x+2 y-3 z+1=0$ and $3 x-2 y+4 z+3=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3 x+26 y+43 z+3=0$
- **(B)** $3 x+26 y-43 z-3=0$
- **(C)** $3 x-26 y-43 z-3=0$
- **(D)** $3 x-26 y+43 z+3=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $3 x-26 y+43 z+3=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/8eHCnXuqIVR58992_ND6tqqNmQZhtKa9NYs9OfNRl3A.original.fullsize.png">
But it passes through $(2,2,1)$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \Rightarrow(2+2 \times 2-3 \times 1+1)+\lambda(3 \times 2-2 \times 2+4 \times 1+3)=0 \\
& \Rightarrow 4+9 \lambda=0 \\

### Q109. MHT CET 2022 (06 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The co-ordinates of the foot of the perpendicular drawn form the origin to the plane $3 x+2 y+6 z=56$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left(\frac{48}{7}, \frac{24}{7}, \frac{16}{7}\right)$
- **(B)** $\left(\frac{24}{7}, \frac{48}{7}, \frac{16}{7}\right)$
- **(C)** $\left(\frac{16}{7}, \frac{24}{7}, \frac{48}{7}\right)$
- **(D)** $\left(\frac{24}{7}, \frac{16}{7}, \frac{48}{7}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\left(\frac{24}{7}, \frac{16}{7}, \frac{48}{7}\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \frac{x-0}{3}=\frac{y-0}{2}=\frac{z-0}{6}=\frac{-(3 \times 0+2 \times 0+1 \times 0-56)}{3^2+2^2+6^2} \\ & \Rightarrow \frac{x}{3}=\frac{y}{2}=\frac{z}{6}=\frac{56}{49} \Rightarrow x=\frac{24}{7}, y=\frac{16}{7}, \mathrm{z}=\frac{48}{7} \\ & \Rightarrow\left(\frac{24}{7}, \frac{16}{7}, \frac{48}{7}\right)\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q110. MHT CET 2022 (05 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane through the intersection of the planes $\mathrm{x}+\mathrm{y}+\mathrm{z}=1$ and $2 \mathrm{x}+3 \mathrm{y}-\mathrm{x}+4=0$ and parallel to $\mathrm{X}$-axis is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $y+3 z+6=0$
- **(B)** $3 y-z+6=0$
- **(C)** $y-3 z+6=0$
- **(D)** $3 y-2 z+6=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $y-3 z+6=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & (x+y+z-1)+\lambda(2 x+3 y-z+4)=0 \\ & \Rightarrow(1+2 \lambda) x+(1+3 \lambda) y+(1-\lambda) z+(4 \lambda-1)=0\end{aligned}$
To be parallel to the $x$-axis $1+2 \lambda=0 \Rightarrow \lambda=\frac{-1}{2}$
$\Rightarrow-\frac{1}{2} y+\frac{3}{2} z-3=0 \Rightarrow y-3 z+6=0$

</details>

### Q111. MHT CET 2022 (05 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The co-ordinates of the foot of the perpendicular drawn from the origin to the plane $2 x+6 y-3 z=63$ are

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(4,2,-4)$
- **(B)** $\left(\frac{18}{7}, \frac{54}{7}, \frac{-27}{7}\right)$
- **(C)** $\left(\frac{2}{7}, \frac{6}{7}, \frac{-3}{7}\right)$
- **(D)** $\left(\frac{9}{7}, \frac{6}{7}, \frac{-3}{7}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\left(\frac{18}{7}, \frac{54}{7}, \frac{-27}{7}\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The co-ordinates of foot of perpendicular can be obtained by
$\begin{aligned} & \frac{x-x_1}{a}=\frac{y-y_1}{b}=\frac{z-z_1}{c}=\frac{-\left(a_1+b y_1+c_1-d\right)}{a^2+b^2+c^2} \\ & \Rightarrow \frac{x-0}{2}=\frac{y-0}{6}=\frac{z-0}{-3}=\frac{-(2 \times 0+6 \times 0-3 \times 0-63)}{2^2+6^2+(-3)^2} \\ & \Rightarrow \frac{x}{2}=\frac{y}{6}=\frac{z}{-3}=\frac{63}{49} \\ & \Rightarrow x=\frac{18}{7}, y=\frac{54}{7}, z=\frac{-27}{7} \\ & \Rightarrow\left(\frac{18}{7}, \frac{54}{7}, \frac{-27}{7}\right)\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q112. MHT CET 2022 (05 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of a plane containing the line of intersection of the planes $2 \mathrm{x}-\mathrm{y}-4=0$ and $\mathrm{y}+2 \mathrm{z}-4=0$ and passing through the point $(1,1,0)$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x-y-z=0$
- **(B)** $2 x-z=2$
- **(C)** $x-3 y-2 z=-2$
- **(D)** $x+3 y+z=4$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $x-y-z=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of plane containing the line of intersection of the planes
$2 x-y-4=0$ and $y+2 z-4=0$ is
$(2 x-y-4)+\lambda(y+2 z-4)=0$
But it passes through $(1,1,0)$
$\Rightarrow \lambda=-1$
$\Rightarrow$ The required equation of plane is
$\begin{aligned} & (2 x-y-4)-(y+2 z-4)=0 \\ & \Rightarrow 2 x-2 y-2 z=0 \\ & \Rightarrow x-y-z=0\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q113. MHT CET 2021 (24 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of the plane which passes through $(2,-3,1)$ and is normal to the line joining the points $(3,4,-1)$ and $(2,-1,5)$ is given by

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+5 y-6 z+19=0$
- **(B)** $x-5 y+6 z-23=0$
- **(C)** $x+5 y+6 z+7=0$
- **(D)** $x-5 y-6 z-11=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $x+5 y-6 z+19=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The required plane passes through $(2,-3,1)$.
It is normal to the line having d.r.s. $(1,5,-6)$.
$\therefore \mathrm{x}+5 \mathrm{y}-6 \mathrm{z}=\mathrm{k} \Rightarrow 2+5(-3)-6(1)=1$ i.e. $\mathrm{k}=-19$
Hence equation of plane is $x+5 y-6 z+19=0$

</details>

---

### Q114. MHT CET 2021 (24 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the vector equation or the plane $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})+\lambda \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\mu(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$ in scalar product form is given by $\bar{r} \cdot(3 \hat{i}+2 \hat{k})=\alpha$ then $\alpha=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2
- **(B)** 3
- **(C)** 1
- **(D)** 0

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 2**

**Detailed Solution:**

Here given plane passes through the point $(2,0,1)$ and let $\overline{\mathrm{b}}=\hat{\mathrm{i}}$ and $\bar{c}=\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}-3 \hat{k}$.
Normal vector $\overline{\mathrm{n}}=\overline{\mathrm{b}} \times \overline{\mathrm{c}}=\left|\begin{array}{ccc}\hat{\mathrm{i}} & \hat{\mathrm{j}} & \hat{\mathrm{k}} \\ 1 & 0 & 0 \\ 1 & 2 & -3\end{array}\right|=3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
The equation of plane in scalar product from is $\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{n}}=\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{n}}$
Here $\bar{a} \cdot \bar{n}=(2 \hat{i}+\hat{k}) \cdot(3 \hat{j}+2 \hat{k})=2$
$\therefore \alpha=2$

</details>

---

### Q115. MHT CET 2021 (24 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The Cartesian equation of a plane which passes through the points $\mathrm{A}(2,2,2)$ and making equal nonzero intercepts on the co-ordinate axes is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+y+z=6$
- **(B)** $x-2 y+z=0$
- **(C)** $2 x+y+z=7$
- **(D)** $x-y+z=6$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $x+y+z=6$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $a, b, c$ be the intercepts on the coordinate axes and we have $a=b=c$.
$\therefore \quad \frac{\mathrm{x}}{\mathrm{a}}+\frac{\mathrm{y}}{\mathrm{a}}+\frac{\mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{a}}=1$ is required equation of plane.
Since plane passes through point $(2,2,2)$, we write $\frac{2+2+2}{\mathrm{a}}=1 \Rightarrow \mathrm{a}=6$
$\therefore \quad$ Equation of plane is $\mathrm{x}+\mathrm{y}+\mathrm{z}=6$

</details>

---

### Q116. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Equation of planes parallel to the plane $x-2 y+2 z+4=0$ which are at a distance of one unit from the point $(1,2,3)$ are

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+2 y+2 z=6, x+2 y+2 z=0$
- **(B)** $x-2 y+2 z=0, x-2 y+2 z-6=0$
- **(C)** $x-2 y-6=0, x-2 y+z=6$
- **(D)** $x+2 y+2 z=-6, x+2 y+2 z=5$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $x-2 y+2 z=0, x-2 y+2 z-6=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of planes parallel to the plane $x-2 y+2 z+4=0$ is
$$
\mathrm{x}-2 \mathrm{y}+2 \mathrm{z}+\lambda=0
$$
The required planes are at a distance of one unit from $(1,2,3)$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \left|\frac{\mathrm{ax}_1+\mathrm{by}_1+\mathrm{cz} z_1+\mathrm{d}}{\sqrt{\mathrm{a}^2+\mathrm{b}^2+\mathrm{c}^2}}\right|=1 \\
& \left|\frac{1(1)+(-2)(2)+2(3)+\lambda}{\sqrt{1+4+4}}\right|=1 \Rightarrow\left|\frac{1-4+6+\lambda}{3}\right|=1 \Rightarrow 3+\lambda= \pm 3 \\
& \therefore 3+\lambda=3 \quad \text { or } \quad 3+\lambda=-3 \\
& \lambda=0 \quad \text { or } \quad \lambda=-6
\end{aligned}
$$
The equation of planes are $x-2 y+2 z=0$ and $x-2 y+2 z-6=0$

</details>

---

### Q117. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The coordinates of the foot of the perpendicular drawn from the origin to the plane $2 x+y-2 z=18$ are

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(4,2,-4)$
- **(B)** $(1,2,-3)$
- **(C)** $(4,2,4)$
- **(D)** $(4,-2,-4)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $(4,2,-4)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
2 x+y-2 z=18
$$
Dividing both sides $\sqrt{(2)^2+(1)^2+(-2)^2}=3$, we get
$$
\left(\frac{2}{3}\right) \mathrm{x}+\left(\frac{1}{3}\right) \mathrm{y}-\left(\frac{2}{3}\right) \mathrm{z}=6
$$
Hence length of perpendicular from origin to the plane is 6 . Therefore coordinates of foot of perpendicular are $\left[\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)(6),\left(\frac{1}{3}\right)(6),\left(\frac{-2}{3}\right)(6)\right]$ i.e. $(4,2,-4)$

</details>

### Q118. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Equation of the plane passing through the point $(1,2,3)$ and parallel to the plane $2 x+3 y-4 z=0$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2 x+3 y+4 z-8=0$
- **(B)** $2 x+3 y-4 z+4=0$
- **(C)** $2 x+3 y+4 z+4=0$
- **(D)** $2 x+3 y+4 z=20$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $2 x+3 y-4 z+4=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Required plane passes through $(1,2,3)$ and is parallel to plane $2 x+3 y-4 z=0$. Hence equation of required plane is
$$
2(x-1)+3(y-2)-4(z-3)=0 \Rightarrow 2 x+3 y-4 z+4=0
$$

</details>
