### Q119. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $A$ and $B$ are the foot of the perpendicular drawn from the point $\mathrm{Q}(\mathrm{a}, \mathrm{b}, \mathrm{c})$ to the planes $\mathrm{YZ}$ and $\mathrm{ZX}$ respectively, then the equation of the plane through the points $\mathrm{A}, \mathrm{B}$, and $\mathrm{O}$ is (where $\mathrm{O}$ is the origin)

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{x}{a}-\frac{y}{b}-\frac{z}{c}=0$
- **(B)** $\frac{x}{a}+\frac{y}{b}-\frac{z}{c}=0$
- **(C)** $\frac{x}{a}-\frac{y}{b}+\frac{z}{c}=0$
- **(D)** $\frac{x}{a}+\frac{y}{b}+\frac{z}{c}=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{x}{a}+\frac{y}{b}-\frac{z}{c}=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

From given data, we write $\mathrm{A} \equiv(0, \mathrm{~b}, \mathrm{c})$ and $\mathrm{B} \equiv(\mathrm{a}, 0, \mathrm{c})$ Equation of plane passing through $\mathrm{A}, \mathrm{B}$ and $\mathrm{O}$ is
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \left|\begin{array}{lll}
x-0 & y-0 & z-0 \\
0-0 & b-0 & c-0 \\
a-0 & 0-0 & c-0
\end{array}\right|=0 \Rightarrow\left|\begin{array}{lll}
x & y & z \\
0 & b & c \\
a & 0 & c
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
& \therefore x(b c)-y(-a c)+z(-a b)=0 \Rightarrow b c x+a c y-a b z=0
\end{aligned}
$$
Dividing both sides by abc, we get $\frac{\mathrm{x}}{\mathrm{a}}+\frac{\mathrm{y}}{\mathrm{b}}-\frac{\mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{c}}=0$

</details>

---

### Q120. MHT CET 2021 (22 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If a plane meets the axes $\mathrm{X}, \mathrm{Y}, \mathrm{Z}$ in $\mathrm{A}, \mathrm{B}, \mathrm{C}$ respectively such that centroid of $\triangle \mathrm{ABC}$ is $(1,2,3)$, then the equation of the plane is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+2 y+3 z=1$
- **(B)** $x+\frac{y}{2}+\frac{z}{3}=3$
- **(C)** $\frac{x}{3}+\frac{y}{6}+\frac{z}{9}=1$
- **(D)** $\frac{x}{4}+\frac{y}{8}+\frac{z}{12}=1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{x}{3}+\frac{y}{6}+\frac{z}{9}=1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $A=(x, 0,0) ; B=(0, y, 0) ; C=(0,0, z)$ Centroid of $\triangle A B C$ is $(1,2,3)$
$$
\therefore \frac{\mathrm{x}}{3}=1, \frac{\mathrm{y}}{3}=2, \frac{\mathrm{z}}{3}=3 \Rightarrow(\mathrm{x}, \mathrm{y}, \mathrm{z})=(3,6,9)
$$
Hence equation of required plane is
$$
\frac{x}{3}+\frac{y}{6}+\frac{z}{9}=1
$$

</details>

### Q121. MHT CET 2021 (21 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Equation of the plane passing through the point $(2,0,5)$ and parallel to the vectors $\hat{i}-\hat{j}+\hat{k}$ and $3 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+\hat{k}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x-4 y-z+3=0$
- **(B)** $x+4 y+5 z-27=0$
- **(C)** $x-4 y-5 z+23=0$
- **(D)** $x-4 y+z-7=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $x-4 y-5 z+23=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Normal to the plane is perpendicular to the given vectors.
Hence equation of normal is
$$
\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
\hat{\mathrm{i}} & \hat{\mathrm{j}} & \hat{\mathrm{k}} \\
1 & -1 & 1 \\
3 & 2 & -1
\end{array}\right|=\hat{\mathrm{i}}(-1)-\hat{\mathrm{j}}(-4)+\hat{\mathrm{k}}(5)=-\hat{\mathrm{i}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{k}}
$$
Hence equation of required plane is
$$
\begin{aligned}
& (-1)(\mathrm{x}-2)+(4)(\mathrm{y}-0)+(5)(\mathrm{z}-5)=0 \\
& \therefore-\mathrm{x}+2+4 \mathrm{y}+5 \mathrm{z}-25=0 \Rightarrow \mathrm{x}-4 \mathrm{y}-5 \mathrm{z}+23=0
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q122. MHT CET 2021 (21 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane containing the line $\frac{x+1}{-3}=\frac{y-3}{2}=\frac{z+2}{1}$ and the point $(0,7,-7)$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2 x+y+z=0$
- **(B)** $x+y+z=0$
- **(C)** $x+2 y-3 z=35$
- **(D)** $x+3 y+z=14$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $x+y+z=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $a, b, c$ be the direction cosines of the required plane.
It contains the line $\frac{x+1}{-3}=\frac{y-3}{2}=\frac{z+2}{1}$ and passes through the point $(0,7,-7)$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \therefore \mathrm{a}(\mathrm{x}+1)+\mathrm{b}(\mathrm{y}-3)+\mathrm{c}(\mathrm{z}+2)=0 \quad \ldots(1) \\
& \therefore \mathrm{a}(0+1)+\mathrm{b}(7-3)+\mathrm{c}(-7+2)=0 \Rightarrow \mathrm{a}+4 \mathrm{~b}-5 \mathrm{c}=0 .
\end{aligned}
$$
$$
\text { Also }-3 \mathrm{a}+2 \mathrm{~b}+\mathrm{c}=0
$$
From (2) and (3), we write
$$
\frac{\mathrm{a}}{\left|\begin{array}{cc}
4 & -5 \\
2 & 1
\end{array}\right|}=\frac{-b}{\left|\begin{array}{cc}
1 & -5 \\
-3 & 1
\end{array}\right|}=\frac{\mathrm{c}}{\left|\begin{array}{cc}
1 & 4 \\
-3 & 2
\end{array}\right|}
$$
$$
\therefore \frac{\mathrm{a}}{14}=\frac{\mathrm{b}}{14}=\frac{\mathrm{c}}{14} \Rightarrow \mathrm{a}=\mathrm{b}=\mathrm{c}=1
$$
Hence eq. (1) becomes
$$
x+1+y-3+z+2=0 \Rightarrow x+y+z=0
$$

</details>

---

### Q123. MHT CET 2021 (21 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An urn contains 9 balls of which 3 are red, 4 are blue and 2 are green. Three balls are drawn at random from the urn. The probability that the three balls have difference colours is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{1}{14}$
- **(B)** $\frac{3}{14}$
- **(C)** $\frac{1}{7}$
- **(D)** $\frac{2}{7}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{2}{7}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \text { Required probability } & =\frac{{ }^3 \mathrm{C}_1 \times{ }^4 \mathrm{C}_1 \times{ }^2 \mathrm{C}_1}{{ }^9 \mathrm{C}_3} \\ & =\frac{3 \times 4 \times 2}{\left(\frac{9 !}{3 ! 6 !}\right)}=\frac{24 \times 6}{9 \times 8 \times 7}=\frac{2}{7}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q124. MHT CET 2021 (21 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane passing through $(-2,2,2)$ and $(2,-2,-2)$ and perpendicular to the plane $9 x-13 y-3 z=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5 x-3 y+2 z=12$
- **(B)** $5 x+3 y+2 z=0$
- **(C)** $5 x+3 y-2 z+8=0$
- **(D)** $5 x-3 y+2 z+12=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $5 x+3 y+2 z=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of plane passing through $(-2,2,2)$ is $a(x+2)+b$
$$
(\mathrm{y}-2)+\mathrm{c}(\mathrm{z}-2)=0
$$
Since this plane also passes through $(2,-2,-2)$, we get
$$
4 \mathrm{a}-4 \mathrm{~b}-4 \mathrm{c}=0 \Rightarrow \mathrm{a}-\mathrm{b}-\mathrm{c}=0
$$
Normal of the plane is parallel to
$$
9 x-13 y-3 z=0 \Rightarrow 9 a-13 b-3 c=0
$$
Solving (1) and (2), we write


$$
\frac{\mathrm{a}}{\left|\begin{array}{cc}
-1 & -1 \\
-13 & -3
\end{array}\right|}=\frac{\mathrm{b}}{\left|\begin{array}{cc}
1 & -1 \\
0 & -3
\end{array}\right|}=\frac{\mathrm{c}}{\left|\begin{array}{cc}
1 & -1 \\
9 & -13
\end{array}\right|}
$$

$$
\therefore \frac{\mathrm{a}}{-10}=\frac{-\mathrm{b}}{6}=\frac{\mathrm{c}}{-4} \Rightarrow \frac{\mathrm{a}}{5}=\frac{\mathrm{b}}{3}=\frac{\mathrm{c}}{2}
$$
Hence equation of required plane is
$$
5(x+2)+3(y-2)+2(z-2)=0 \text { i.e. } 5 x+3 y+2 z=0
$$

</details>

### Q125. MHT CET 2021 (21 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The Cartesian equation of the plane passing through the point A $(7,8,6)$ and parallel to the XY plane is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $z=1$
- **(B)** $y=8$
- **(C)** $x=7$
- **(D)** $z=6$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $z=6$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The required plane passes through the point $(7,8,6)$ and is parallel to XY plane.
$\therefore$ It is perpendicular to $\mathrm{z}$ axis and direction ratios of $\mathrm{z}$ axis are $0,0,1$.
$\therefore$ Required equation of plane is
$$
0(\mathrm{x}-7)+0(\mathrm{y}-8)+1(\mathrm{z}-6)=0 \Rightarrow \mathrm{z}=6
$$

</details>

### Q126. MHT CET 2021 (20 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of the plane that contains the line of intersection of the planes. $x+2 y+3 z-4=0$ and $2 x+y+5=0$ and is perpendicular to the plane $5 x+3 y-6 z+8=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $14 x+7 y-7 z-4=0$
- **(B)** $33 x+45 y+50 z-41=0$
- **(C)** $-33 \mathrm{x}+45-50 \mathrm{z}+41=0$
- **(D)** $5 x+31 y+50 z-41=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $33 x+45 y+50 z-41=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the required plane is $(x+2 y+3 z-4)+\lambda$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& (2 x+y-z+5)=0 \text { i.e. } \\
& (1+2 \lambda) x+(2+\lambda) y+(3-\lambda) z+(-4+5 \lambda)=0
\end{aligned}
$$
Since (1) is perpendicular to the plane $5 x+3 y-6 z+8=0$, we write
$$
\begin{aligned}
& (1+2 \lambda)(5)+(2+\lambda)(3)+(3-\lambda)(-6)=0 \\
& \therefore 5+10 \lambda+6+3 \lambda-18+6 \lambda=0 \\
& 19 \lambda=7 \\
& \Rightarrow \lambda=\frac{7}{19}
\end{aligned}
$$
Substituting value of $\lambda$ in eq. (1), we get
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \left(1+\frac{14}{19}\right) \mathrm{x}+\left(2+\frac{7}{19}\right) \mathrm{y}+\left(3-\frac{7}{19}\right) \mathrm{z}+\left(-4+\frac{35}{19}\right)=0 \\
& \therefore 33 \mathrm{x}+45 \mathrm{y}+50 \mathrm{z}-41=0
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q127. MHT CET 2020 (20 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The unit vector perpendicular to the plane $4 x-3 y+12 z=15$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{4 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}+12 \hat{k}}{13}$
- **(B)** $\frac{4 \hat{\imath}-3 \hat{\jmath}+12 \hat{k}}{13}$
- **(C)** $\frac{-4 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}+12 \hat{k}}{13}$
- **(D)** $\frac{-4 \hat{\imath}-3 \hat{\jmath}+12 \hat{k}}{13}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{4 \hat{\imath}-3 \hat{\jmath}+12 \hat{k}}{13}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The unit vector perpendicular to plane $4 x-3 y+12 z=15$ is $\frac{4 \hat{i}-3 \hat{j}+12 \hat{k}}{\sqrt{16+9+144}}=\frac{4 \hat{i}-3 \hat{j}+12 \hat{k}}{13}$

</details>

### Q128. MHT CET 2020 (20 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of a plane containing the line $x-2=\frac{y-4}{4}=\frac{z-6}{7}$ and parallel to the
line $\bar{r}=(\hat{i}+3 \hat{\jmath}+5 \hat{k})+\lambda(3 \hat{\imath}+5 \hat{\jmath}+7 \hat{k})$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x-2 y+z=10$
- **(B)** $3 x-2 y+z=4$
- **(C)** $x-2 y+z=9$
- **(D)** $x-2 y+z=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $x-2 y+z=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

d.r. of given liens are $(1,4,7)$ and $(3,5,7)$. Normal to the plane is perpendicular to them.
$$
\begin{array}{l}
=\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
\hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\
1 & 4 & 7 \\
3 & 5 & 7
\end{array}\right| \\
=\hat{i}(-7)-\hat{j}(-14)+\hat{k}(-7) \\
=-7(\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+\hat{k})
\end{array}
$$
Hence eq. of plane is $-7(x-2 y+z)=a$
This plane plasses through point $(2,4,6)$
$\therefore \quad a=-7(2-8+6)=0$
$\therefore$ Eq. of plane is $x-2 y+z=0$

</details>

### Q129. MHT CET 2020 (20 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the origin and the points $(1,2,3),(2,3,4)$ and $(x, y, z)$ are coplanar, then

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x-2 y+z=0$
- **(B)** $x+y+z=6$
- **(C)** $x-2 y+z+1=0$
- **(D)** $z-2 x+y=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $x-2 y+z=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For give coplanar points, we write
$$
\begin{aligned}
&\left|\begin{array}{lll}
1 & 2 & 3 \\
2 & 3 & 4 \\
x & y & z
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
\therefore & x(8-9)-y(4-6)+z(3-4)=0 \\
\therefore &-x+2 y-z=0 \Rightarrow x-2 y+z=0
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q130. MHT CET 2020 (20 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of a plane containing the point $(1,-1,1)$ and parallel to the plane $2 x+3 y-4 z=17$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(2 \hat{\imath}-3 \hat{\jmath}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}})=-1$
- **(B)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(\hat{\imath}-\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})=3$
- **(C)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(2 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}})=-5$
- **(D)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(2 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}})=5$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(2 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}})=-5$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Here $\bar{a}=\hat{i}-\hat{j}+\hat{k}$
$\& \quad \bar{n}=2 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}-4 \hat{k}$
Now $\bar{a} \cdot \bar{n} \quad=2-3-4=-5$
Vector equation of plane passing through $\mathrm{A}(\bar{a})$ is
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{n}}=\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{n}} \\
\Rightarrow & \overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}})=-5
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q131. MHT CET 2020 (19 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equations of planes parallel to the plane $x+2 y+2 z+8=0$, which are at a
distance of 2 units from the point $(1,1,2)$ are

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+2 y+2 z-13=0$ or $x+2 y+2 z-1=0$
- **(B)** $x+2 y+2 z-6=0 \quad$ or $\quad x+2 y+2 z-7=0$
- **(C)** $x+2 y+2 z+3=0 \quad$ or $\quad x+2 y+2 z-5=0$
- **(D)** $x+2 y+2 z-5=0 \quad$ or $\quad x+2 y+2 z-3=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $x+2 y+2 z-13=0$ or $x+2 y+2 z-1=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(B)
The equation of the plane parallel to the plane $x+2 y+2 z+18=0$ is $x+2 y+2 z+\lambda=0$. Now, the distance of this plane from the print $(1,1,2)$ is
$\therefore\left|\frac{1(1)+2(1)+2(2)+\lambda}{\sqrt{1^{2}+2^{2}+2^{2}}}\right|=\left|\frac{7+\lambda}{3}\right|$
$\begin{array}{l}
\text { We have }\left|\frac{7+\lambda}{3}\right|=2 \Rightarrow \frac{7+\lambda}{3}=\pm 2 \\
\therefore \lambda=\pm 6-7=1 \Rightarrow \lambda=-1 \text { or } \lambda=-13
\end{array}$
Hence, equations of plane are $x+2 y+2 z-1=0$ or $x+2 y+2 z-13=0$

</details>

### Q132. MHT CET 2020 (19 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of a plane containing the point $(1,-1,2)$ and perpendicular to the
planes $2 x+3 y-2 z=5$ and $x+2 y-3 z=8$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\bar{r} \cdot(5 \hat{\imath}-4 \hat{\jmath}-\hat{k})=7$
- **(B)** $\bar{r} \cdot(5 \hat{\imath}+4 \hat{\jmath}+2 \hat{k})=5$
- **(C)** $\bar{r} \cdot(4 \hat{\imath}-5 \hat{\jmath}+3 \hat{k})=15$
- **(D)** $\bar{r} \cdot(5 \hat{\imath}+4 \hat{\jmath}-\hat{k})=5$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\bar{r} \cdot(5 \hat{\imath}-4 \hat{\jmath}-\hat{k})=7$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(A)
The equation of a plane passing through $(1,-1,2)$ is $a(x-1)+b(y+1)+c(z-2)=0$
If is perpendicular to the planes $2 x+3 y-2 z=5$ and $x+2 y-3 z=8$.
$\therefore 2 \mathrm{a}+3 \mathrm{~b}-2 \mathrm{c}=0$ and $\mathrm{a}+2 \mathrm{~b}-3 \mathrm{c}=0$
Solving the above equation, we get,
$\begin{array}{l}
\frac{a}{\left|\begin{array}{cc}
3 & -2 \\
2 & -3
\end{array}\right|}=\frac{-b}{\left|\begin{array}{ll}
2 & -2 \\
1 & -3
\end{array}\right|}=\frac{c}{\left|\begin{array}{ll}
2 & 3 \\
1 & 2
\end{array}\right|} \\
\frac{a}{-5}=\frac{b}{4}=\frac{c}{1}
\end{array}$
Substituting $\mathrm{a}=-5, \mathrm{~b}=4$ and $\mathrm{c}=1$, we get, $-5 x+4 y+z=-7 \Rightarrow 5 x-4 y-z=7$
$\therefore$ Required equation can be written as $\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(5 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}})=7$

</details>

### Q133. MHT CET 2020 (19 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The distance of a point $(1,2,-1)$ from the plane $x-2 y+4 z+10=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{3}{\sqrt{7}}$ units
- **(B)** $\frac{\sqrt{3}}{7}$ units
- **(C)** $\sqrt{\frac{7}{3}}$ units
- **(D)** $\sqrt{\frac{3}{7}}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\sqrt{\frac{3}{7}}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

(D)
$\begin{aligned} \text { Required distance } &=\frac{|1(1)+-2(2)+4(-1)+10|}{\sqrt{1^{2}+(-2)^{2}+(4)^{2}}} \\ &=\frac{3}{\sqrt{21}}=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{\sqrt{7}} \text { units } \end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q134. MHT CET 2020 (19 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of the line passing through the point $(1,2,3)$ and perpendicular to the
lines $\frac{x-1}{1}=\frac{y-2}{2}=\frac{z-3}{3}$ and $\bar{r}=\lambda(-3 \hat{\imath}+2 \hat{\jmath}+5 \hat{k})$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\bar{r}=(\hat{\imath}+2 \hat{\jmath}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(2 \hat{\imath}+7 \hat{\jmath}-4 \hat{k})$
- **(B)** $\bar{r}=(\hat{\imath}+2 \hat{\jmath}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(2 \hat{\imath}+7 \hat{\jmath}+4 \hat{k})$
- **(C)** $\bar{r}=(\hat{\imath}+2 \hat{\jmath}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(2 \hat{\imath}-7 \hat{\jmath}-4 \hat{k})$
- **(D)** $\bar{r}=(\hat{\imath}+2 \hat{\jmath}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(2 \hat{\imath}-7 \hat{\jmath}+4 \hat{k})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\bar{r}=(\hat{\imath}+2 \hat{\jmath}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(2 \hat{\imath}-7 \hat{\jmath}+4 \hat{k})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(D)
The vector perpendicular to both the given lines is given by $\left|\begin{array}{lll}\hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & 2 & 4 \\ 2 & 2 & 1\end{array}\right|=\hat{i}(-6)-\hat{j}(-7)+\hat{k}(-2)=-6 \hat{i}+7 \hat{j}-2 \hat{k}$
Hence d.r.s. of required line are $6,-7,2$.
Thus eq. of required line is
$\frac{x-1}{6}=\frac{y-2}{-7}=\frac{2-3}{2} \text { i.e. } \frac{x-1}{6}=\frac{2-y}{7}=\frac{z-3}{2}$

</details>

### Q135. MHT CET 2020 (16 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the plane $2 x+3 y+5 z=1$ intersects the co-ordinate axes at the points $A, B, C$, then the centroid of $\triangle \mathrm{ABC}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left(\frac{3}{2}, 1, \frac{3}{5}\right)$
- **(B)** $\left(\frac{1}{2}, \frac{1}{3}, \frac{1}{5}\right)$
- **(C)** $\left(\frac{1}{6}, \frac{1}{9}, \frac{1}{15}\right)$
- **(D)** $(2,3,5)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\left(\frac{1}{6}, \frac{1}{9}, \frac{1}{15}\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(D)
Given equation of plane can be rewritten as
$\frac{\mathrm{x}}{\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)}+\frac{\mathrm{y}}{\left(\frac{1}{3}\right)}+\frac{\mathrm{z}}{\left(\frac{1}{5}\right)}=1$ i.e. intercepts on $\mathrm{X}, \mathrm{Y}, \mathrm{Z}$ axis are $\frac{1}{2}, \frac{1}{3}, \frac{1}{5}$ respectively. $\mathrm{A}=\left(\frac{1}{2}, 0,0\right), \mathrm{B}=\left(0, \frac{1}{3}, 0\right), \mathrm{C}=\left(0,0, \frac{1}{5}\right)$ Thus centroid of $\triangle \mathrm{ABC}=\left(\frac{\frac{1}{2}+0+0}{3}, \frac{0+\frac{1}{3}+0}{3}, \frac{0+0+\frac{1}{5}}{3}\right)=\left(\frac{1}{6}, \frac{1}{9}, \frac{1}{15}\right)$

</details>

### Q136. MHT CET 2020 (16 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the foot of perpendicular drawn from the origin to the plane is $(3,2,1)$, then the equation of plane is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3x+2y-z=12$
- **(B)** $3x+2y-z=14$
- **(C)** $3x+2y+z=14$
- **(D)** $3x-2y-z=12$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $3x+2y+z=14$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(B)
Let $P(3,2,1)$ be the foot of the perpendicular from the origin to the plane.
$\begin{aligned}
\overline{\mathrm{OP}} &=\overline{\mathrm{n}}=3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}} \text { and } \\
\mathrm{P} &=|3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}|=\sqrt{3^{2}+2^{2}+1^{2}}=\sqrt{14} \\
\hat{\mathrm{n}} &=\frac{3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}}{\sqrt{14}}
\end{aligned}$
The vector equation of the plane is
$\begin{aligned}
&(x \hat{i}+y \hat{j}+z \hat{k}) \cdot \frac{(3 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+\hat{k})}{\sqrt{14}}=\sqrt{14} \\
\therefore & 3 x+2 y+z=14 \Rightarrow 3 x+2 y+z-14=0
\end{aligned}$

</details>

The distance of the point $(2,-1,0)$ from the plane $2 x+\mathrm{y}+2 \mathrm{z}+8=0$ is

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{11}{3}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{d}=\left|\frac{2(2)+(-1)(1)+0+8}{\sqrt{4+1+4}}\right|=\frac{11}{3}$ units

</details>

### Q138. MHT CET 2020 (15 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of a plane containing the lines $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(\hat{\imath}+2 \hat{\jmath}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}})+\lambda(2 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}+6 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$ and
$\overline{\mathbf{r}}=(\hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}})+\mu(\hat{\imath}+\hat{\jmath}-\hat{\mathrm{k}})$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $9 x+8 y+z+11=0$
- **(B)** $9 x-8 y-z-11=0$
- **(C)** $9 x-8 y-z+11=0$
- **(D)** $9 x-8 y+z+11=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $9 x-8 y+z+11=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Normal vector of a plane would be perpendicular to both the given lines and parallel to their cross product.
Now $\bar{\ell}_{1} \times \bar{\ell}_{2}=\left|\begin{array}{ccc}\hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & 3 & 6 \\ 1 & 1 & -1\end{array}\right|$
$=-9 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+8 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}}$ i.e. $\Rightarrow 9,-8,1$ are d.r. of normal to a plane
Let $\bar{a}=\hat{i}+3 \hat{j}+4 \hat{k}$ and $\bar{n}=9 \hat{i}-8 \hat{j}+\hat{k}$
$\therefore \overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(9 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-8 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}) \quad=9(1)+(-8)(3)+4 \times 1=9-24+4$
$\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(9 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-8 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}) \quad=-11 \Rightarrow 9 \mathrm{x}-8 \mathrm{y}+\mathrm{z}+11=0$ is the equation of plane.

</details>

### Q139. MHT CET 2020 (15 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the points $(1,1, \lambda)$ and $(-3,0,1)$ are equidistant from the plane
$3 x+4 y-12 z+13=0$, then integer value of $\lambda$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2$
- **(B)** $1$
- **(C)** $3$
- **(D)** $4$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\mathrm{A}(1,1, \lambda)$ and $\mathrm{B}(-3,0,1)$ are equidistant from $3 \mathrm{x}+4 \mathrm{y}-12 \mathrm{z}+13=0$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \therefore\left|\frac{3(1)+4(1)-12 \lambda+13}{\sqrt{9+16+144}}\right| \\
& \therefore\left|\frac{20-12 \lambda}{13}\right|=\left|\frac{-8}{13}\right| \\
\therefore & 20-12 \lambda=\pm 8 \\
\therefore & 20-12 \lambda=8 \text { or } 20-12 \lambda=-8 \Rightarrow \lambda=1 \text { or } \lambda=\frac{7}{3}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q140. MHT CET 2020 (14 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A $A(3,2,-1)$ and $B(1,4,3)$, then equation of the plane which bisects segment
AB perpendicularly

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+y+2 z+3=0$
- **(B)** $x-y+2 z-3=0$
- **(C)** $x+y-2 z-3=0$
- **(D)** $x-y-2 z+3=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $x-y-2 z+3=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since the plane bisects seg $A B$, the plane meets the line $A B$ at the mid point i.e. $\left(\frac{3+1}{2}, \frac{2+4}{2}, \frac{-1+3}{2}\right) \equiv(2,3,1)$
Now line $A B$ is $\perp$ er to the plane
Direction ratios of plane are $1-3,4-2,3+1$ i.e. $-2,2,4$ i.e. $-1,1,2$
Equation of plane passing through $(2,3,1)$ and having d.r.s. $(-1,1,2)$ are
$-(x-2)+(y-3)+2(z-1)=0 \Rightarrow-x+2+y-3+2 z-2=0$
$\therefore x-y-2 z+3=0$

</details>

### Q141. MHT CET 2020 (14 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of a plane passing through the intersection of two planes $x+2 y-3 z+2=0$ and $6 x+y+z+1=0$ and parallel to the line $x-1=y+2=7-z$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5 x-y+4 z+1=0$
- **(B)** $5 x+y+4 z+1=0$
- **(C)** $5 x-y+4 z=1$
- **(D)** $5 x+y+4 z=1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $5 x-y+4 z=1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of plane passing through the line of intersection of given planes is $(x+2 y-3 z+2)+\lambda(6 x+y+z+1)=0$
$(1+6 \lambda) x+(2+\lambda) y+(-3+\lambda) z+(2+\lambda)=0$
This is parallel to the line $\frac{x-1}{1}=\frac{y+2}{1}=\frac{z-7}{-1}$
$\therefore(1+6 \lambda)(1)+(2+\lambda)(1)+(-3+\lambda)(-1)=0 \Rightarrow \lambda=-1$
Hence required equation of plane is
$-5 x+y-4 z+1=0 \Rightarrow 5 x-y+4 z=1$

</details>

---

### Q142. MHT CET 2020 (13 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the plane passing through the points $(2,3,1),(4,-5,3)$ and parallel
to $\mathrm{y}$ -axis is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+z=3$
- **(B)** $x+z=1$
- **(C)** $x-z=1$
- **(D)** $z-x+2=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $x-z=1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of plane passing through the point $(2,3,1)$ is 
$a(x-2)+b(y-3)+c(z-1)=0$ $\ldots(1)$
Given point $(4,-5,3)$ lies on plane
$2 a-8 b+2 c=0$ $\ldots(2)$
Since plane is parallel to $\mathrm{Y}$-axis, having d.r. $(0,1,0)$
(a) $(0)+(\mathrm{b})(1)+(\mathrm{c})(0)=0 \Rightarrow \mathrm{b}=0$
Putting in equation (2) we get
$2 a+2 c=0 \Rightarrow a=-c$
Putting values of $\mathrm{a}, \mathrm{b}$ in equation (1)
$\begin{aligned}
&-c(x-2)+c(z-1)=0 \\
\therefore &(x-2)-(z-1)=0 \\
\therefore & x-z=1
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q143. MHT CET 2020 (13 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The foot of the perpendicular drawn from the origin to the plane $x+y+3 z-4=0$
is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left(\frac{2}{11}, \frac{2}{11}, \frac{9}{11}\right)$
- **(B)** $\left(\frac{4}{11}, \frac{4}{11}, \frac{12}{11}\right)$
- **(C)** $\left(\frac{1}{7}, \frac{1}{7}, \frac{6}{7}\right)$
- **(D)** $\left(\frac{1}{5}, \frac{1}{5}, \frac{3}{5}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\left(\frac{4}{11}, \frac{4}{11}, \frac{12}{11}\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

d.r. of $\perp$ er drawn from origin to the given plane are $1,1,3$.
Hence equation of $\perp e r$ line to the plane and passing through origin is
$\frac{x}{1}=\frac{y}{1}=\frac{z}{3}=K$ ... say
Now let foot of the $\perp$ er be $P(K, K, 3 K)$
Also this point P lies on given plane 
$\therefore \mathrm{K}+\mathrm{K}+9 \mathrm{~K}-4=0 \Rightarrow 11 \mathrm{~K}=4 \Rightarrow \mathrm{K}=\frac{4}{11}$ 
Hence $P \equiv\left(\frac{4}{11}, \frac{4}{11}, \frac{12}{11}\right)$

</details>

### Q144. MHT CET 2020 (13 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A plane $\mathrm{E}_{1}$ makes intercepts $1,-3,4$ on the co-ordinate axes. The equation of a plane
parallel to plane $\mathrm{E}_{1}$ and passing through $(2,6,-8)$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{x}{2}-\frac{y}{3}+\frac{z}{4}+3=0$
- **(B)** $\frac{x}{1}-\frac{y}{3}+\frac{z}{4}+12=0$
- **(C)** $\frac{x}{1}-\frac{y}{3}+\frac{z}{4}+2=0$
- **(D)** $\frac{x}{3}-\frac{y}{6}+\frac{z}{2}+\frac{13}{3}=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{x}{1}-\frac{y}{3}+\frac{z}{4}+2=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

A plane $\mathrm{E}_{1}$ makes intercepts $1,-3,4$ on the coordinate axes
Equation of plane is $\frac{x}{1}+\frac{y}{-3}+\frac{z}{4}=1 \Rightarrow 12 x-4 y+3 z=12$
d.r.s. are $12,-4,3$
Since required plane is parallel to given plane, normal vector $\bar{n}$ to required plane is
$\overline{\mathrm{n}}=12 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
The vector equation of the plane passing through $(2,6,-8)$ is
$\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{n}}=\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{n}}$, where $\overline{\mathrm{a}}=2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+6 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-8 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
$\bar{a} \cdot \bar{n}=(12)(2)-(4)(6)+(3)(-8)=24-24-24=-24$
$\therefore$ Required equation is $\overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot(12 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})=-24$
$\therefore$ Cartesion form of equation is $12 x-4 y+3 z+24=0$
$\therefore \frac{x}{1}-\frac{y}{3}+\frac{z}{4}+2=0$

</details>

---

### Q145. MHT CET 2020 (12 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The length of the perpendicular to the plane $\bar{r} \cdot(\hat{\imath}-2 \hat{\jmath}+3 \hat{k})=14$ from the origin is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{7}$ units
- **(B)** 7 units
- **(C)** 14 units
- **(D)** $\sqrt{14}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\sqrt{14}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

Length of $\perp$ er from the point $A(\bar{a})$ to the plane $\bar{r} \cdot \bar{n}=p$ is $\frac{|\bar{a} \cdot \bar{n}-p|}{|\bar{n}|}$
Here $\overline{\mathrm{a}}=0 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+0 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+0 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{n}}=\hat{\mathrm{i}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
$\therefore \overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{n}}=0 \quad$ and $|\overline{\mathrm{n}}|=\sqrt{1+4+9}=\sqrt{14}$
Hence required distance is $\frac{|0-14|}{\sqrt{14}}=\sqrt{14}$

</details>

### Q146. MHT CET 2020 (12 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of a plane containing the point $(1,-1,1)$ and parallel to the plane
$2 x+3 y-4 z=17$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\bar{r}_{\cdot}(2 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}-4 \hat{k})=-5$
- **(B)** $\bar{r} \cdot(2 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}-4 \hat{k})=-15$
- **(C)** $\bar{r} \cdot(4 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}-4 \hat{k})=-3$
- **(D)** $\bar{r} \cdot(3 \hat{\imath}+4 \hat{\jmath}-2 \hat{k})=-3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\bar{r}_{\cdot}(2 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}-4 \hat{k})=-5$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of plane passing through the point having position vector $\bar{a}$ and normal to $\overline{\mathrm{n}}$ is
$\therefore \overline{\mathrm{r}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{n}} \cdot(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}})=(\hat{\mathrm{i}}-\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}) \cdot(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}})=2-3-4=-5$

</details>

### Q147. MHT CET 2019 (02 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The equation of the plane passing through the point <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and perpendicular to the line joining the points <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> is ________

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>.</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>.</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>.</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>.</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>.</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since, direction ratio of normal 
Are <math><mfenced close="}" open="{" separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>  
= <math><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mn>2</mn></math>  
Then, equation of plane is <math><mn>5</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>  
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>  
<math><mo>=</mo><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo>.</mo></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>j</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>  
Or Equation of plane passing through 
<math><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>j</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi></math> and the position vector of normal <math><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>j</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi></math>  
is <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo>.</mo></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>j</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

</details>

### Q148. MHT CET 2019 (02 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The coordinates of the foot of perpendicular drawn from origin to the plane 
<math><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> are ________

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>30</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>30</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>30</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mi></mi><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mi></mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mi></mi><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mi></mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mi></mi><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mi></mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Use foot of perpendicula \((x, y, z)\) of a point \(\left(x_{1}, y_{1}, z_{1}\right)\) in a plane \(a x+b y+c z\) \(+d=0\) is given by \(\frac{x-x_{1}}{\mathrm{a}}=\frac{\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{y}_{1}}{\mathrm{~b}}=\frac{\mathrm{z}-\mathrm{z}_{1}}{\mathrm{c}}=\underline{-(\mathrm{ax}}\)
Given equation of plane is \(2 x-y+5 z-3\) \(=0\)
\(\therefore\) Foot of perpendicular drawn from origin to the given plane
\(
\begin{aligned}
&\Rightarrow \frac{x-0}{2}=\frac{y-0}{-1}=\frac{z-0}{5} \\
&=\frac{-(0+0+0-3)}{(2)^{2}+(-1)^{2}+(5)^{2}} \\
&\Rightarrow \frac{x}{2}=\frac{y}{-1}=\frac{z}{5}=\frac{3}{30}=\frac{1}{10} \\
&\therefore \frac{x}{2}=\frac{1}{10}, \frac{y}{-1}=\frac{1}{10}, \frac{z}{5}=\frac{1}{10} \\
&\Rightarrow x=\frac{1}{5}, y=-\frac{1}{10}, z=\frac{1}{2}
\end{aligned}
\)

</details>

### Q149. MHT CET 2019 (Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the foot of the perpendicular drawn from the point (0,0,0) to the plane is (4, -2, -5) then the equation of the plane is …..

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>13</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>45</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>37</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>45</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

We have, foot of the perpendicular drawn from the point (0,0, 0) to the plane is (4, -2, -5). 
<math><mo>∴</mo></math> Direction ratios of normal to the required plane is 
<math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4,0</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2,0</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>5)</mn><mi></mi><mi>i</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>e</mi><mo>(-</mo><mn>4,2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>  
<math><mo>∴</mo></math> Required equation of plane passing through (4, -2, 5) is 
<math><mi>a</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>  
<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>  
<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>16</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>  
<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>45</mn></math>

</details>

### Q150. MHT CET 2019 (Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Equations of planes parallel to the plane <math><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> which are at a distance of one unit from the point <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1,2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> are …..

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given equation of plane 
<math><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mi></mi></math> ….(i) 
Equation of plane parallel (ii) from point (1, 2, 3) is 1 unit 
<math><mo>∴</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><msqrt><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>  
<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>  
<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>9</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>  
<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>±</mo><mn>3</mn></math>  
<math><mo>∴</mo></math> <math><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>  
<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

</details>

### Q151. MHT CET 2018

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If planes <math><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>b</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi>c</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math><mi>b</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> pass through a straight line then <math><msup><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>c</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>c</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>c</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>c</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>c</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since the given planes pass through a straight line 
<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/332756.png" style="width: 184px; height: 100px;" />
<math> <mrow> <mo>⇒</mo><mrow><mo>|</mo> <mrow> <mtable> <mtr> <mtd> <mn>1</mn> </mtd> <mtd> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow> </mtd> <mtd> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mi>b</mi></mrow> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <mi>c</mi> </mtd> <mtd> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow> </mtd> <mtd> <mi>a</mi> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <mi>b</mi> </mtd> <mtd> <mi>a</mi> </mtd> <mtd> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow> </mtd> </mtr> </mtable></mrow> <mo>|</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow> </math>
<math> <mo>⇒</mo><mrow> <mn>1</mn><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><msup> <mi>a</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi></mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>b</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mi>a</mi><mi>c</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi></mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow> </math>
<math> <mo>⇒</mo><mrow> <mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><msup> <mi>a</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>−</mo><msup> <mi>c</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>−</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>c</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>c</mi><mo>−</mo><msup> <mi>b</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow> </math>
<math> <mo>⇒</mo><mrow> <mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>c</mi><mo>−</mo><msup> <mi>a</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>−</mo><msup> <mi>b</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>−</mo><msup> <mi>c</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow> </math>
<math> <mo>⇒</mo><mrow> <mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><msup> <mi>a</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo><msup> <mi>b</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo><msup> <mi>c</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q152. MHT CET 2018

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If planes <math> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>r</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>.</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mi>p</mi><mover accent="true"> <mi>i</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>−</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>j</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>k</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math> and <math> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>r</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>.</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>i</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>−</mo><mi>p</mi><mover accent="true"> <mi>j</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>−</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>k</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math> include angle <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> then the value of <math><mi>p</mi></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>3</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>3</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mn>3</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <mi>cos</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mfrac> <mi>π</mi> <mn>3</mn> </mfrac> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mi>p</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> <mrow> <msqrt> <mrow> <msup> <mi>p</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow> </msqrt> <msqrt> <mrow> <msup> <mi>p</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow> </msqrt> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow> </math>
<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn><mi>p</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mi>p</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow> </math>

- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>r</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>⋅</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>i</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>j</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>k</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mover accent="true"> <mi>r</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>⋅</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mn>3</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>i</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>j</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>k</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mover accent="true"> <mi>r</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>.</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>i</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>j</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>k</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>r</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>⋅</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>i</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>j</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>k</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation plane passing through
<math> <mrow> <mi>A</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>a</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow> </mrow> </math> and <math> <mo>⊥</mo> </math> to <math> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>n</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> </mrow> </math> is

Here <math><mover accent="false"><mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>a</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mover accent="false"><mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>n</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover><mo>=</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mover></mrow></mover></math>
<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mover accent="false"><mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>r</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover><mo>⋅</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mi> </mi><mo>.</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi> </mi></mrow></mover></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>m</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>⇒</mo><msup><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>±</mo><mi> </mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>6</mn><mi>m</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>A</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><mi>B</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
Equation of plane is <math> <mrow> <mfenced close="|" open="|" separators=","> <mrow> <mtable> <mtr> <mtd> <mtext>x</mtext> </mtd> <mtd> <mtext>y</mtext> </mtd> <mtd> <mtext>z</mtext> </mtd> <mtd> <mn>1</mn> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <mn>0</mn> </mtd> <mtd> <mtext>b</mtext> </mtd> <mtd> <mtext>c</mtext> </mtd> <mtd> <mn>1</mn> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <mtext>a</mtext> </mtd> <mtd> <mn>0</mn> </mtd> <mtd> <mtext>c</mtext> </mtd> <mtd> <mn>1</mn> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <mn>0</mn> </mtd> <mtd> <mn>0</mn> </mtd> <mtd> <mn>0</mn> </mtd> <mtd> <mn>1</mn> </mtd> </mtr> </mtable> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math>
<math><mo>&rArr;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mtable><mtr><mtd><mi>x</mi></mtd><mtd><mi>y</mi></mtd><mtd><mi>z</mi></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>0</mn></mtd><mtd><mi>b</mi></mtd><mtd><mi>c</mi></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi>a</mi></mtd><mtd><mn>0</mn></mtd><mtd><mi>c</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<math><mo>&rArr;</mo><mi>b</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

</details>

### Q157. MHT CET 2011

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A plane meets the axes in $A, B$ and $C$ such that centroid of the $\Delta A B C$ is $(1,2,3)$. The equation of the plane is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+y / 2+z / 3=1$
- **(B)** $x / 3+y / 6+z / 9=1$
- **(C)** $x+2 y+3 z=1$
- **(D)** None of the above

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $x / 3+y / 6+z / 9=1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the plane meets the coordinate axes at $A, B$ and $C .$
$$
\text { Let } \quad O A=a, O B=b \text { and } O C=c
$$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/KsCvCV_8vyXPKxr4KpjY5jFPF7ilbTRx9XW4aw5d9aI.original.fullsize.png">

Then, the centroid of $\Delta A B C$
is
$$
\left(\frac{a}{3}, \frac{b}{3}, \frac{c}{3}\right)=(1,2,3)
$$
(given)
$$
\Rightarrow \quad a=3, b=6, c=9
$$
Then, the equation of plane, meet the coordinate axes is
$$
\frac{x}{a}+\frac{y}{b}+\frac{z}{c}=1
$$
i.e.
$$
\frac{x}{3}+\frac{y}{6}+\frac{z}{9}=1
$$

</details>

### Q158. MHT CET 2010

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of the plane which passes through $(2,-3,1)$ and is normal to the line joining the points $(3,4,-1)$ and $(2,-1,5)$, is given by

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+5 y-6 z+19=0$
- **(B)** $x-5 y+6 z-19=0$
- **(C)** $x+5 y+6 z+19=0$
- **(D)** $x-5 y-6 z-19=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $x+5 y-6 z+19=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of plane is
$$
\begin{array}{lr}
(x-2)+5(y+3)-6(z-1)=0 \\
\Rightarrow \quad x+5 y-6 z+19=0
\end{array}
$$

</details>

### Q159. MHT CET 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Equation of the plane passing through $(-2,2,2)$ and $(2,-2,-2)$ and perpendicular to the plane $9 x-13 y-3 z=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5 x+3 y+2 z=0$
- **(B)** $5 x-3 y+2 z=0$
- **(C)** $5 x-3 y-2 z=0$
- **(D)** $5 x+3 y-2 z=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $5 x+3 y+2 z=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Any plane passing through $(-2,2,2)$ is $A(x+2)+B(y-2)+C(z-2)=0$
$\because$ It passes through $(2,-2,-2)$ $\Rightarrow \quad 4 A-4 B-4 C=0$
It is parallel to $9 x-13 y-3 z=0$ $\begin{array}{lll}\therefore & 9 A-13 B-3 C=0 & \ldots \text { (ii) }\end{array}$
Solving Eqs. (i) and (ii),
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \frac{A}{12-52}=\frac{B}{-36+12}=\frac{C}{-52+36} \\
\Rightarrow \quad \frac{A}{-40}=\frac{B}{-24}=\frac{C}{-16}
\end{aligned}
$$
$\therefore$ Required equation of plane is $-40(x+2)-24(y-2)-16(z-2)=0$
$\begin{array}{lr}\Rightarrow & -40 x-80-24 y+48-16 z+32=0 \\ \Rightarrow & 40 x+24 y+16 z=0 \\ \Rightarrow & 5 x+3 y+2 z=0\end{array}$

</details>

### Q160. MHT CET 2008

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of the plane containing the line $\frac{x+1}{-3}=\frac{y-3}{2}=\frac{z+2}{1}$ and the point $(0,7,-7)$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+y+z=1$
- **(B)** $x+y+z=2$
- **(C)** $x+y+z=0$
- **(D)** None of these

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $x+y+z=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of plane containing the line $\frac{x+1}{-3}=\frac{y-3}{2}=\frac{z+2}{1}$ is
$$
a(x+1)+b(y-3)+c(z+2)=0 ...(i)
$$
where $\quad-3 a+2 b+c=0$ ...(ii)
This passes through $(0,7,-7)$.
$\therefore$
$a+4 b-5 c=0$ ...(iii)
From Eqs.
(ii) and (iii),
or
$$
\begin{aligned}
\frac{a}{-14} &=\frac{b}{-14}=\frac{c}{-14} \\
\frac{a}{1} &=\frac{b}{1}=\frac{c}{1}
\end{aligned}
$$
Thus, the required plane is $x+y+z=0$

</details>
